Pivots the hook's accumulated events with **`buildDateGroups` REUSED VERBATIM** from the daily block; the `DayBlock` renderer mirrors `DateSection`'s REAL-row render (box outline via `BOX` borders, group dividers via `GROUP`, shift letters / grade chips) but STATIC (no edit machinery, no editable draft rows — "first-in-group" is derived POSITIONALLY from map indices, not mutable render flags). **Consistent-height day blocks (filler padding):** each day-block is padded up to `MIN_DAY_ROWS` (imported from `production-daily-block.tsx` — the SAME shared floor the focus daily block uses, so the two views can't drift) with faint **READ-ONLY** filler rows after the last real leaf and before the footer: `fillerCount = max(0, MIN_DAY_ROWS − dayBodySpan)`, `dateRowSpan = dayBodySpan + fillerCount`. A filler row emits NO Date/merged-identity cell (the Date + Shift/Grade/Source `rowSpan`s already cover it → the day box stays a rectangle, footer separate), just opaque `bg-background` frozen identity slots (Shift/Grade/Source/Recv, frozen-pane rule) + faint `bg-muted/30` numeric cells so the area reads as reserved/empty SLOTS on the uniform grid. Purely visual — NO inputs (editing endless pivots is a later phase). A day with ≥ `MIN_DAY_ROWS` real rows gets 0 fillers (unchanged). Adding fillers changes a block's HEIGHT only (virtuoso measures dynamically), NOT the number of day-block items — the `firstItemIndex` day-count anchor math is unaffected. **Per-day "Daily total" footer only — NO cross-scroll grand total (locked decision #7).** `firstItemIndex` (from the hook, DAY-BLOCK units) → `itemContent` computes `pos = index - firstItemIndex` for the month-boundary check (slim `MonthSeparator` bar when `prod_date` crosses a month, 1:1 with blocks, no separator items). `startReached`→`fetchOlder`, `endReached`→`fetchNewer`; `initialTopMostItemIndex` = last block for `latest` (opens at the newest day = bottom), `0` for a period (opens at its first day). **Toolbar = `CenaproPeriodPicker` (jump anchor) + `ViewModeSwitcher(mode=view)` + `ScopeToggle(scope=endless)` + "N days loaded".** loadError → persistent copyable banner (HARD RULE); empty → Inbox + notice. **NO row/day-block entrance animation** (virtualized — they recycle; only the pre-existing `transition-colors` hover). **Mobile:** the frozen 5-col matrix can't shrink to a phone; it **horizontal-scrolls at every breakpoint without crashing** (Scroller `overflow-x:auto`) — no phone-specific summary this phase (a focus-daily-block-style summary can be added later). **PHASE 3b — FULLY EDITABLE (unlock to edit).** The hard rule from the virtualization audit: **NO row-local edit state anywhere** (virtuoso recycles day-blocks). ALL pending state is PARENT-OWNED in one serializable `PivotPending` object (`modified` by eventId, `deleted` set, `staged` real-blank inserts by cellKey, `drafts` new-pulls by `${prodDate}#slot-i`, `pullEdits` identity re-tags by leafKey, `addedDays`, `extraRows`); the SINGLE active in-progress `editValue` lives in the parent too (one cell edits at a time → a half-typed value scrolled off-screen and back rehydrates). **Keyboard nav is DATA-DERIVED** (`buildNavModel` builds the ordered nav-cell list + per-row lanes from the pivoted `groups`+draft slots — NOT `querySelectorAll`, which stops at the virtualization boundary; a custom `NavResolver<string>` walks it; scroll-into-view-before-focus for off-screen targets via `virtuosoRef.scrollIntoView`). **Central, data-addressed commit** (no per-cell closure — scroll-safe). **Popovers (`InsertPopover`/`BaggingMetaPopover`) render at the PARENT level** anchored to captured cell coords (`PopoverAnchor`), so a recycled day-block never orphans them. Capabilities: weight edit / clear-to-delete (confirm) / type-into-blank→Insert popover; new pulls via filler rows (per-day `batch`/`batch_year` from the day's OWN prod_date, never a global period — sends explicit `batch_year`); **Edit-pull** row-level identity re-tag (right-click → dialog → re-buckets after save, the AG-Grid pattern; NOT inline on merged rowSpan cells); per-row context menu (Edit / Duplicate / Delete-or-Restore pull) via the shared `GridContextMenu`/`useGridContextMenu`; **Add day** (append-edge only, enabled when `!hasNewer`) + **Remove/Delete day** (day-footer menu; added-empty→✕, added-with-drafts→confirm, committed→confirm w/ event count → stages deletes); **undo/redo** over the whole pending buffer (⌘Z/⌘Y + toolbar, snapshot stacks); loss-proof localStorage mirror (`cenapro-pivot-drafts:v1:${plant}:${uid}`) + Resume/Discard prompt; collision cells (>1 event) stay locked with an "open in Ledger" deep-link; unified Save → `saveProductionEvents(dirtyRows, deletedIds)` then `win.refreshWindow()`. **Dirty-nav guard**: when `totalDirty>0`, the toolbar's `CenaproPeriodPicker`/`ViewModeSwitcher`/`ScopeToggle` are `disabled` so a switch can't silently discard edits. Locked mode is byte-for-byte the prior read-only renderer. |
| `production/draft-entry-zone.tsx` | Client — **`DraftRowCells`** + **`DraftDatalists`** (Phase 2A, IN-LIST model). The retired Bulk Add modal → a pinned draft section → now IN-LIST blank rows that are `TableVirtuoso` ITEMS in the SAME list as committed rows (the natural "Google Sheets" model). This module is **purely presentational**: `DraftRowCells` renders ONE draft row's `<td>` cells (row# + remove control + the 12 editable data cells via the shared `GridCell` + `<Input list=…>` typeahead) as a fragment — the endless sheet's virtuoso `TableRow` supplies the `<tr>`, so blanks align with committed rows exactly (same colgroup/widths). It holds **NO draft state** — `row` comes in as a prop and every edit calls up to `updateRow(draftIndex, field, value)`; the data lives in the endless sheet's PARENT-OWNED `draftRows` array (keyed by position), so virtuoso recycling an off-screen half-typed row never loses it (rehydrates from the array on remount) — the flat-list equivalent of `production-daily-block.tsx`'s parent-owned `drafts` Map keyed by a positional slot id, minus the monotonic-id indirection since this list never reorders. Draft cells are intentionally NOT frozen (editable + non-sticky; columns still align via the shared colgroup). `DraftDatalists` renders the 7 categorical `<datalist>`s once. Reuses `bulk-paste-utils.ts` types. **Phase 3a:** the 12 editable data cells (cols 1–12) are extracted into a shared **`EditableDataCells`** used by BOTH `DraftRowCells` (lead `+`/× cell) AND the new **`CommittedRowCells`** (lead row# + hover Delete/Restore toggle, amber "modified" rail / rose "deleted" strike) — so unlocked committed rows reuse the EXACT SAME cell editors as drafts (one consistent flat-spreadsheet editing surface). `DraftRowCells`'s coordinate prop is now the UNIFIED `rowIdx` (`committed.length + draftIndex`) instead of a bare draft index. |
| `production/bulk-paste-utils.ts` | The Bulk Add **paste + canonicalization layer** (pure, no React) — RC IN's `paste-utils.ts` analogue plus the categorical brain. Exports `BulkRow`/`BulkField`, `BULK_COLUMN_MAP` (**12** editable cols in the modal's left→right Excel order — `disposition_kind`+`partner_equipment_code` collapsed into a single `ccc_flec` cell), `createEmptyRow`, `isBlankRow`, `cleanBulkPasteValue` (dates→`parseExcelDate`, numerics strip `₱,"'%`/spaces; categoricals + `ccc_flec` kept raw so the cell shows what was pasted), per-column canonicalizers (`canonicalizeShift/Grade/Plant/Warehouse/Source/Side` — forgiving on case/aliases, e.g. `W7`/`whse7`/`7`→`WHSE 7`, `3x50`→`3X50`, `TNK1`→`TNK 1`, `left`→`LS`), and `mapBulkRowToDirty(row, defaultYear?)` which validates a row (weight > 0, categoricals map to real codes), runs both date cells through `normalizeTypedDate(value, defaultYear ?? currentYear)` as a **safety net** (so a typed shorthand that escaped commit-time normalization still lands canonical `yyyy-MM-dd`), and **resolves the single `ccc_flec` cell via the shared `parseCccFlec` from `types.ts`** (`FLEC`→`flec_bagging`; `C1`→`partner_crusher`+`C1`; `RK3`→`partner_kiln`+`RK3`) → `ProductionEventDirtyRow` or a list of human errors. The old separate `canonicalizeDisposition`/`canonicalizeEquipment` + the disposition⇄equipment CHECK dance are gone (the single column subsumes them — `parseCccFlec` returns null only for genuinely unrecognized input). `rowLabel(row, i)` for error messages. |
| `production/production-grid-v2-shared.tsx` | **The Blackwood Table ADAPTER for the production ledger (2026-08-19, `?grid=v2`).** The tenant half of the universal-table rewire: the 13 `ColumnSpec`s (same keys, order and pixel widths as the live `<colgroup>` — Σ = `PRODUCTION_MIN_WIDTH` = **1228px**, with `SIDE` newly `pin: 'end'`), the ONE `event` row family (`occupies()` — `#` is `addressable: false`: it RENDERS the ordinal and the caret steps over it), the three chrome families (`group-header` / `summary` / `spacer`, none addressable), `flattenProductionRows(rows, scope, groupField)` (focus → day heading + `Σ DAY TOTAL`; endless → month heading + a literal blank day `spacer`), `productionStoredText`, and the chrome renderers `renderDayGapCells` / `renderHeadingCell` / `renderDayTotalCells`. **READ-ONLY BY CONSTRUCTION: no spec declares a `parse`, so `columnAcceptsEdit` refuses every cell** — the editor, Delete, and paste all gate on it, so the whole write surface is removed by one omission. Display goes through the live ledger's own exported `toGridRow` / `formatKg` / `rowDirection` / `rowDirectionTint` / `rowDirectionFrozenTint` (the path `production-endless-sheet.tsx` already uses), so there is still ONE definition of every cell's characters. `pinnedTint()` paints the IN/OUT/DVO wash INSIDE a pinned cell's `format` at **`z-index: -10`** — a `<tr>` wash cannot reach a pinned `<td>` (it carries an opaque `bg-background`), and the negative layer lands above that background but below the module's interactive layer so the selection tint and active ring still read. Verified in-browser. **Since 2026-08-20 `specFor` also emits `sortable: false` on the two DATE columns and `filterable: false` on the six that carry a `filterColumn`** — the columns both v2 grids already hang a `DateSortSlot` / `ColumnFilterMenu` off through `renderHeaderSlot`. The platform's built-in sort caret and filter trigger arrived default-ON for `scope="focus"` and draw the same lucide glyphs, so the focus ledger's headers rendered two identical controls side by side; and they answer different questions — the consumer's write the URL and re-query the SERVER, the built-ins are local view state over the loaded rows that additionally hides every chrome row (the day headings and `Σ DAY TOTAL`) while active. One control per question; every other column keeps both. Emitted in the SHARED `specFor` so the focus ledger and the endless sheet can never disagree, and harmless in the endless scope, which offers neither by default. |
| `production/production-ledger-grid-v2.tsx` | Client — **the FOCUS-scope v2 ledger** (`?scope=focus&view=ledger&grid=v2`). Built BESIDE `production-ledger-grid.tsx`, which is not edited. Read-only: selection + rectangular ranges, the full keyboard (arrows / Tab / Ctrl+Arrow / Home-End / Ctrl+Home-End / PageUp-Down), copy, column resize (`TableSettings` in local state), frozen panes at both ends, day headings + `Σ DAY TOTAL`, a sticky `Σ PERIOD` footer, the two DATE sorts and the six column filters — the last two hung off `renderHeaderSlot` → `HeaderCell.filterSlot`. Reuses `CenaproPeriodPicker` / `ViewModeSwitcher` / `ScopeToggle` / `ColumnFilterMenu` / `useLedgerFilters` unmodified. Filters client-side via `matchesLedgerFilters` (rows REMOVED, not `hidden`, so `#` runs 1…N without gaps — the one visible difference from the live grid). **Absent, not inert:** Save · Discard · row context menu · `Add rows in the sheet` · mobile cards · the Daily pivot. |
| `production/production-endless-sheet-v2.tsx` | Client — **the ENDLESS-scope v2 sheet** (the default view, at `?grid=v2`). Built BESIDE `production-endless-sheet.tsx`. Pages through **`useLedgerWindow` unmodified** (same read-only `fetchLedgerPage`, same server-applied filters — no new query). **It does NOT reuse that hook's `firstItemIndex`:** the hook decrements by the RECORDS it prepended, and this sheet's flat array grows by MORE (a month heading, a day spacer), so the base is derived here as a pure `useMemo` over `shiftFirstItemIndex` measuring **how many ITEMS sit above a FIXED anchor row** — which also makes an `fetchNewer` APPEND a no-op for the base. Measured: 4 records prepended add **6** items, base 100 000 → **99 994** (counting records would have left it 2 rows out). `initialTopMostItemIndex` stays a RAW array position. Sticky `Σ LOADED` footer (never "total" — this is a window). **No sort control:** the endless order IS the server's keyset order, and a client re-sort would make `hasOlder`/`hasNewer` describe a different sheet. No lock/unlock, no draft pool, no localStorage mirror, no resume prompt, no Save. |
| `inventory/page.tsx` | Server — reads `?whse=&date=` URL params (defaults WHSE 7 / 2026-03-10), fetches `fetchFlecInventory()` + `fetchOpeningBalances()` + `fetchOpeningBalanceHistory()` in parallel → `<FlecInventoryClient />`. The START date is the "as of" for both the openings seed and the ledger seed; history is warehouse-scoped (date-independent). |
| `inventory/actions.ts` | `'use server'` — `fetchFlecInventory(warehouse, startDate)` (`cenapro_flec_balance` + `cenapro_flec_ledger` in parallel); `fetchOpeningBalances(warehouse, asOf)` (`cenapro_opening_balances` — the effective opening per grade×side, seeds the editable STARTING block); `fetchOpeningBalanceHistory(warehouse)` (`cenapro_opening_balance_history` — the full append-only trail, newest-first); **`saveOpeningBalances(changes[])`** loops `cenapro_set_opening_balance` once per CHANGED cell (append-only INSERT, effective date = page START date), stops + reports the first failing cell, `revalidatePath('/cenapro/inventory')` on success. See "Opening-balance write path". |
| `inventory/flec-inventory-client.tsx` | Client — warehouse `<Select>` + native start-date input (both push to URL params via `router.replace` in a transition). **The selection persists to `localStorage` (`cenapro_flec_prefs` = `{ whse, date }`):** `applyParams` writes it on every change, and a once-on-mount effect restores it when the URL carries NO `whse`/`date` param (a clean visit — nav menu / landing card), so the operator's last warehouse + start date permeate across refresh, navigation, and sessions. An explicit URL param ALWAYS wins (shared links keep working); localStorage is only the fallback, then the hardcoded default. SSR-guarded; the restore runs once (no replace loop); a stale `whse` not in `FLEC_WAREHOUSES` or a non-ISO date is ignored. **Editable STARTING block** (`StartingBlock`): a `table-fixed` grid, rows = `GRADE_CODES`, columns = `RS`/`LS` each split into **Starting** (editable digit-only `<input>`, seeded from `cenapro_opening_balances`) + **Now** (read-only closing from `cenapro_flec_balance`, "—" when absent per the empty-period caveat). Local edit buffer with amber dirty-border per cell, "N unsaved" badge, Discard + "Save starting balances" (writes only changed cells → `saveOpeningBalances` → success toast / `errorToast`; `router.refresh()` re-seeds). Per-cell `CellHistoryPopover` (History icon) + a collapsible whole-warehouse `OpeningHistoryPanel` grouped by grade×side (newest-first, "current" badge on the top entry) — the backtracking surface. Then the existing balance cards + show-your-math movement ledger. Local `StartingBlock`/`StartingCell`/`CellHistoryPopover`/`OpeningHistoryPanel`/`BalanceCard`/`LedgerDirection`. |

## Data
All reached through **read-only accessors in the already-served `public` schema** — the standard Supabase client hits them directly (no `.schema('cenapro')`, no cast). See "Data path" below for why. These accessors are thin wrappers over the underlying `cenapro` schema objects.

- **`public.cenapro_production_events`** (VIEW, ~755 rows across 9 (batch_year, batch) periods; recv_date 2025-12-01 → 2026-06-01). 16 columns: `id, recv_date, prod_date, batch, batch_year, shift_code, grade_code, plant_code, warehouse_code (nullable — "unplaced" rows), source_location_code, weight_kg, disposition_kind, partner_equipment_code, flec_count, whse_side, unique_tag`. **The ledger loads ONE period at a time** (filtered `.eq('batch_year').eq('batch')`), not all rows — `batch` = a month name (JANUARY…DECEMBER), `batch_year` = an int. Heaviest single period ≈ 162 rows; newest (the default) can be small (e.g. JUNE 2026 = 2 rows). Loading everything (the old path, ~755 rows × ~14 interactive cells with no virtualization) was the perf bottleneck.
  - PostgREST types all VIEW columns as nullable (`T | null`) — the client consumers guard accordingly (e.g. the sort comparator coalesces `recv_date`/`id` to `''`).
  - `disposition_kind` ∈ `flec_bagging | partner_crusher | partner_kiln`. The **production grid + bulk modal** render the single Excel `CCC/FLEC` value (`FLEC` / `C1` / `RK3`) via `formatCccFlec`; the **Flec Inventory ledger** still renders the friendly label (Bag / Crusher C1 / Kiln RK3) via `formatDisposition`.
  - Verified distinct values: shift `M`; grades `2X6/3.5/3X50`; warehouses `WHSE 1/5/7`; equipment `C1/C2/RK1/RK2/RK3/RK4`. (Filter options are derived from data at runtime, not hardcoded.)
- **`public.cenapro_flec_balance(p_warehouse_code text, p_start_date date)`** → `(warehouse_code, grade_code, side, current_flec, opening_seed, as_of)`. Closing count per (grade, side). NOTE: a (grade,side) with an opening but no events ≥ start date does NOT appear (empty-period caveat, schema §6.2).
- **`public.cenapro_flec_ledger(p_warehouse_code text, p_start_date date)`** → `(…, recv_date, grade_code, side, disposition_kind, partner_equipment_code, kg_moved, flec_in, flec_out, opening_seed, flec_in_to_date, flec_out_to_date, running_balance)`. The show-your-math movement detail (function Returns are non-null).
- **`public.cenapro_opening_balances(p_warehouse_code text, p_as_of_date date)`** → `(warehouse_code, grade_code, side, period_start_date, opening_flec_count, created_at)`. The **current effective** opening per (grade, side) as of the date = greatest `period_start_date ≤ as_of`, tie-broken by greatest `created_at`. Seeds the editable STARTING block. Unlike `cenapro_flec_balance`, it returns a (grade, side) even with no events forward.
- **`public.cenapro_opening_balance_history(p_warehouse_code text)`** → `(id, warehouse_code, grade_code, side, period_start_date, opening_flec_count, created_at)`. **ALL** append-only opening entries, newest-first per (grade, side) — the backtracking data.
- **`public.cenapro_set_opening_balance(p_warehouse_code, p_grade_code, p_side, p_effective_date date, p_count int)`** → INSERTS a new append-only entry (never overwrites), RETURNS the row. SECURITY INVOKER, granted to `authenticated` + `service_role` (NOT `anon`) — written only by `saveOpeningBalances` (server action, runs as the logged-in user). See "Opening-balance write path".
- Flec warehouses = `WHSE 1/2/5/7` (flec-count). **WHSE 3 is kg/DVO — DEFERRED, never surfaced.**
- Opening balances seeded for **WHSE 7** (3X50 RS=53, 2X6 LS=26 @ 2026-03-10) → that is the inventory page's default. With the seeded ledger, WHSE 7 closing as of 2026-03-10 is 3X50/RS = 56, 2X6/LS = 0; the other 6 grade×side cells have no opening AND no movement, so they appear in neither `cenapro_opening_balances` nor `cenapro_flec_balance` (the STARTING grid renders them blank → "—", ready to type into).

## Key Behaviors
- **Production = EDITABLE ledger; Flec Inventory = read-only.** The app is now the maintaining file for production rows.
- **Production screen = TWO orthogonal URL axes (Phase 1 reframe, 2026-07-21; a third, EDIT lock/unlock, is Phase 3).** **VIEW** (`?view=ledger\|daily-w6\|daily-w7`) × **SCOPE** (`?scope=endless\|focus`) — every combination is valid and reachable (six no-dead-end combos); the View switcher + Scope toggle + period dropdowns render in ALL of them. `endless` is the SCOPE default (omits the param); a legacy `?focus=1` maps to `scope=focus`. **ENDLESS + LEDGER = the Endless Sheet (Phase 1, read-only):** one virtualized, oldest-first (`recv_date ASC, id ASC`) view of the ENTIRE history, keyset-paginated bidirectionally (`fetchLedgerPage` + `useLedgerWindow` + `react-virtuoso`'s `TableVirtuoso`). The `?year=&batch=` dropdowns are a **JUMP-TO anchor** — the FIRST DB query is already anchored (period → its first row loading forward; no params → `latest`, opening at the BOTTOM = newest rows), never load-from-the-beginning-then-teleport; scrolling up prepends older rows with the viewport pinned (`firstItemIndex` decremented in the same batch), scrolling down streams newer months; **column FILTERS shipped 2026-08-03 and are pushed into the keyset QUERY** (sort controls are still focus-only); a dropdown jump `router.replace`s the URL → server re-prefetch → anchor-keyed remount. **FOCUS (any view) = the existing month-scoped EDITABLE grid, unchanged** (sort toggles + inline editing + right-click Insert intact) — `scope=focus` is a PURE month-clamp with no exclusive features; the SAME dropdowns are the clamp selector there. **ENDLESS + DAILY-W6/W7 = a Phase-1 fallback** (option (b): transparently render the month-scoped daily block + a small "Endless {W6/W7} coming" hint); Phase 2 replaces it with the true day-windowed endless pivot. The axis controls are the shared `ViewModeSwitcher` + `ScopeToggle` (`ledger-controls.tsx`), the pure helpers `parseScope`/`parseViewMode`/`plantViewOf` (`ledger-url.ts`); the old `LedgerModeToggle` + `?focus` silo are retired. Phase 2A DELIVERED the draft entry zone (retiring the Bulk Add modal); Phase 3 adds inline edit of committed rows on the sheet — see `.agents/prompts/cenapro-ledger-endless-sheet-spec.md`.
- **Production ledger:** scoped to **one (year, batch) period** via the toolbar `CenaproPeriodPicker` (URL params `?year=&batch=`); only that period's rows load (server-side `.eq` filter). **Default = the NEWEST period** (`fetchCenaproPeriods` returns periods newest-first; the page picks `periods[0]` when the URL has no param) so the page opens fast on recent data — NOT all ~755 rows. An explicit URL period wins; a stale one falls through to newest. There is intentionally **no "All periods" option** (a future "All" behind virtualization is out of scope). WITHIN the loaded period, filters + date-sort happen client-side (the per-period set is small). **Header filters = Shift / Grade / Plant / Whse / Source / CCC/FLEC** (2026-08-03: **MULTI-select**, canonical option domain from `types.ts` — not distinct-in-data — driven by the URL filter axis; they HIDE rows, not sort; a dirty row is EXEMPT and never hidden). See "Column filtering — the FILTER axis" below. **Both date headers sort:** clicking the Recv or Prod header (`DateSortHeader`) makes that date the in-memory sort key; clicking the active one flips asc/desc; default = newest-first by recv_date (the chevron marks the active key, an idle `ChevronsUpDown` the other). Sticky glass header (`bg-muted/90 backdrop-blur-sm`). One VIEW row = one grid row keyed by stable `id`. **12 data columns**, Excel order: `# · recv · prod · batch · shift · grade · plant · whse · source · weight · CCC/FLEC · flec · side`. Inline edit (click-to-activate, F2/type-over, keyboard nav, paste-from-Excel), `SelectCell` dropdowns for the categorical columns (shift/grade/plant/whse/source/side), the single `CCC/FLEC` typeahead `<Input list>` cell, `DatePickerCell` for recv/prod, numeric `<Input>` for weight/flec. **Rows are a `React.memo`'d `ProductionRow`** fed primitive active/selection slices + stable callbacks, so editing one cell re-renders only the touched row(s) — not the whole grid (a cheap complementary win on top of the period scoping). Dirty tracking (new=blue / modified=amber left border; deleted=strikethrough) + single Save/Discard; **NO trailing always-empty new row** — adds happen via Bulk Add (primary) or right-click Insert Above/Below (paste may still auto-extend). Empty state shows when the period has zero data rows. Switching period is blocked (picker disabled) while there are unsaved edits. No row animations (`transition-all duration-150` hover only).
  - **Frozen identity columns (Excel freeze panes — canonical pattern, see CLAUDE.md "Frozen Panes" + `globals.css`):** the 4 leftmost columns `# · Recv · Prod · Batch` are pinned with cumulative px offsets from the colgroup widths (36/96/96/120 → left: 0 / 36 / 132 / 228), so they stay visible when scrolling right. The table uses `borderCollapse: 'separate'` (required for sticky). Uses the shared `.frozen-col` (body) / `.frozen-corner` (header identity cells) / `.frozen-edge` (anti-seam right divider on the last frozen col, Batch) utilities. **All frozen surfaces are fully OPAQUE** — header/corner `bg-muted` solid, body `bg-background` solid + the row's opaque IN/OUT tint via `rowDirectionFrozenTint` + `group-hover:bg-muted/50` — so scrolling content can't bleed THROUGH the pinned cells (the previous translucent `bg-muted/90 backdrop-blur` thead was the bleed-through bug). Selection/edit/ring still render inside the frozen cells (GridCell/DatePickerCell paint their own selection feedback on top). **Z-scale (canonical):** frozen header corners (sticky top + left) `z-30` > scrolling header row `z-20` > frozen body col `z-10` > normal scrolling cells (auto). Mirrors the ICTC daily-ledger freeze-pane approach.
  - **Bold + direction tint + badges (display-only):** the ledger text is **bold** throughout. Each row gets a **bold row tint** keyed on the WAREHOUSE first, then the CCC/FLEC disposition — **priority: UNPLACED > WHSE 3 (DVO) > disposition** (via `rowDirection(row)`, which now takes the whole row so it can read `warehouse_code`): **UNPLACED** (`warehouse_code` blank) → **NO tint** (an unplaced bagging row is NOT highlighted until it's placed — this is the intentional behavior change; setting the Whse dropdown places it and re-tints it green); **WHSE 3** = the DVO warehouse → **BLUE** (`bg-blue-50 dark:bg-blue-950/40`) regardless of disposition (a DVO row is never counted as "taken out of a real warehouse"); a **real placed warehouse** (`WHSE 1/2/5/7`) → disposition decides: bagged-IN/`flec_bagging` → **GREEN** (`bg-emerald-50 dark:bg-emerald-950/40`), any withdrawal/`partner_crusher`+`partner_kiln` → **RED** (`bg-rose-50 dark:bg-rose-950/40`); unrecognized CCC/FLEC at a real warehouse → none. Frozen cells use the opaque variant (`-950/60` in dark, blue included). Tint is layered UNDER the dirty borders + selection highlight so those still read clearly. **CCC/FLEC** and **PLANT** render as compact colored badges in display mode, punchier now (`/20`–`/25` fill, `/40` border, bold `-700/-300` text): `FLEC` emerald / `Cn` amber / `RKn` rose; `W6` blue / `W7` teal / `W6/W7` indigo / `DVO` slate — each with a `dark:` variant; the edit `<Input>` stays plain so paste/typing is unaffected.
  - **CCC/FLEC single column:** disposition + equipment render as ONE Excel-parity cell (`FLEC`/`Cn`/`RKn`) between weight and flec. Edited as raw text, parsed to `disposition_kind` + `partner_equipment_code` on Save via the shared `parseCccFlec`; an unrecognized value is rejected with a persistent `errorToast`. A single typed cell can't make an inconsistent pair, so the prior partner-equipment dependency dance + the separate Disposition/Equipment columns are gone. See "CCC/FLEC single column" above.
  - **Unplaced-warehouse fix:** `warehouse_code` is nullable; a null row reads "unplaced" (subtle amber tint). Setting the Whse dropdown is how an operator places the unplaced bagging rows.
- **View modes (`?view=`):** the VIEW axis — the production screen shows a period's rows through multiple lenses, switched via the shared toolbar `ViewModeSwitcher` (now in `ledger-controls.tsx`, available in BOTH scopes) driving `?view=ledger|daily-w6|daily-w7` (preserves the other axis params — `scope`/`year`/`batch`; `useTransition` + `router.replace`; `ledger` is the default and omits the param; absent/invalid → `ledger`; **legacy `?view=daily` → `daily-w6`**). In focus scope the data does NOT re-fetch on a view change — every mode consumes the same `initialRows`. The typed `ViewMode` union + `parseViewMode()` + `plantViewOf()` now live in the pure `ledger-url.ts` (shared by the server page + the client controls), keeping it extensible (future: per-product, stats).
  - **Ledger mode (default):** the editable Industrial-Spreadsheet grid described above (unchanged — date auto-format, Enter-anchor nav, warehouse-first highlight all intact).
  - **Daily W6 / Daily W7 modes (EDITABLE — Phase 1 + 2):** the visually-styled boss "PROD 2026" pivot (`ProductionDailyBlock`, `plantView='W6'|'W7'`) — two variants showing **production OUTPUT** for each plant, **always grouped by `prod_date`** (PROD-ONLY; the recv/prod toggle was removed). The period picker + view switcher STAY visible; the editable-only toolbar controls HIDE; the `loadError` banner still shows. **Source filter:** rows are filtered by `source_location_code` before pivoting — **W6 keeps `{TNK 1, TNK 2, TNK 3, TNK 4, W6}`, W7 keeps `{W7}`; FLEC and DVO EXCLUDED from both** (inventory withdrawals, not output). **Bagging kept** (bagged tank/W6/W7 material is still output). **Hierarchy:** PROD_DATE → SHIFT (M/E/N) → GRADE (3X50/2X6/3.5/4X8) → SOURCE → **RECV_DATE** — a **LEAF = one (source, recv_date) pull**. The **SOURCE cell `rowSpan`s its recv-date rows**, so each source shows ONCE with its recv dates stacked **chronologically** in a dedicated **Recv Date** column (= how many pulls that tank had that prod date). **Columns:** `Date · Shift · Grade · Source · Recv Date · C1–C4 · RK1–RK4 · Bagging · Sub-Total · Total`. **Only rollup = the per-DATE "Daily total" footer** (the grade subtotal was removed). **Padded + outlined day blocks:** each day pads with blank FILLER rows up to `MIN_DAY_ROWS` (=6, tunable) — filler numeric cells get a faint `bg-muted/30` (reserved empty slot, not missing data). **Complete 2-tier header:** the **5 identity headers `rowSpan={2}`** (fill full header height, no empty white top-left, `.frozen-corner`, vertically centered) + the tinted group banner over the code cells — one solid bordered band edge-to-edge. **Three border tokens (one each, used everywhere):** **GRID** = uniform 1px `border-b border-r border-border` (solid, crisp) on every cell, both axes (merged cells skip internal horizontal lines); **GROUP** = 2px `border-l-2 border-l-border` divider between the column groups (Crushers|Kilns|Bagging|Totals), carried through header+body+filler+footer; **BOX** = 2px day outline (top row / footer / Date col / Total col) — SOFT NEUTRAL `border-border` (same color as gridlines, stands out by 2px weight not by color; NOT black). The `<table>` carries a `border border-border` perimeter (fully closed). Clean card bg, **no tint**. **Layout — REAL merged cells (`rowSpan`):** DATE spans the day's REAL + FILLER rows (**NOT** the footer); SHIFT spans its shift; GRADE its grade's leaf rows (`= leafCount`); SOURCE its recv-date rows; `dateRowSpan = dayBody + filler`, day has `dayBody + filler + 1` `<tr>`s (footer separate) — **rectangular**. Merged content **align-top**; merged cells sticky-left + opaque. **BOLD identity columns** (Date/Shift/Grade/Source/Recv Date); numeric cells normal weight (daily total bold). **No data-bars** (removed). **FIXED equipment columns** — all 8 always render; blank when 0. **Frozen panes** on **5 identity cols** (Date/Shift/Grade/Source/**Recv Date**) + the 2-tier header (`.frozen-edge` on Recv Date). **Full-width day footer:** a TRUE edge-to-edge totals row — a "Daily total" label `colSpan={5}` over ALL FIVE identity cols (sticky at `left:0`), then the numeric totals; one continuous solid `bg-muted` band, bold numbers, group dividers carried. **Excel-matched widths** (Date 76 / Shift 48 / Grade 100 / Source 140 / Recv 80 / equip 56 / Bag 66 / Sub 70 / Total 84), `width:max-content`, `px-1.5 py-0.5` / `text-[11px]`. **Visual treatment:** **shift = a BOLD colored letter** (M amber / E violet / N indigo — not a pill, can't overflow) + per-grade **chips**; group banner tints (Crushers amber / Kilns rose / Bagging emerald / Totals) on the group cells only; toolbar carries a `{plantView}` pill. Section-level fade only, NO per-row animation. Per-variant empty state. **EDITABLE (Phase 1):** equipment/bagging NUMBER cells on REAL leaf rows are click-to-edit (Enter/Tab/blur commit, Esc cancel) and round-trip through the EXISTING `saveProductionEvents(dirtyRows, deletedIds)` (no new backend): **edit** → UPDATE (dirty row WITH the cell's event `id`, amber ring); **clear** → DELETE (id → `deletedIds`, `AlertDialog` confirm); **type into a blank cell** → a **column-aware `Popover`** → stage an INSERT (no id, blue tint). The popover shows read-only **context chips** (auto Plant, prod date, shift, grade, target column) and branches: **crushers/kilns** → **Source only** (selector over `SOURCE_SETS[plantView]`, pre-filled to the row's source; no warehouse/side/flec); **Bagging** → **Source + Warehouse + Side + Flec count**. The pivot records each cell's event id(s); the COLUMN is the disposition (`columnDisposition()`: Cn→`partner_crusher`, RKn→`partner_kiln`, Bagging→`flec_bagging`+null). INSERT dims = the leaf + the popover's Source + `selectedPeriod.batch`, with **`plant_code` AUTO-STAMPED = `plantView`** (W6/W7 — no blank Plant in the Ledger); batch_year trigger-derived, not sent. A cell mapping to >1 event is **collision-LOCKED** (🔒, edit in Ledger; 1 such cell exists). A toolbar Save/Discard bar (+"N unsaved") appears on `hasChanges`; Save → action → `onSaveSuccess()` remount; errors via `errorToast`. **EDITABLE (Phase 2):** the blank FILLER rows are **"new pull" input slots** — identity cells are **Excel-style input + `<datalist>` typeahead** (not `<Select>`): Shift/Grade/Source filter native suggestions, normalized + validated on commit (`normalizeIdentity`); **Recv Date** is typed (normalized) and starts EMPTY (no prefill). Then editable equipment/Bagging weights. A weight commit **validates identity is complete** (shift+grade+source+recv, else a gentle toast) before staging; a complete filler stages **one INSERT per filled column** (C1+C2 = 2 events sharing the pull dims) with `plant_code` auto = `plantView`, dims from the row, batch from the period (crushers/kilns direct; Bagging via a small warehouse/side/flec popover). **Add-row drawer:** the `MIN_DAY_ROWS` rectangle stays; auto-grow-on-fill was removed — a hover-revealed "+ Add row" pill (at the footer label's top edge, `group-hover/day`, opacity-only) appends a row to that day via `extraRows`. A toolbar **"Add day"** popover (validated date in-period) spawns a NEW empty fillable day block (`addedDays`, sorted; empty added days save nothing). Drafts render FLAT until save+refresh; the Date `rowSpan` covers real+draft+slot rows so the grid stays rectangular; draft inserts feed the SAME `saveProductionEvents` payload + `hasChanges`/Discard. **Excel-fidelity cells:** **uniform row height** (`ROW_H` `h-7` on every `<tr>` incl. the footer, `align-middle`); **no height expansion on edit** (the inline `EditInput` matches the static cell metrics exactly — `EDIT_INPUT`: `text-[11px]`/`px-1.5`/`leading-none`/`bg-transparent`/no border/ring/`h-full`/`appearance-none`, caret-only change); **placeholder vanishes on focus**, restores on empty blur. **Keyboard nav (spreadsheet-style):** every editable cell is an always-rendered `EditInput` with `data-navid="${rowKey}|${colKey}"`; a grid-level `onKeyDown` reads the ordered cell list from the DOM and moves the active cell — **Tab/Shift+Tab** right/left (wrap at row ends), **Enter** down with the **Enter-anchor** (returns to the Tab-run's start column, mirroring the bulk-add modal; cleared by arrows/click), **arrows** within row/column, **Esc** cancels + stays. Skips read-only/locked cells (no `data-navid`); click + keyboard share one `activeNavId`. The active cell gets a **highlight matching the ledger** — `ACTIVE_RING` = `ring-2 ring-primary ring-inset` (+`z-20` so it's above the z-10 frozen cols, never clipped); an inset ring takes no layout space (no height change) and is static (no transition), sitting over any dirty `bg-*` tint so both read. Bagging Tab/Enter opens the warehouse/side/flec popover (focus briefly moves there; resume by click/Tab). Props `selectedPeriod`+`onSaveSuccess` threaded from `production-ledger-grid.tsx`. No cenapro audit-log trigger + no per-row `provenance` tag (gaps, deferred).
  - **Known gaps (boss-sheet parity, do NOT fabricate):** there is **no "RECV CCC" column** — the data model has no CCC bucket (only crushers C1–C4 / kilns RK1–RK4 / FLEC bagging); and there is **no tank-composition string** (the boss's "10+10+10+3 FLEC" source breakdown isn't stored, so Source shows the distinct `source_location_code` values joined instead). Future view-mode work should treat these as missing source data, not as a rendering TODO.
- **Draft entry zone (fast multi-row entry — Phase 2A, replaced the Bulk Add modal):** the editable ledger toolbar's **"Add rows in the sheet →"** affordance switches to the endless Ledger (`?scope=endless`, `?add=1`), which UNLOCKS a pool of **IN-LIST blank draft rows** appended below the last committed row in the SAME virtuoso list (the "Google Sheets" model — scroll DOWN into an effectively-infinite supply, topped up to ~25 trailing blanks). Blanks render through the same `itemContent` as committed rows (`DraftRowCells`), so columns align exactly. **Paste from Excel/Google Sheets fills the grid** (`.split(/\r\n|\n|\r/)` → rows, `.split('\t')` → columns, filling from the active cell, **auto-extending** rows when the paste is taller). "Add row" appends; hovering a row reveals an X to remove it. Column order (left→right, **12 data cols**): `recv_date · prod_date · batch · shift · grade · plant · warehouse · source · weight_kg · CCC/FLEC · flec_count · whse_side`.
  - **Loss-proof (the whole reason this exists — BUG-012):** draft rows live in the endless sheet's state and are **mirrored to `localStorage`** (debounced, versioned, keyed to user id), restored on mount, cleared ONLY on a confirmed Save/Discard. NOTHING destroys drafts — Escape only reverts the current cell (no Dialog to close), click-out/lock keep them, a reload restores them. This is the opposite of the retired modal, which had no close-guard and reset its rows to 8 blanks on every reopen.
  - **Typed-date auto-transcription (Excel parity):** unchanged — both date cells normalize a typed shorthand → `yyyy-MM-dd` on commit via `normalizeTypedDate(raw, yr)` (`yr` = `selectedPeriod?.batch_year`); `mapBulkRowToDirty` re-applies it at save as a safety net.
  - **Categoricals stay paste-friendly:** every categorical cell is a typeable `<Input list=…>` backed by a `<datalist>` — NOT a strict dropdown. Canonicalization happens **on Save** in `bulk-paste-utils.ts` (forgiving aliases — `W7`/`7`→`WHSE 7`, `3x50`→`3X50`, `left`→`LS`); the single `CCC/FLEC` cell resolves to both DB fields via the shared `parseCccFlec`.
  - **Save (INSERT-only):** the parent (endless sheet) drops blank rows, validates the rest via `mapBulkRowToDirty` (weight > 0; categoricals resolve to real codes; `CCC/FLEC` resolves via `parseCccFlec`) — invalid rows block the save with **one persistent, copyable `errorToast`** naming them + a red rail on each offending row (HARD RULE) — then INSERTs via the **existing** `saveProductionEvents(dirtyRows, [])`. On success: clear drafts + mirror, reseed a fresh sheet, `refreshNewest()` so the rows land at the bottom, scroll to LAST. On throw: drafts stay (state + mirror) for retry.
- **Flec Inventory:** warehouse + start-date drive state via **URL search params** (`?whse=&date=`); changing either `router.replace`s in a `useTransition` → server re-fetch. **The selection also persists to `localStorage` (`cenapro_flec_prefs`) and permeates a fresh visit:** `applyParams` writes `{ whse, date }` on every change, and a once-on-mount effect restores it whenever the URL has NO `whse`/`date` param (clean `/cenapro/inventory` from the nav menu or landing card) — so the operator's chosen warehouse + start date survive refresh, navigation, AND sessions (a critical detail that previously fell back to the WHSE 7 / 2026-03-10 default). **An explicit URL param always wins** (shared links unaffected); localStorage is only the fallback, then the hardcoded default. SSR-guarded; restore runs once (no replace loop). Start-date semantics made explicit ("Balances as of `<date>` forward"). Layout top→bottom mirrors the Excel PC WHSE sheet: **editable STARTING block** → current-balance cards → collapsible opening-balance history → show-your-math movement ledger ("opening + ins − outs = balance").
  - **Editable STARTING block:** rows = the 4 flec grades, columns = `RS`/`LS`, each cell = an editable **Starting** input (seeded from `cenapro_opening_balances(warehouse, startDate)`) + a read-only **Now** closing (from `cenapro_flec_balance`). Edits are local (amber dirty-border, "N unsaved" badge) until **Save starting balances** writes only the changed cells. The **START date is the effective date** of every saved opening — the modular part: change the date and/or values to set a fresh starting point for a period, and old values are kept (append-only). After save → success toast + `router.refresh()` re-seeds the grid and re-derives the ledger.
  - **Backtracking:** every saved opening is preserved. Surfaced two ways — a per-cell `CellHistoryPopover` (History icon next to each input) and a collapsible whole-warehouse `OpeningHistoryPanel` (grouped by grade×side, newest-first, top entry tagged "current"), both reading `cenapro_opening_balance_history(warehouse)`.
- **Error handling (HARD RULE):** every inline error banner is persistent + has a **Copy** button. `error.tsx` boundary likewise. Banners show generic retry/copy guidance (the old "schema not exposed" copy was removed once the public `cenapro_*` accessors went live).
- **Excel Standard:** `table-fixed`, explicit px widths, `px-2 py-1`, `h-8` rows, `text-xs`, `font-mono` right-aligned numerics, `tabular-nums`.
- **Navbar:** registered in `getBreadcrumb()` (`/cenapro`, `/cenapro/production`, `/cenapro/inventory`) + a **separate "Cenapro · Cebu" section** in the Modules dropdown (distinct from "ICTC · Davao").

## Editable-ledger lookups (hardcoded, FK-safe)
The categorical production-event columns are FK-constrained to the seeded `cenapro` lookup tables, which are **not exposed** over PostgREST. So the canonical option lists are **hardcoded constants in `types.ts`** — each string MUST match a seeded `code` value EXACTLY or the FK insert fails. Keep them in lockstep with the cenapro seed migration.

| Column | Options (constant) | Nullable |
|---|---|---|
| `shift_code` | `M` `E` `N` (`SHIFT_CODES`) | no |
| `grade_code` | `3X50` `2X6` `3.5` `4X8` (`GRADE_CODES`) | no |
| `plant_code` | `W6` `W7` `W6/W7` `DVO` (`PLANT_CODES`) | **yes** |
| `warehouse_code` | `WHSE 1/2/3/5/7` (`WAREHOUSE_CODES`) | **yes** (null = unplaced) |
| `source_location_code` | `TNK 1–4` `W6` `W7` `FLEC` `DVO` (`SOURCE_LOCATION_CODES`) | no |
| `disposition_kind` | `flec_bagging` `partner_crusher` `partner_kiln` (`DISPOSITION_KINDS`) | no |
| `partner_equipment_code` | crushers `C1–C4` / kilns `RK1–RK4` / none — **depends on disposition** (`partnerEquipmentOptions()`) | depends |
| `whse_side` | `LS` `RS` or blank (`WHSE_SIDES`) | **yes** |

> **UI note:** `disposition_kind` + `partner_equipment_code` are NOT two separate on-screen columns. The grid + bulk modal render a SINGLE `CCC/FLEC` column (Excel parity) whose options are `CCC_FLEC_OPTIONS` = `FLEC`/`C1`–`C4`/`RK1`–`RK4`. The DB schema stays normalized — the two fields are derived from / formatted to that one cell via the shared `parseCccFlec` / `formatCccFlec` helpers. See "CCC/FLEC single column" below.

## CCC/FLEC single column (one UI column ⇄ two normalized DB fields)
Renzo's Excel has ONE `CCC / FLEC` column; the DB normalizes it into `disposition_kind` + `partner_equipment_code`. Splitting it into two on-screen columns was redundant AND broke copy-paste alignment, so the UI collapses them back into a single `CCC/FLEC` column (between WEIGHT and the FLEC count, matching the Excel column order `… WHSE · SRC · WT · CCC/FLEC · FLEC AMT · WHSE SIDE`). **The DB schema is unchanged** — only the UI merges; the two fields are derived on save.

- **Shared helpers (`types.ts`, pure):** `parseCccFlec(raw)` → `{disposition_kind, partner_equipment_code} | null` (forward; forgiving case/alias; null on unrecognized) and `formatCccFlec(disposition, equipment)` → the single Excel string (inverse). `CCC_FLEC_OPTIONS` is the typeahead/datalist list. These are the canonical pair used by BOTH the inline grid's `saveProductionEvents` payload build AND the bulk modal (via `bulk-paste-utils.mapBulkRowToDirty`).
- **Mapping:** `FLEC` ⇄ `flec_bagging` + null equipment; `C1`–`C4` ⇄ `partner_crusher` + that code; `RK1`–`RK4` ⇄ `partner_kiln` + that code.
- **Inline grid:** the cell is a raw-text `ccc_flec` field (seeded via `formatCccFlec` on load, edited directly, parsed via `parseCccFlec` on Save). A paste-friendly `<Input list>` (datalist), not a dropdown — so a pasted `FLEC`/`C1`/`RK3` lands directly and copy round-trips the raw value.
- **Bulk modal:** `ccc_flec` is a `BulkRow` field (raw on paste, canonicalized on Save via `parseCccFlec`).
- **Validation:** a single typed cell can't produce an inconsistent (disposition, equipment) pair, so the old cross-field CHECK dance is removed; the only failure is an unrecognized value, surfaced as a persistent copyable `errorToast` (HARD RULE) naming the offending row(s).
- **Round-trip proven (2026-06-02):** `C1`→`partner_crusher`+`C1`; `FLEC`→`flec_bagging`+null; `RK3`→`partner_kiln`+`RK3`; and DB→display→parse→DB is identity for all 5 disposition/equipment combos (18/18 assertions). Paste alignment fix = the grid + bulk column maps now both have 12 data columns in Excel order (`ccc_flec` at index 10, between `weight_kg` and `flec_count`), so a pasted tab-block maps positionally.

## Column filtering — the FILTER axis (`?shift=&grade=&plant=&whse=&src=&ccc=`, 2026-08-03)

Per-column **MULTI-SELECT** filtering on the ledger's six categorical columns — **Shift · Grade · Plant · Whse · Source · CCC/FLEC**. It replaced the old single-select `DropdownMenuRadioGroup` (`'ALL'` = no filter) that lived only in the focus grid and covered only five columns (CCC/FLEC, the column Renzo actually asked about, had no filter at all).

### The contract
- **Empty selection = no filter = show all**, and the param is OMITTED (clean URLs). There is deliberately no "show nothing" state, so a filter can never strand the operator on an empty grid with no way back.
- **Columns AND together; values within a column OR together.**
- `__NULL__` is the sentinel for a blank cell (Plant "— None", Whse "— Unplaced") — the same sentinel the pre-multi-select grid used.
- Values are the raw DB codes, so a URL reads plainly: `?whse=WHSE+1,__NULL__&ccc=FLEC,C1`. Unknown values are preserved verbatim rather than dropped.
- State lives ONLY in the URL (CLAUDE.md rule) → shareable, reload-safe, back-button-correct, and readable by the server page. Owned by `ledger-url.ts`; written by `useLedgerFilters()`.

### CCC/FLEC filtering — ONE column, TWO DB fields, an OR across them
Because the on-screen `CCC/FLEC` column stands in for `disposition_kind` + `partner_equipment_code`, a selection like `{FLEC, C1}` is **an OR spanning two columns**:

- `FLEC` ⇒ `disposition_kind = 'flec_bagging'`
- `C1`–`C4` / `RK1`–`RK4` ⇒ `partner_equipment_code IN (…)`

`cccFlecPredicateParts()` in `ledger-url.ts` is the ONE place that split is expressed. `actions.ts` turns it into PostgREST — `.or('disposition_kind.eq.flec_bagging,partner_equipment_code.in.("C1")')` when both sides are picked, and a plain single-column `.in()` when only one side is (no `or` needed). The client matcher compares against the merged `formatCccFlec()` cell. Same rule, two renderings. The menu groups the values **Bagging / Crushers / Kilns** with a one-click group toggle, because picking C1–C4 individually was the exact tedium being complained about.

### Endless filtering is a QUERY, not a view — non-negotiable
The endless sheet is a virtualized keyset pager. A client-side hide there would filter only the LOADED window while scrolling kept fetching unfiltered pages, and `hasOlder`/`hasNewer` would describe the UNFILTERED history — "show only C1" could look nearly empty while thousands of C1 rows sat further back. So in **endless scope the predicates go into the SQL**: `applyLedgerFilters()` is applied to every `fetchLedgerPage` branch (anchor-latest, the period anchor's first-row resolution, the period forward load, the `hasOlder` count probe, cursor-older, cursor-newer), and `useLedgerWindow` carries the filters into every page it pulls. The keyset walk itself is filtered, a page = the next 100 MATCHING rows however far back they are, and the edge flags stay truthful.

- **Composition trap (verified, don't re-litigate):** PostgREST ANDs repeated top-level params **including repeated `or=`**, which is what lets a filter `.or(...)` coexist with the composite-keyset `.or(...)` already on these queries. Measured against live data: keyset-only 752 rows, ccc-only 892, both applied **580** — strictly narrower than either, and byte-identical to the nested-`and(or(…),or(…))` spelling. Values with spaces/slashes (`WHSE 1`, `W6/W7`) are double-quoted inside `in.(…)`.
- **A filter change RESETS the window via the React key** (`` `${anchorKey}|${filterKey}` `` in `page.tsx`), so the server re-prefetches the filtered first page and `firstItemIndex` resets by construction — the pager's prepend-anchor invariant is never violated and there is no scroll jump.
- **A period with ZERO matches does not dead-end.** `fetchLedgerPage`'s period branch falls back to the newest matching rows anywhere in history and says so in `notice`, instead of returning an empty window with both edge flags dead (nothing to scroll toward).
- **Focus scope stays client-side** — it loads one bounded period, so hiding is complete and instant. Both scopes read the SAME `LedgerFilters` from the SAME URL params and the same `matchesLedgerFilters` semantics; only the mechanism differs.

### Options come from the canonical domain, not the loaded window
Deriving options from loaded rows (the old behavior) made the menu mutate while scrolling and made a value that exists only further back unreachable. `FILTER_SPECS` sources them from the seeded `cenapro` lookup constants in `types.ts` instead, plus `mergeDiscoveredOptions()` appends any present-but-unmapped value (badged `new`). Options with no rows in the current window are **dimmed + "none here"** — never hidden, never disabled, because in endless scope the query WILL still find them.

### UNSAVED WORK IS NEVER HIDDEN — the rule, per scope
A dirty row silently disappearing behind a filter destroys work the operator cannot see. The two scopes protect it differently because the mechanisms differ, and BOTH state it in the UI:

- **Focus (client-side hide):** a `new` / `modified` / `deleted` row is **EXEMPT from every filter** and stays in place with its dirty rail. The toolbar reads e.g. `10 of 60 rows · 1 unsaved · 1 unsaved kept visible` (`exemptVisibleCount`). Nothing refetches, so keeping the row costs nothing. *Verified:* with `?whse=WHSE+3` active, editing a visible row's warehouse to `WHSE 1` keeps it on screen and bumps the "unsaved kept visible" counter.
- **Endless (server-side predicate):** changing a filter REFETCHES the window, and a pending edit whose row falls outside the new window would be counted as unsaved while being invisible. So all six filter menus **and** the chip's clear button are **disabled while `totalDirty > 0`**, with the hint *"Save or discard your unsaved changes before filtering — filtering reloads the window."* — the same dirty-guard idiom the period picker / view switcher / scope toggle already use. Draft rows are appended client-side and are never subject to a filter at all. After a Save with filters active, rows the refetched window legitimately can't bring back are counted and surfaced as *"N saved rows hidden by filters"* with a one-click **Show**.

### Affordances
Toolbar `FilterSummaryChip` (`Filters · N ✕`), per-column count chip + primary-tinted chevron on the header, the existing "Clear filters" button kept working, a `filtered` marker in the endless row counter, and a `FilteredEmptyState` that NAMES the responsible filters (`Shift M · CCC/FLEC C3, C4`) rather than a bare "nothing here". Header cells stay `h-8` — the trigger is the same tiny chevron as before.

## Production write path (`saveProductionEvents` → auto-updatable VIEW)
`public.cenapro_production_events` is an **auto-updatable VIEW** with INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE granted to **`authenticated`** (SELECT also to `anon`; `service_role` gets SELECT only — so service-role/anon writes are correctly denied). The server action runs as the logged-in user (`authenticated`), so:
- **Upsert:** `supabase.from('cenapro_production_events').upsert(rows)` — rows WITH `id` UPDATE, rows WITHOUT INSERT.
- **Delete:** `supabase.from('cenapro_production_events').delete().in('id', ids)`.
- The base trigger computes `unique_tag` + `batch_year` — both are **READ-ONLY, never sent**. (`saveProductionEvents` deletes first, then upserts; coerces strings→number/date and strips empties→null via the view's generated `Insert` type; surfaces DB errors as a single string → `errorToast`.)
- The DB enforces CHECK `production_event_partner_equipment_presence` (bagging ⇒ equipment NULL; non-bagging ⇒ equipment NOT NULL); the grid guards both halves client-side.
- **Write path proven (2026-06-01)** by running INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE as the `authenticated` role directly in Postgres: an INSERT with no `id`/`unique_tag`/`batch_year` returned `batch_year=2026` and a computed `unique_tag`; UPDATE + DELETE through the view both succeeded; CHECK confirmed. Throwaway proof cleaned up (0 leftover rows). `npx tsc --noEmit` = 0 errors.

## Opening-balance write path (`saveOpeningBalances` → append-only RPC)
The Flec Inventory STARTING block is written through a **dedicated append-only RPC**, NOT view DML (the `warehouse_opening_balance` table intentionally has no UNIQUE — every "set" is a new row so history is preserved):
- **`public.cenapro_set_opening_balance(p_warehouse_code, p_grade_code, p_side, p_effective_date date, p_count int)`** — SECURITY INVOKER; **INSERTS** a new entry (never UPDATE/DELETE) and RETURNS it. Granted to `authenticated` + `service_role` only (NOT `anon`).
- **`saveOpeningBalances(changes[])`** loops the RPC **once per CHANGED cell** (the grid diffs draft vs seed and sends only edited cells). `effectiveDate` = the page's START date for all cells. Stops at the first failing cell and returns `"Failed to save <grade>/<side> (= <count>, as of <date>): <msg>"` → `errorToast` (persistent + Copy); earlier cells already committed (each RPC is its own INSERT). On success: `revalidatePath('/cenapro/inventory')`, the client toasts + `router.refresh()` so the grid re-seeds and the ledger re-derives from the new opening.
- **Effective semantics:** the opening effective as of date D = greatest `period_start_date ≤ D`, tie-broken by greatest `created_at`. So saving the same (grade, side) at the same START date again simply supersedes (a new later-`created_at` row wins) while the prior value stays in history. Changing the START date sets a fresh opening for a new period.
- **Write path proven (2026-06-01)** from Node via the SDK: `cenapro_set_opening_balance` as the **`anon`** key → `permission denied for table warehouse_opening_balance` (correct — the server action runs as `authenticated`); as **`service_role`** → INSERT returned the row, `cenapro_opening_balances` read it back as the effective opening, and `cenapro_opening_balance_history` listed it. Throwaway sentinel row (WHSE 2 / 4X8 / LS @ 2000-01-01) hard-deleted afterward (0 leftover). `npx tsc --noEmit` = 0 errors; `npm run build` clean.

## RC Deliveries (`/cenapro/deliveries`) — LIVE (data layer + route, 2026-08-04)

Cenapro's **raw-charcoal receipts** — the Cenapro analogue of ICTC's `public.deliveries`, sourced from the **"RC 2026" tab of Cenapro's RC workbook**. **971 receipts** + 244 moisture sub-samples are loaded (see "Live state" below — 991 came in from the workbook, 22 duplicates were deleted 2026-08-04, 2 have since been created in the app). The **route is live at `/cenapro/deliveries`** — see its own **`deliveries/CONTEXT.md`** for the UI (columns, the two single-column cells, the formula cells, the per-cell nav resolver, the two scopes, price gating). The rest of this section is the schema, the read model, the write path and the import that it sits on. Liquidation (assigning cheques to receipts) is the feature this exists to support.

**Migrations:** `supabase/migrations/20260804070000_cenapro_rc_deliveries.sql` (tables, generated money columns, touch trigger, RLS, views, accessors, RPCs, dimension seed), `20260804071000_cenapro_rc_delivery_sheet_total_witness.sql` (adds `sheet_total_matches` to the read model) `20260804072000_cenapro_rc_delivery_duplicate_groups.sql` (adds the duplicate-pair surface — see "Duplicate pairing" below) `20260805090000_cenapro_rc_delivery_flag_resolution.sql` (adds the flag-resolution surface — see "Flag resolution" below), `20260805100000_cenapro_rc_delivery_audit.sql` (the append-only change trail — see "Audit trail" below) `20260805110000_cenapro_rc_supplier_subgroups.sql` (the cheque-payee subgroup — see "Supplier subgroups" below) `20260805120000_cenapro_rc_payments.sql` (banks, bank accounts, payments, the payment audit trail and the per-supplier running balance — see "Liquidation" below) `20260805130000_cenapro_rc_supplier_opening_balance.sql` (the append-only stated opening balances and the reworked balance views — see "Opening balances" below) and `20260806060000_cenapro_rc_payment_allocation.sql` (the payment→receipt allocation edge, the settlement / payment-state read models, `advance_php`, the allocation audit trail and the release-on-delete rule — see "Allocation" below). **Importer:** `scripts/cenapro/import-rc-deliveries.mjs` + its committed source `scripts/cenapro/rc2026-extract.json`.

### Three decisions that govern everything here
1. **Deliveries share ZERO dimensions with production.** `cenapro.rc_supplier` / `cenapro.rc_destination` are NEW tables, deliberately NOT `cenapro.warehouse` / `source_location` / `plant`. A raw-charcoal yard (`WHSE A`, `WHSE 13`, `W6 PROD`, `DRYER`) is a different physical place with a different code space from a finished-goods FLEC warehouse. **Never add an FK from an `rc_*` table to a production dimension.**
2. **Money is decomposed, never one opaque number.** The sheet's WT column is often `=27045*88%` (gross scale weight − a quality deduction) and TTL PRICE always multiplies the DISCOUNTED weight. So `net_weight_kg`, `price_php_kg` and `total_price_php` are **STORED GENERATED** columns over `gross_weight_kg` / `deduction_pct` / `base_price_php_kg` / `price_adjustment_php_kg`. Nothing can overwrite them — the DB rejects any write to a generated column.
3. **Imported rows land as reference data — flagged, not fixed** (all 991 of them, on import day). Nothing is silently corrected. Every extractor complaint is stored on the row in `import_flags` (jsonb array of `{kind, detail, raw}`) plus the fast boolean `is_suspected_duplicate`, and an unresolved label leaves its FK NULL while `supplier_raw` / `destination_raw` / `delivery_date_raw` keep the operator's original text.

### Tables (`cenapro`)
- **`rc_supplier`** — the cheque-payee dimension. `code` PK, `display_name`, `sort_order`, `active`, `notes`, plus (2026-08-05) **`parent_code`** (self-FK, the SUBGROUP — see "Supplier subgroups" below) and **`row_version`**. 12 seeded, all still roots. Exists so a trader can be split or re-pointed (PALAWAN → RANDY / BROOKE'S) **without a migration**.
- **`rc_supplier_audit`** — the append-only change trail for `rc_supplier`, trigger-written (2026-08-05). See "Supplier subgroups" below.
- **`rc_destination`** — the raw-charcoal yard dimension. `code` PK, `display_name`, `kind` (`warehouse` | `plant_feed` | `dryer`), `has_sides`, `sort_order`, `active`, `notes`. 16 seeded. `has_sides` is descriptive only — it is NOT enforced against `rc_delivery.destination_side`, so re-classifying a yard can never invalidate historic rows.
- **`rc_delivery`** — the fact table, columns in the sheet's own left-to-right order: `id` · `delivery_date` · `delivery_date_raw` · `delivery_year`(gen) · `truck_no` · `supplier_code`(FK) · `supplier_origin` · `permit_no` · `supplier_raw` · `sacks` · `gross_weight_kg` · `deduction_pct` · `net_weight_kg`(gen) · `weight_formula` · `bd` · `moisture_pct` · `grit` · `ash` · `dust` · `vm` · `fc` · `destination_code`(FK) · `destination_side` · `destination_raw` · `remarks` · `base_price_php_kg` · `price_adjustment_php_kg` · `price_php_kg`(gen) · `price_formula` · `total_price_php`(gen) · `provenance` · `source_sheet` · `source_row` · `sheet_total_php` · `is_suspected_duplicate` · `import_flags` · `row_version` · `created_at/by` · `updated_at/by`.
- **`rc_delivery_sample`** — the `#1 / #2 / #3` (also `BLUE SACKS`, `NO MARK/SUNDRY`) moisture sub-samples, 1–6 per receipt. `delivery_id` FK ON DELETE CASCADE, `position`, free-text `label`, the same seven nullable lab columns, `source_row`. UNIQUE `(delivery_id, position)`.

### The generated-column-referencing-a-generated-column problem
A Postgres generated column **cannot reference another generated column** (verified live on PG 17.6: `cannot use generated column "net" in column generation expression`). `net_weight_kg` and `price_php_kg` reference only base columns, so they are fine — but `total_price_php` is naturally `net × price`, exactly the forbidden shape.

**Resolution: repeat the full arithmetic over the base columns**, and guard the duplication with a CHECK.
- `net_weight_kg = trim_scale(gross_weight_kg * (100 - COALESCE(deduction_pct,0)) / 100)`
- `price_php_kg  = trim_scale(base_price_php_kg + COALESCE(price_adjustment_php_kg,0))`
- `total_price_php = trim_scale(COALESCE(gross_weight_kg * (100 - COALESCE(deduction_pct,0)) / 100, 0) * COALESCE(base_price_php_kg + COALESCE(price_adjustment_php_kg,0), 0))`
- `CONSTRAINT cenapro_rc_delivery_total_consistent CHECK (total_price_php = COALESCE(net_weight_kg,0) * COALESCE(price_php_kg,0))` — a CHECK **may** reference generated columns, so the two forms cannot drift apart.

Chosen over pushing the total into the read view because liquidation will SUM and index the payable total, a view column is only correct for callers who remember the view, and a STORED generated column is the only form that is *impossible* to overwrite. `COALESCE(...,0)` on both factors so a receipt with no weight or no price reads **₱0 payable, not NULL** — which is what the workbook's own TTL PRICE cell says and what a SUM needs.

**Exact decimal, never rounded.** The workbook keeps fractional centavos (row 47 = ₱1,027,132.875). Rounding to 2 dp reproduces only 972 of 991 sheet totals; the unrounded numeric product reproduces **991 of 991**. (An import-day measurement over the 991 rows then present — the rule it proves is permanent, the row count is not.) `trim_scale()` only strips the trailing zeros numeric division leaves behind.

### Data quality is data, not constraints
- `delivery_date` is **NULLABLE** (the brief asked for NOT NULL). Two rows read `5/262026`, which has no parseable date; requiring one would mean dropping them or inventing a date, both forbidden by decision 3. The NOT NULL *intent* survives as `CHECK cenapro_rc_delivery_date_present`: **a NULL date is legal only on a `sheet_import` row that carries the operator's literal text in `delivery_date_raw`.** An app write with no date is still refused.
- **The lab columns carry NO range CHECK**, unlike `cenapro.analysis_sample`. A range CHECK turns one operator typo into a failure of the whole import — the exact "silently drop it" failure mode decision 3 exists to prevent. Only shape guards exist (`sacks >= 0`, `gross_weight_kg >= 0`, `0 <= deduction_pct < 100`, `base_price_php_kg >= 0`, `destination_side ∈ LFT|RT`).
- `sheet_total_php` holds the workbook's **own printed TTL PRICE** as an independent witness against the computed total. Never used in a calculation. The read model exposes `sheet_total_matches` because **PostgREST cannot filter one column against another** — without it every consumer would re-derive exact decimal arithmetic in floating-point JavaScript.

### Duplicate pairing — which row is this a copy OF? (2026-08-04)

> **STATUS 2026-08-05 — the four columns still EXIST and still WORK. They simply pair NOTHING today.**
> The keep-or-drop decision was made and executed: all **22** duplicate copies were hard-DELETEd on
> 2026-08-04, taking ₱17,185,938.70 with them. So live right now: `is_suspected_duplicate` = **0 rows**,
> `duplicate_group_key IS NOT NULL` = **0 rows**, and the `?issue=duplicate` lens is empty. **Nothing here
> was removed, disabled or reverted** — the derivation runs on every read, and the day a receipt is pasted
> twice again it pairs on sight. Everything below describes the live mechanism and the import-day
> measurements that chose it. (That deletion is also the reason `rc_delivery_audit` now exists — it left
> no trace anywhere; see "Audit trail" below.)

`is_suspected_duplicate` is a bare boolean, and **the importer flagged only the SECOND copy of each receipt** — so on import day a query over it returned 22 orphans and could never name their partners. The read model therefore re-derives the pairing from the DATA, in four appended columns:

| Column | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `duplicate_group_key` | `text` | md5 of the exact-copy signature. **NULL when the receipt has no twin.** |
| `duplicate_group_size` | `int` | How many receipts share the signature. **1 = unique.** |
| `duplicate_group_ordinal` | `int` | 1-based position in the group. **1 = the original, 2 = the paste.** |
| `duplicate_peer_ids` | `uuid[]` | The OTHER receipt ids in the group, self excluded. NULL on a singleton. |

**The signature (22 fields):** `delivery_date` · `truck_no` · `supplier_code` / `supplier_origin` / `permit_no` / `supplier_raw` · `sacks` · `gross_weight_kg` · `deduction_pct` · the seven lab values (`bd`/`moisture_pct`/`grit`/`ash`/`dust`/`vm`/`fc`) · `destination_code` / `destination_side` / `destination_raw` · `remarks` · `base_price_php_kg` · `price_adjustment_php_kg`. Every numeric is `trim_scale()`-normalised so 20725 and 20725.00 hash alike. The digest is `md5(ROW(...)::text)` — record output renders NULL and `''` differently, so two NULL trucks pair while a NULL and an empty string do not.

**Chosen empirically over all 991 rows then present, not assumed.** A NARROWER tuple (date + truck + supplier + gross + deduction + base price) finds 25 pairs — three of them FALSE, and each names a field the signature must carry: rows 1321/1322 differ only in their lab readings (two different SEVILLA lab samples), rows 907/908 only in `destination_side` LFT vs RT plus `remarks` (one load split across two sides of WHSE A), rows 1422/1423 only in `destination_code`. The adopted tuple finds **exactly 22 pairs, all of size 2**, matching the 22 flagged rows one-for-one. (That was the state on 2026-08-04. The 22 flagged copies have since been deleted; their 22 originals remain, now unpaired and indistinguishable from any other receipt — which is correct.)

**Deliberately EXCLUDED from the signature:** `is_suspected_duplicate` / `import_flags` (the grouping must be independent of the importer's opinion, or the unflagged original could never join its own group — this is the whole point); `provenance` / `source_sheet` / `source_row` (exactly what DIFFERS between an original and its paste, 639 vs 664); the generated money columns and `sheet_total_php` (functions of base columns already in the tuple); `weight_formula` / `price_formula` (how a number was typed, not what it is); `delivery_date_raw`; the `row_version` / `created_*` / `updated_*` bookkeeping.

**The trade-off, stated plainly:** because the lab panel and `remarks` are in the signature, editing EITHER copy un-pairs the group. That is correct — once a value differs the rows are no longer exact copies. `is_suspected_duplicate` is untouched and keeps flagging until a human clears it.

**No ₱ leak.** `base_price_php_kg` is an INPUT to the digest, but an md5 over 22 fields is one-way and discloses only that two rows are equal, never what either is worth. **None of the four columns needs adding to `stripPrices()`** in `deliveries/types.ts` — a gated viewer sees the same grouping an ungated one does, because "this receipt is duplicated" is an operational fact, not a money fact.

**Implementation:** one named `WINDOW w AS (PARTITION BY signature ORDER BY delivery_date, source_row NULLS LAST, created_at, id ROWS BETWEEN UNBOUNDED PRECEDING AND UNBOUNDED FOLLOWING)` drives `count(*)`, `row_number()` and `array_agg(id)`. **The explicit unbounded frame is load-bearing** — `count`/`array_agg` are aggregates used as window functions, so an ORDER BY without a frame would give them the default `RANGE …CURRENT ROW`, i.e. a RUNNING count and a RUNNING array. Aggregate-level `array_agg(id ORDER BY …)` is not an option (Postgres does not implement aggregate ORDER BY for window functions), so the window's own ORDER BY is what makes `duplicate_peer_ids` deterministic.

**A STORED GENERATED signature column + index is NOT possible and would not help.** `md5(ROW(...)::text)` is not IMMUTABLE and Postgres refuses it outright (`generation expression is not immutable`, verified live); and no index can prune `count(*) OVER (PARTITION BY …)`, which must see every row regardless. The signature CTE therefore projects only `(id, delivery_date, source_row, created_at, signature)` so the sort payload stays narrow. Measured on the 991 rows live at the time, a realistic keyset page (`WHERE (delivery_date,id) > (…) ORDER BY delivery_date NULLS FIRST, id LIMIT 100`) goes **3.7 ms → 12.0 ms**; the keyset predicate still pushes into `idx_cenapro_rc_delivery_date` (it now uses the index where the old shape chose a seq scan). The pre-existing per-row LATERAL sample rollup remains the plan's biggest buffer consumer (1089 buffers over 514 loops) — revisit it alongside this window if the table ever reaches tens of thousands of rows.

### Flag resolution — is the flag's problem STILL TRUE? (2026-08-05)

**`import_flags` IS NEVER MUTATED. Not by this migration, not by the importer, not by any server action.** It is the permanent record of what the extractor saw on the day — the only surviving witness that the workbook literally said `WHSE A/R#16` — and "flagged, never fixed" (decision 3) is why. What migration `20260805090000` adds is the ability to **DERIVE, on read**, whether each flag's underlying condition still holds. Nothing is cleared, nothing is deleted, and the migration contains no `UPDATE` at all.

**Why it was needed.** Renzo repaired most of the flagged rows, and the `?issue=flagged` lens became a lie: **12 receipts carry a flag but only 2 still have a live problem** (live, 2026-08-05). A worklist where five in six entries are already done is a worklist nobody opens, and every future repair makes it worse.

| Column | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `import_flags_state` | `jsonb` | The same array as `import_flags`, each element carrying an extra derived **`resolved`** boolean. Every key the extractor wrote is preserved verbatim — this **adds** a field, it never edits or drops one. Always an array (empty, never NULL), matching `import_flags`. |
| `unresolved_flag_count` | `int` | How many of this row's flags still describe a LIVE problem. **This is the honest worklist size.** |
| `resolved_flag_count` | `int` | How many describe a condition a human has since repaired. |
| `has_unresolved_flags` | `boolean` | `unresolved_flag_count > 0` — **the predicate the `?issue=flagged` lens should filter on.** `has_import_flags` is now the wrong test (12 vs 2). |

`import_flag_count` / `has_import_flags` are **unchanged and stay** — they are the historical record. The new pair is the live one.

**The predicate per kind.** Each asks ONE question of the row's CURRENT state, never of the flag's own text. Counts are live 2026-08-05 over 971 receipts.

| kind | flags | RESOLVED ⟺ | live |
|---|---|---|---|
| `destination_unmapped` | 5 | `destination_code IS NOT NULL` | 5 resolved (all now `WHSE 3A`) |
| `supplier_no_trader_prefix` | 3 | `supplier_code IS NOT NULL` | 3 resolved (all `NEGROS`) |
| `date_unparseable` | 2 | `delivery_date IS NOT NULL` | 2 resolved (both `2026-05-06`) |
| `bd_out_of_range` | 1 | `bd IS NOT NULL` | **1 unresolved** (`source_row` 928) |
| `supplier_unmapped` | 1 | `supplier_code IS NOT NULL` | **1 unresolved** (`source_row` 343) |
| `suspected_duplicate` | 0 | `NOT is_suspected_duplicate` **AND** no exact twin remains | none carry this kind |
| *anything else* | — | **never resolved** | — |

Three of those need their reasoning stated, because guessing them wrong hides a real problem:

- **`supplier_no_trader_prefix` is NOT informational and NOT permanently unresolved.** Its text ("`MANAGAYTAY` has no trader prefix; mapped to NEGROS for review") describes a property of an **immutable historical string**, so a predicate over the string could never become false — permanent, un-clearable noise, exactly what this change exists to remove. It gets the **same predicate as `supplier_unmapped`**, deliberately: both flags say "we may not know who to pay", one because the importer could not decide and one because it guessed, and both are answered by "is there a payee on this row now?". If a human ever clears `supplier_code`, the flag correctly fires again. What the database genuinely **cannot** see is whether a human has *reviewed and agreed with* the guess — there is no acknowledgement column, and deriving one from `row_version` would be a fiction (all three sit at `row_version` 1, which means *untouched*, not *unreviewed*). That residual belongs in the UI copy, not in a resolution state.
- **`bd_out_of_range` does NOT test a range.** There is **no BD range anywhere in this schema** — the lab columns deliberately carry no range CHECK (see "Data quality is data, not constraints" above), so a threshold invented here would be a number with no authority masquerading as a rule. The predicate asks the question the flag actually created: the extractor **refused to store** `23995.0`, so the receipt has **no BD reading at all**. That is real, checkable and closeable — someone looks the reading up and types it in. (Only 4 of 971 receipts have a NULL `bd`, so a missing BD is exceptional, not the norm.)
- **An UNKNOWN kind is never resolved.** A new extractor version, or an element that is not even a JSON object, counts as unresolved and is wrapped rather than dropped so it still renders. Getting a verdict wrong in the "resolved" direction silently hides a problem; getting it wrong the other way merely leaves a row where a human will see it. That asymmetry decides the default.

**Price gating: none.** Two counts, a boolean, and the extractor's own `{kind, detail, raw}` text — no `stripPrices()` entry is needed in `deliveries/types.ts`, same reasoning as the duplicate columns. "This receipt still has an open data problem" is an operational fact every role needs.

**Implementation + cost.** The array is expanded by a set-returning expression in its **own CTE guarded to `import_flags <> '[]'`**, so 959 of the 971 receipts never enter the SRF and are `LEFT JOIN`ed to a `COALESCE`d zero — not a correlated subquery per flag, which would run once per row of the ledger page for no result. On the endless ledger's own page query (`ORDER BY delivery_date DESC NULLS LAST, id DESC LIMIT 120`): **`shared hit=2132` → `shared hit=2145`, i.e. +13 buffers**, one guarded pass over a 36-block table, no new window and a 12-row sort. The one plan-shape change is that `grouped` is now referenced twice and is therefore materialised as a CTE Scan rather than inlined. The pre-existing per-row sample-rollup LATERAL (2020 buffers) remains the view's dominant cost, unchanged.

### Views
- **`cenapro.view_rc_delivery`** (`security_invoker`) — the read model: the fact row + `supplier_name` / `destination_name` / `destination_kind` / `destination_has_sides` + `sample_count` / `sample_avg_moisture_pct` (SQL `avg`, rounded to 3 dp — never in TypeScript) + the data-quality surface `sheet_total_matches` / `import_flag_count` / `has_import_flags` / `supplier_unresolved` / `destination_unresolved` + the duplicate-pair surface `duplicate_group_key` / `duplicate_group_size` / `duplicate_group_ordinal` / `duplicate_peer_ids` (see "Duplicate pairing" above) + the flag-resolution surface `import_flags_state` / `unresolved_flag_count` / `resolved_flag_count` / `has_unresolved_flags` (see "Flag resolution" above). **Every new column is APPENDED at the end** — the original 52 keep their exact names, types and order, then the four duplicate columns, then the four resolution columns, because several consumers `select('*')`. That is why migrations `20260804072000` and `20260805090000` both use `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` on the inner view (Postgres refuses the replace if any existing column moved, which makes the column list self-verifying) and only DROP+CREATE the `public` accessor, whose `SELECT v.*` was expanded at creation time.
- **`public.cenapro_rc_deliveries`**, **`public.cenapro_rc_delivery_samples`**, **`public.cenapro_rc_suppliers`**, **`public.cenapro_rc_destinations`** — thin single-table projections, therefore **AUTO-UPDATABLE** (`is_insertable_into=YES`, `is_updatable=YES`, no INSTEAD OF trigger), exactly like `public.cenapro_production_events`. **Writers must OMIT the four generated columns** (`delivery_year`, `net_weight_kg`, `price_php_kg`, `total_price_php`) — the base table rejects an explicit value (`cannot insert a non-DEFAULT value into column "total_price_php"`), by design.
- **`public.cenapro_rc_delivery_rows`** — read-only accessor over the enriched view (`is_insertable_into=NO`).
- **`cenapro.view_rc_supplier_group`** (`security_invoker`) → **`public.cenapro_rc_supplier_groups`** — the ONE definition of supplier-subgroup membership (`group_code`, `is_parent`/`is_child`, `child_codes`). Read-only. See "Supplier subgroups" above.
- **`public.cenapro_rc_supplier_audit`** — read-only accessor over `cenapro.rc_supplier_audit`. Carries **no ₱ columns** (unlike `cenapro_rc_delivery_audit`), because `rc_supplier` has none.
