- **`cenapro.view_rc_bank_account`**, **`cenapro.view_rc_payment`**, **`cenapro.view_rc_payment_state`**, **`cenapro.view_rc_payment_allocation`**, **`cenapro.view_rc_delivery_settlement`**, **`cenapro.view_rc_supplier_balance`**, **`cenapro.view_rc_supplier_group_balance`**, **`cenapro.view_rc_supplier_opening_balance`** (all `security_invoker`) → the read-only accessors `public.cenapro_rc_bank_accounts` / `cenapro_rc_payments` / `cenapro_rc_payment_state` / `cenapro_rc_payment_allocations` / `cenapro_rc_delivery_settlement` / `cenapro_rc_supplier_balances` / `cenapro_rc_supplier_group_balances` / `cenapro_rc_supplier_opening_balances` / `cenapro_rc_supplier_opening_balance_history`. **The liquidation read model** — see "Liquidation", "Opening balances" and "Allocation" below. All are **entirely ₱-bearing**; every consumer is behind `canViewPrices()`.

### Write path (`cenapro_save_analysis_sample` idiom — compare-and-set on `row_version`)
All three RPCs are SECURITY INVOKER, `SET search_path = ''`, EXECUTE revoked from `PUBLIC`/`anon`, granted to `authenticated` + `service_role`.
- **`public.cenapro_save_rc_delivery(p_id uuid, p_expected_row_version int, p_patch jsonb) → jsonb`** — `p_id` NULL ⇒ INSERT (`provenance='app'`, `delivery_date` required); otherwise UPDATE gated on `p_expected_row_version` **in the same statement as the write**. **Patch-shaped, not a 26-argument signature** — the UI is a grid where an operator edits one cell. The patch keys are **allowlisted**: an unknown key REFUSES the whole call (`unsupported_field`) rather than being ignored, and `provenance` / `source_sheet` / `source_row` / `sheet_total_php` / `import_flags` / the generated columns are deliberately absent, so an app write can never rewrite an imported row's provenance or erase its flags. Outcomes: `inserted` | `updated` | `version_conflict` | `not_found` | `unsupported_field` | `invalid`.
- **`public.cenapro_delete_rc_delivery(p_id uuid, p_expected_row_version int) → jsonb`** — same gate; children cascade and the count comes back as `samples_deleted`. Outcomes: `deleted` | `version_conflict` | `not_found` | `invalid`.
- **`public.cenapro_save_rc_delivery_samples(p_delivery_id uuid, p_expected_row_version int, p_samples jsonb) → jsonb`** — replaces a receipt's WHOLE sample block in one call (pass `[]` to clear). **There is no separate version token on the child**: gating on the PARENT's `row_version` is simpler and stricter (it also catches "someone edited the receipt while I retyped its samples"). The parent UPDATE runs FIRST so it row-locks the receipt before any child row moves, and the parent's touch trigger bumps `row_version`, making a sample edit visible to another editor's optimistic lock. Outcomes: `saved` | `version_conflict` | `not_found` | `unsupported_field` | `invalid`.
- **`public.cenapro_save_rc_supplier(p_code text, p_expected_row_version int, p_patch jsonb) → jsonb`** (2026-08-05) — the dimension's write path, including the SUBGROUP. `p_expected_row_version` NULL ⇒ INSERT with code `p_code`; otherwise UPDATE gated on that version **in the same statement as the write**. Allowlist: `display_name` / `parent_code` / `sort_order` / `active` / `notes` — an unknown key refuses the whole call, and **`code` is not editable** (a re-key cascades through `rc_delivery` and splits the supplier audit trail). Pass `parent_code: null` to clear a grouping. Outcomes: `inserted` | `updated` | `version_conflict` | `not_found` | `unsupported_field` | `invalid`. Every refusal carries a human-readable `message` — these land straight in a toast.
- `row_version` / `updated_at` / `updated_by` are bumped by the **trigger** `cenapro.fn_touch_rc_delivery` (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE), not by the RPCs — so a raw write through the auto-updatable accessor view advances the token too and cannot silently defeat the lock. `cenapro.fn_touch_rc_supplier` is the same idiom on `rc_supplier` (`row_version` + `updated_at` only — that table has no actor columns; who changed a grouping is answered by `rc_supplier_audit.changed_by`).

### Audit trail — `cenapro.rc_delivery_audit` (2026-08-05, migration `20260805100000`)

**Why it exists.** On 2026-08-04, 22 duplicate receipts were hard-DELETEd, taking ₱17,185,938.70 with them, and there is **no record of it anywhere**: `public.audit_logs` contains **zero** rows mentioning cenapro or `rc_delivery`, and this table had never had an audit trigger. Not which rows, not who, not when. For reference data transcribed out of a workbook that can be re-read, that is arguably survivable — **for a cheque it is not**, and liquidation is about to point cheques at these rows. This is Step 1 of the liquidation plan (`.agents/prompts/liquidation-feature.md` §7), deliberately built first because retrofitting an audit onto a system already writing money is the expensive version.

**Why a `cenapro` table and not `public.audit_logs`.** `audit_logs` is ICTC's and it is not an inert log: the ICTC audit UI reads it, `audit_comments` / `notification_subscriptions` join it, and `_insert_notification` fans out of it. Cenapro rows there would surface inside another tenant's screens — the coupling `CLAUDE.md` forbids. Same reasoning that produced `cenapro.production_event_audit` on 2026-08-03, and this is a line-for-line clone of that migration's idiom.

**ONE table for BOTH the receipt and its sub-samples.** `rc_delivery_sample` is a CASCADE child; a moisture draw has no meaning apart from its receipt, and the question anyone asks is *"what has happened to THIS receipt"*. So one table, discriminated by `entity` (`'delivery'` | `'sample'`) and **always keyed by the parent `delivery_id`** — a sample's audit row carries the PARENT's id, never only its own. A receipt's whole history is therefore one indexed query, not a UNION over two trails.

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `delivery_id` | **The parent receipt, for both entities.** The read key. |
| `entity` | `'delivery'` or `'sample'`. CHECKed, with a shape guard: a sample row must name its `sample_id`, a delivery row must not. |
| `sample_id` / `sample_position` | The sub-sample's own identity; NULL on a delivery row. |
| `delivery_date` / `supplier_code` / `truck_no` | **Denormalized identity**, so the trail stays readable after the receipt is deleted — the same reason `production_event_audit` denormalizes `unique_tag` / `recv_date`. |
| `operation` | `INSERT` \| `UPDATE` \| `DELETE`. |
| `changed` | UPDATE only: `{column: {old, new}}` for the columns that actually moved. `'{}'` on INSERT/DELETE, where the whole row IS the change. |
| `snapshot` | The complete row as jsonb — NEW on INSERT/UPDATE, OLD on DELETE. |
| `source` | From `current_setting('cenapro.audit_source')`. **NULL for every write today** — the three save RPCs and the importer predate this trail and were not modified. |
| `changed_at` / `changed_by` / `changed_by_role` | When, `auth.uid()` (NULL for a service-role/importer write — the honest answer), and the PostgREST role. **No FK to `profiles`**: an audit row must outlive the account that wrote it. |

**Five design calls, each with a reason:**

- **AFTER, not BEFORE.** STORED GENERATED columns are not computed until after all BEFORE triggers run, so a BEFORE trigger's `NEW.total_price_php` is *not* the value the row is about to have — auditing from there would record the money wrong, which is worse than not recording it. (Same family of mistake as BUG-017.) AFTER also means the trail sees the `row_version`/`updated_at` that `fn_touch_rc_delivery` actually stored.
- **The generated money columns are deliberately IN the diff, not filtered out.** `net_weight_kg`, `price_php_kg` and `total_price_php` are derived — and they are the numbers a cheque is cut against. A weight correction that silently moves ₱54,000 of payable total is precisely what someone will come looking for. Verified live: editing `gross_weight_kg` 10,000 → 12,000 produces `changed` = `{gross_weight_kg, net_weight_kg, total_price_php}` with `total_price_php` 270,000 → 324,000.
- **`changed` excludes `updated_at` AND `row_version`.** The touch trigger bumps both on *every* write, so a diff that reported them could never be empty and the no-op skip could never fire. An UPDATE whose only difference is that bookkeeping returns NULL and writes nothing. Verified: a no-op UPDATE moved `row_version` 1 → 2 and left the trail untouched.
- **Trigger, not RPC.** `rc_delivery` has several live writers — the three `cenapro_save_rc_delivery*` RPCs, direct DML through the auto-updatable `public.cenapro_rc_deliveries` accessor, and the REST importer. An RPC-side audit would trail one and miss the others, which is worse than none because it *looks* complete.
- **SECURITY DEFINER + nobody holds write.** `authenticated` and `service_role` hold **SELECT only**; the identity sequence is revoked from `anon` and `authenticated` by name. RLS is additionally ON with a **SELECT-only policy and no insert/update/delete policy at all** — the one divergence from `production_event_audit`, taken because this table's whole value is that it cannot be tampered with, so a future blanket `GRANT … ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA cenapro` still cannot write a row. The definer triggers run as the table owner and bypass RLS, so the trail keeps being written. Verified as `authenticated` in a rolled-back transaction: SELECT **OK**; forge a row / erase the trail / rewrite a snapshot all **DENIED**; and the same role's ordinary receipt INSERT was still trailed.

**The CASCADE-delete subtlety, and why it is handled.** A sample row does not carry the receipt's identity, and on a cascade delete the parent is **already gone** by the time any AFTER trigger fires. Verified live (and independently of trigger *name*): the parent's own AFTER DELETE trigger fires **before** the RI cascade reaches the children. So when the live parent lookup misses, `fn_audit_rc_delivery_sample` reads the identity back off the parent's own DELETE audit row, which is guaranteed to be sitting there already. Verified end-to-end: deleting a receipt with one sample produced `delivery/DELETE` and `sample/DELETE` rows **both** carrying the truck number and date, and the trail survived the receipt.

**Read it through `public.cenapro_rc_delivery_audit`** (`security_invoker = true`, SELECT only, `anon` revoked) — `cenapro` is not exposed to PostgREST, so a trail nobody can read from the app is only half a trail. **₱ NOTE:** `changed` and `snapshot` carry the price columns, so any server action exposing this view is subject to the `canViewPrices()` gate in `deliveries/types.ts` — a gated viewer must not receive a snapshot containing `total_price_php`. Nothing consumes the view yet; **the per-row history UI is not built.**

**No backfill, nothing synthetic.** The migration writes not one historical row (`count(*)` = **0** immediately after applying). The 22 deletions left no evidence, and inventing a row for them would put a fabrication in the one table whose entire value is that it is not fabricated. **The trail starts 2026-08-05**; everything before it is simply unrecorded. Advisors after applying: **0 ERROR**, the only findings on the new objects being the project-wide `pg_graphql_authenticated_table_exposed` WARN that every `authenticated`-readable relation carries.

### Supplier subgroups — `rc_supplier.parent_code` (2026-08-05, migration `20260805110000`)

**Liquidation Step 2.** Renzo: *"Paquibot would have a subgroup of suppliers like Llanto. The system should be able to understand that if a cheque is labeled Paquibot but is being assigned to a Llanto delivery, then it should push through because it verified that Llanto is a sub-supplier of Paquibot."*

**The group is a PAYMENT fact, not a delivery fact** — it says who may be paid for whom. It therefore lives on `rc_supplier` and nowhere near `rc_delivery`: the migration adds no column to the fact table, changes none of its views and touches none of its RPCs. **Nothing was seeded** — all 12 suppliers are still roots, and nothing here ever infers a grouping from name similarity.

| Object | What it is |
|---|---|
| `cenapro.rc_supplier.parent_code` | Self-FK, `ON UPDATE CASCADE ON DELETE SET NULL` (rc_delivery.supplier_code's idiom: a re-point never orphans, retiring a parent just makes its children roots again). `CHECK (parent_code IS DISTINCT FROM code)`. Indexed — PG does not index an FK automatically. |
| `cenapro.rc_supplier.row_version` | Optimistic-concurrency token, bumped by the **trigger** `fn_touch_rc_supplier` (which also fixes a latent bug: `updated_at` never moved on an UPDATE, only on INSERT). |
| `cenapro.view_rc_supplier_group` → `public.cenapro_rc_supplier_groups` | **The ONE definition of group membership.** Per supplier: `parent_code` / `parent_display_name` / **`group_code` = `coalesce(parent_code, code)`** / `group_display_name` / `group_sort_order` / `is_parent` / `is_child` / `child_count` / `child_codes` + `row_version`. Read-only accessor. |
| `cenapro.rc_supplier_audit` → `public.cenapro_rc_supplier_audit` | Append-only trail, SECURITY DEFINER trigger, RLS + SELECT-only. Columns `supplier_code` / `display_name` / **`parent_code`** / `operation` / `changed` / `snapshot` / `source` / `changed_at` / `changed_by` / `changed_by_role`. |
| `public.cenapro_save_rc_supplier(p_code, p_expected_row_version, p_patch)` | The write path. Allowlist `display_name, parent_code, sort_order, active, notes`. Outcomes `inserted \| updated \| version_conflict \| not_found \| unsupported_field \| invalid`. |

**ONE LEVEL, ENFORCED — not merely documented.** A supplier with children may not have a parent, and a supplier with a parent may not acquire children. That is a cross-row invariant so a CHECK cannot express it: `tr_cenapro_rc_supplier_one_level` is a **CONSTRAINT TRIGGER (DEFERRABLE INITIALLY IMMEDIATE)** that fires on any row being pointed at a parent. **One rule covers both directions** — "give Y a parent while Y has children" is the same statement evaluated on Y — so there is no separate parent-side check. Deferrable so a legal reorganisation (X leaves the group in the same transaction that makes Y a child of Z) can be done under `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL DEFERRED`; verified live that the reorganisation is allowed and a genuine chain is still refused at the deferred check. Concurrency is closed by a transaction advisory lock (all parent-setting writes serialise) plus `FOR SHARE` on the parent row (READ COMMITTED re-reads the latest committed version).

**The hierarchy is checked TWICE, on purpose.** The RPC pre-checks it to produce a *readable* refusal (`"LLANTO" is already a sub-supplier of "PAQUIBOT", so it cannot also be a parent…`); the constraint trigger is the guarantee that holds for raw DML, the importer and a manual SQL fix. Folding the rule into the UPDATE's `WHERE` would have made a one-level violation indistinguishable from a version conflict — the wrong thing to tell someone who just tried to build a three-level chain. Verified: refused through the RPC **and** through raw `UPDATE`/`INSERT`, in both directions, SQLSTATE `23514`.

**Why the trail.** Re-pointing a parent **retroactively changes which past payments were legitimate** — a cheque to PAQUIBOT that legally settled a LLANTO receipt yesterday becomes an illegal allocation the moment LLANTO leaves the group, with no record that the *rule* changed rather than the cheque. It is a **separate table from `rc_delivery_audit`**, not an `entity`-discriminated extension of it: that table's read key is `delivery_id uuid NOT NULL` and its whole shape exists so a receipt's history is one indexed lookup; a supplier has no `delivery_id`, and folding it in would mean a nullable key plus a second text key, taxing the hot per-receipt read to save one small relation. Same idiom, two tables. `changed` excludes `updated_at` **and** `row_version` (both bumped by the touch trigger, so a diff reporting them could never be empty and the no-op skip could never fire) — verified: a no-op UPDATE bumped `row_version` 1→2 and wrote nothing. A code rename produces **two** audit rows (the parent's `code` change and the child's cascaded `parent_code` change); deleting a parent produces its DELETE row plus the child's `parent_code → null`.

**`public.cenapro_rc_suppliers` is deliberately UNCHANGED (still exactly 7 columns).** It is the *writable* dimension accessor: putting `parent_code` on it would open a second write path into the field that decides which cheques were legal, one that skips every readable refusal. It also means a re-run of `scripts/cenapro/import-rc-deliveries.mjs` — which upserts through that view — **cannot reach, and therefore cannot wipe, a hand-set parent**.

**Deliberately not built:** no delete RPC (a retired trader is `active = false`, never a DELETE — historic payments must keep naming it, and a DELETE would `SET NULL` the `supplier_code` of every receipt it ever sold); `code` is **not** in the patch allowlist (a re-key cascades through `rc_delivery` and splits this trail — a data-migration act, not a cell edit); and nothing from Step 3+ (no banks, no `rc_payment`, no allocations, no balance view).

**Verified live** (rolled-back transactions as `authenticated`, 35/35): insert/update/clear-parent/rename/deactivate/reactivate; grandchild refused both ways; self-parent refused by the CHECK; unknown patch key → `unsupported_field`; stale version → `version_conflict` + the current version; a blind write on an existing code → `invalid` ("already exists"); non-existent parent and blank display name refused with readable messages; FK CASCADE and SET NULL both behave as declared; `authenticated` can read the trail but cannot forge, erase or rewrite it (`42501`) and cannot write the group view; **`anon` holds ZERO grants** on any new relation, no EXECUTE on the RPC and no USAGE on the audit sequence. Advisors: **0 ERROR**, the only findings on the new objects being the project-wide `pg_graphql_authenticated_table_exposed` WARN. After the run: 12 suppliers, **0 with a parent**, **0 audit rows**, `rc_delivery` untouched at 971 receipts / ₱729,637,074.1125 and `view_rc_delivery` still 60 columns.

### Liquidation — banks, payments and the running balance (2026-08-05, migration `20260805120000`)

**Liquidation Step 3 — the first thing in the system that can answer "what do we owe BRIX right now".** `rc_delivery` already knows what CI owes for every truck; this is the money going back out. It ships a **real balance BEFORE allocation exists** — assigning a cheque to particular receipts is Step 4, and allocation *refines* this number rather than enabling it. Strictly additive: no column on `rc_delivery`, nothing written in it, `view_rc_delivery` untouched (still 60 columns).

| Object | What it is |
|---|---|
| `cenapro.rc_bank` → `public.cenapro_rc_banks` | CI's OWN banks — **data, never an enum**, because the list grows and shrinks. Seeded BDO / CHINABANK / METROBANK / AUB (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`). Retire with `active = false`. |
| `cenapro.rc_bank_account` → `public.cenapro_rc_bank_accounts` | The cheque book's home. `bank_code` FK, `account_label` ("current – Cebu"), `account_no` (secondary detail, never front-of-screen), `active`, `sort_order`. **Structurally necessary**, not cosmetic: a cheque number is unique only per ACCOUNT, and Step 7's skipped-number detection is per cheque book. `UNIQUE (bank_code, account_label)` + a partial `UNIQUE (bank_code, account_no)`. |
| `cenapro.rc_payment` | One money movement. `supplier_code` **NOT NULL**, `payment_date`, `method` ∈ {`cheque`, `bank_transfer`, `adjustment`} (**no `cash`**), `amount_php` **always > 0**, `direction` ∈ {`outgoing`, `incoming`}, `stated_term`, `bank_account_id`, `cheque_no`, `cheque_date`, `reference_no`, `remarks`, **`deleted_at`/`deleted_by`**, `row_version` + actor columns. **No `status` column** — the cheque lifecycle was declined outright. |
| `cenapro.rc_payment_audit` → `public.cenapro_rc_payment_audit` | Append-only trail, SECURITY DEFINER trigger, RLS + SELECT-only — built in the SAME migration that creates the table. `amount_php` and `cheque_no` are promoted out of `snapshot` into indexed columns ("what was this cheque worth when it was voided" is the query). |
| `cenapro.rc_delivery_is_priceable(gross, base_price)` | **The ONE definition of a priceable receipt.** Step 4's settlement view reads it; nothing re-types the predicate. |
| `cenapro.view_rc_bank_account` → `public.cenapro_rc_bank_accounts` | Accounts + bank display name + a `display_label` for the picker. |
| `cenapro.view_rc_payment` → `public.cenapro_rc_payments` | Payment list, **and the ONE definition of `balance_effect_php`** (+amount outgoing, −amount incoming) and `is_cash` (false only for an adjustment). Soft-deleted rows ARE included — filter `NOT is_deleted` for anything numeric. No allocation columns (Step 4). |
| `cenapro.view_rc_supplier_balance` → `public.cenapro_rc_supplier_balances` | **THE object.** One row per supplier + the synthetic unassigned row. **53 columns since 2026-08-06** — see "Opening balances" below. |
| `cenapro.view_rc_supplier_group_balance` → `public.cenapro_rc_supplier_group_balances` | The same measures rolled up by `group_code`, built ON TOP of the per-supplier view so the two can never disagree. **49 columns since 2026-08-06.** |
| `public.cenapro_save_rc_bank` / `_rc_bank_account` / `_rc_payment`, `cenapro_delete_rc_payment`, `cenapro_restore_rc_payment` | The write path. Allowlisted patch (unknown key refuses the whole call), compare-and-set on `row_version` in the same statement as the write. Outcomes `inserted \| updated \| deleted \| restored \| version_conflict \| not_found \| unsupported_field \| invalid`, every refusal carrying a toast-ready message. |

**⚠️ THE PRICEABILITY PREDICATE, and why it is subtler than it looks.** `receipts_php` sums `total_price_php` over **priceable receipts only** (`gross_weight_kg IS NOT NULL AND base_price_php_kg IS NOT NULL`). `total_price_php` is a generated column that `COALESCE`s both factors to 0, so **`SUM(total_price_php)` and `SUM(...) FILTER (priceable)` are numerically IDENTICAL on every supplier, forever** — measured live: peso gap **0** on ALI UNGA, PALAWAN, SEVILLA and across all 971 rows. The naive balance therefore does **not** produce a wrong peso number; it produces the *right* peso number with a **silent hole** in it and marks every unpriced receipt settled the instant it exists. **No amount anywhere reveals the gap** — only `unpriced_receipt_count` does. SEVILLA is the live proof: 2 receipts, `receipts_php` ₱0, `running_balance_php` ₱0 — it reads perfectly square while carrying two receipts nobody can price. Renzo (decision 10): these are **incomplete entries, not ₱0 payable**, and *"priced but not yet weighed"* is a **normal daily stage** in a receipt's life (the in-app INSERT path creates exactly that), so the count will never sit at zero for long. **Never drop the filter as redundant** — it is the single definition that also produces the honesty columns, and it is what keeps `receipts_php` correct if `total_price_php` ever becomes NULL instead of 0.

**THE SIGN — `running_balance_php = opening_balance_php + payments_php − receipts_php`, so NEGATIVE = WE OWE THE SUPPLIER, positive = the supplier owes us.** (The `opening_balance_php` term arrived 2026-08-06 — see "Opening balances" below; it is 0 for every supplier that has not stated one, so the formula was `payments − receipts` as originally shipped.) Renzo's convention verbatim (decision 5), the **opposite** of the accounts-payable one; stated in the column COMMENT so nobody re-derives it backwards. **A non-zero balance is never an error state** (decision 8): no badge, no red, no auto-close, no nightly job, and **no `rounds_to_php` / `within_rounding`** — the rounding habit is real but not stable enough to encode, and a rule that is right most of the time would license the UI to call a genuine shortfall "expected".

**Honesty columns on the balance:** `unpriced_receipt_count`, `unpriced_receipt_kg` (payable *net* kilos we do know; a receipt with no weight contributes 0 because there is nothing to report), and an exhaustive partition into `unpriced_awaiting_weight_count` / `unpriced_awaiting_price_count` / `unpriced_awaiting_both_count` — genuinely different operational stages, so the screen names the state instead of guessing.

**Money decomposition, exposed so the UI never works it out.** `payments_php` is the SIGNED net entering the balance: outgoing +, incoming −, and an `adjustment` (a write-off, no cash moved) participates exactly like a payment because forgiving a remainder does settle it. Invariant, verified live: **`payments_php = cash_out_php − cash_in_php + adjustment_php` = `cash_net_php + adjustment_php`**.

**The receipt that cannot be liquidated.** One receipt (2026-02-23, ₱864,743.75) has no `supplier_code`, so no cheque can ever point at it (`rc_payment.supplier_code` is NOT NULL). Chosen surface: **a synthetic row, not a documented exclusion** — an exclusion is something a UI can forget to render, a row is not. `view_rc_supplier_balance` emits one extra row with `supplier_code IS NULL`, `group_code IS NULL`, **`is_unassigned = true`**, `display_name = '(no payee recorded)'`, `running_balance_php = −864,743.75`, and it is emitted **only while such receipts exist** — assign the payee and it disappears by itself. Key rows on `is_unassigned`, not on `supplier_code`. It appears in the group view too.

**Soft delete, and the restore that makes it real.** Payments are money records, not transcribed reference data (§5c), so a delete stamps `deleted_at`/`deleted_by` and every balance filters it out. The freed cheque number becomes re-usable because `uq_cenapro_rc_payment_cheque` is partial on `method = 'cheque' AND deleted_at IS NULL`. **`cenapro_restore_rc_payment` exists because a soft delete you cannot undo is not reversibility** — it is its own function rather than a flag on the delete, and it refuses when the number was re-used in the meantime. The trail and `deleted_at` answer *different* questions and both are needed: a soft delete lands in the trail as an UPDATE carrying `deleted_at` in `changed` (find voids with `changed ? 'deleted_at'`).

**Audit scope — `rc_payment` ONLY, not `rc_bank` / `rc_bank_account`. The test applied:** *does changing this row alter the meaning of an already-recorded money fact?* `rc_supplier.parent_code` — **yes** (it decides which allocations were legal), so Step 2 trails it. `rc_bank_account` — **no**: a payment reaches its account by immutable `id`, so renaming it, editing its number or re-pointing its `bank_code` changes a *label*, never which cheques belong to which book, and Step 7 groups by `bank_account_id` regardless. Both dimensions still get `row_version` + a touch trigger + a compare-and-set RPC, so the *safety* half is present; only the *history* half is deferred, and adding it later starts from a truthful date instead of a forbidden backfill. **Residual gap, stated:** a hard DELETE of an account `SET NULL`s `bank_account_id` on its non-cheque payments with no record of what it was — mitigated structurally (no delete RPC for accounts; retire with `active = false`; and for a cheque the shape CHECK turns that SET NULL into an outright refusal, so a cheque's account is undeletable).

**Every new public accessor is READ-ONLY** — unlike `public.cenapro_rc_suppliers`, none of these is a write door, so the RPCs are the only way in.

**Verified live** (rolled-back transactions as `authenticated`, 42/42 + a 13-row balance read): the file on disk is byte-identical to the applied migration (`md5 0585445e…`). Cheque `#001234` on two DIFFERENT accounts → both allowed; on the SAME account → refused; soft-delete then re-use the number → allowed; restore afterwards → refused with the reason. Cheque with no number / no account, `bank_transfer` carrying a cheque number, `method='cash'`, a negative amount, an unknown supplier — all refused with readable messages. A patch containing `deleted_at` + `row_version` → `unsupported_field` naming both; a NULL expected version on an update → blind-write refusal; a stale version → `version_conflict` + the current version. **Balance arithmetic hand-checked**: BRIX with a ₱1,000,000 cheque out, a ₱200,000 refund in and a ₱132.875 write-off gave `cash_out` 1,000,000 / `cash_in` 200,000 / `cash_net` 800,000 / `adjustment` 132.875 / `payments` 800,132.875 / `running` **−211,869,329.625** — exact, invariant TRUE. **Group rollup** with a temporary PULVERA→BRIX pair: group receipts ₱222,633,381.7, group running **−217,633,381.7**, and `group total == sum of member rows` TRUE; the pairing was then rolled back. A no-op UPDATE bumped `row_version` and wrote **0** audit rows; a real amount edit wrote `{"amount_php":{"old":1000000,"new":1027000}}`. `authenticated` cannot forge or erase the trail (`42501`); **`anon` holds ZERO grants** on any new relation, no EXECUTE on any new function and no USAGE on the audit sequence; every public accessor holds SELECT only. Advisors: **0 ERROR** (the 14 new findings are all the project-wide `pg_graphql_authenticated_table_exposed` WARN). After the run: **0 payments, 0 audit rows, 0 bank accounts, 4 seeded banks, 0 suppliers with a parent**, `rc_delivery` untouched at 971 / ₱729,637,074.1125.

**Deliberately NOT built (Step 4+):** `rc_payment_allocation`, `view_rc_delivery_settlement`, `view_rc_payment_state`, and therefore **no `advance_php`** — without allocations every peso of every payment is unallocated, so the column would read 100% on every row and teach the UI something false. Also absent: any period boundary (closing a balance is a human-initiated marker — Step 6, `rc_balance_period`), and any cheque-gap report (Step 7). **No accounts and no payments were seeded** — an account number is a real fact about a real cheque book, and inventing one is exactly the fabrication the audit discipline exists to prevent.

### Opening balances — how the balance becomes TRUE (2026-08-06, Step 3b, migration `20260805130000`)

Renzo, the hour after Step 3 shipped: *"Since it's a bit impossible to check all of the past history, we should be able to modify the starting balances of the suppliers we have listed. I think that's imperative."* Step 3's balance sums **every receipt since January**, so it said CI owes BRIX **₱212,669,462.50** — the entire year's purchases — because no historic cheque was ever entered and back-entering seven months of them is not realistic. An opening balance is how that number becomes true: Renzo **states** what is actually outstanding as of a date, and the system counts forward.

| Object | What it is |
|---|---|
| `cenapro.rc_supplier_opening_balance` | **APPEND-ONLY** stated openings. `id` (bigint identity), `supplier_code` FK→`rc_supplier` (`ON UPDATE CASCADE`, no `ON DELETE` so a supplier with one cannot be deleted), `as_of_date`, `opening_balance_php` (**signed like `running_balance_php`**), `note`, `created_at`, `created_by` (**no FK to profiles** — the row outlives the account). One index `(supplier_code, id DESC)` which also carries the RI scan. |
| `cenapro.fn_stamp_rc_supplier_opening_balance` | BEFORE **INSERT** trigger: `trim_scale`s the amount (so 4200000.00 and 4200000 cannot read as two figures), blanks an empty note, and **forces** `created_at = now()` + `created_by = auth.uid()` so a revision cannot be back-dated or mis-attributed. There is no UPDATE branch because there is no UPDATE path. |
| `cenapro.view_rc_supplier_opening_balance` | **THE ONE definition of "current"** — the LATEST revision (greatest `id`), + `revision_count`. Every consumer reads it here. |
| `public.cenapro_rc_supplier_opening_balances` | Read-only: the current revision per supplier **that has one** (a supplier that never stated one is ABSENT — read `has_opening_balance` on the balance view instead of assuming a row). |
| `public.cenapro_rc_supplier_opening_balance_history` | Read-only: **every revision ever stated**, with `is_current`. |
| `public.cenapro_set_rc_supplier_opening_balance(code, as_of, php, note)` | The **INSERT-only** appender. **No update rpc and no delete rpc** — a correction is a new revision. |

**"MODIFY" MEANS APPEND A REVISION.** Cloned from `cenapro.warehouse_opening_balance` (the flec-count precedent, `20260601113342`): every set is a NEW ROW, nothing is ever UPDATEd or DELETEd, because a money history that can be silently rewritten is worth nothing and Renzo *will* revise these as suppliers confirm them. Enforced by **two independent locks**: no UPDATE/DELETE grant to any client role, **and** RLS with SELECT + INSERT policies and **no UPDATE/DELETE policy at all** — so even a future blanket `GRANT … ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA cenapro` cannot rewrite a revision. Verified live: `authenticated` gets **42501 permission denied** on both UPDATE and DELETE. *(The brief asked for a SELECT-only policy — the audit tables' posture — but those are written only by SECURITY DEFINER triggers, whereas this table is written by a SECURITY INVOKER rpc running as `authenticated`, so a SELECT-only policy would make every append fail. The property the brief was protecting is kept; only its letter changed.)*

**ONE DELIBERATE DIVERGENCE FROM THE FLEC PRECEDENT.** `warehouse_opening_balance` resolves "current" as the latest *effective* row (greatest `period_start_date <= ` the date you ask about) because its consumer is a ledger scoped to an arbitrary start date. Here **current = the LATEST REVISION, greatest `id`** — monotone and tie-free. That is what makes revising *backward* work: a correction whose `as_of_date` is EARLIER than the revision it replaces must still win, because it is the newer statement of fact. Proven live: revision 1 as of 2026-08-03 then revision 2 as of **2026-07-01** → the current accessor returns the 2026-07-01 figure, and both rows stay in the history.

**⚠️ THE AS-OF RULE — stated once, and nothing contradicts it.** **The opening balance stands for everything STRICTLY BEFORE `as_of_date`; receipts and payments dated ON OR AFTER it count fresh on top of it.** That is the natural reading of "as of 1 August the balance was X" — the 1 August truck has not been paid for by a figure quoted that morning. The boundary is `>=` (fresh) / `<` (carried), **never `>`**, and it is repeated verbatim in the table COMMENT, the column COMMENTs and both balance views' COMMENTs. Proven live with `as_of = 2026-07-01`, a date **one BRIX receipt carries**: fresh = 35 = `count(delivery_date >= as_of)`, carried = 246 = `count(< as_of)`; under `>` the fresh count would have been 34. **A receipt with no `delivery_date` counts FRESH, never carried** — written naively both `>=` and `<` are NULL for such a row and it would fall out of *both* sides and vanish from the balance, so `is_carried` is a single strict boolean with the NULL guard inside it (live count of dateless receipts is 0 today; `rc_payment.payment_date` is NOT NULL so its half has no such case).

**THE REGRESSION GUARANTEE: a supplier with no opening balance reads EXACTLY as it did before.** All 12 suppliers plus the synthetic no-payee row are in that state, and the whole 13-row read is **byte-identical** — md5 over the previous 30-column projection is `0e63751b7573b610146393928096f82f` before *and* after, and the group view's 27-column projection `698408774f75cc97303a4320cfac45dc`. Mechanically: `as_of_date IS NULL` collapses every windowed aggregate to its unwindowed form and every carried aggregate to zero. `cenapro.rc_delivery_is_priceable()` is **not** inlined or weakened — it is still the ONE definition, now evaluated once per row instead of five times per aggregate.

**Nothing that existed lost its name or its value.** `receipts_php` / `payments_php` / the whole `cash_*` decomposition are now **windowed** to `>= as_of_date`, and the full-history figures are preserved beside them as **`receipts_all_php` / `payments_all_php` / `running_balance_all_php`** + `receipt_count_all` / `payment_count_all` / `cash_out_all_php` / `cash_in_all_php` / `cash_net_all_php` / `adjustment_all_php` / `adjustment_count_all`. Without them a stated opening balance would be **unauditable** — you could never ask "what does the raw history say, and what did my stated figure change?"

**`carried_*` is what makes a stated figure defensible later.** `carried_receipt_count` / `carried_receipt_php` / `carried_payment_count` / `carried_payment_php` say exactly what the opening stands in for, so the screen can say *"this ₱4.2M stands in for 275 receipts worth ₱207.9M and one payment of ₱190M."* The COUNT is every receipt before the cutoff; the PESOS are the priceable ones only, exactly as `receipt_count` / `receipts_php` relate. Four **closed** invariants, all verified live: `receipt_count_all = receipt_count + carried_receipt_count`, `receipts_all_php = receipts_php + carried_receipt_php`, and the same pair for payments. Plus the cross-check that ties the two worlds together in one line — also verified:

> `running_balance_php − running_balance_all_php` = `opening_balance_php + carried_receipt_php − carried_payment_php`

**⚠️ THE UNPRICED COUNTS STAY ALL-TIME under their existing names** (`unpriced_receipt_count`, `unpriced_receipt_kg` and the exhaustive `awaiting_weight`/`awaiting_price`/`awaiting_both` split); the new `unpriced_receipt_count_window` / `unpriced_receipt_kg_window` are the windowed twins. A deliberate asymmetry with the money columns, and the whole point: **an unpriced receipt from before the cutoff CANNOT have been folded into the opening balance, because nobody knows what it is worth.** Had the primary count been windowed, stating an opening balance would have made SEVILLA's two unpriceable receipts *vanish from the screen* while they were still unpriceable and still covered by nothing — the exact silent hole the priceability predicate exists to refuse. Proven live: SEVILLA with `as_of = 2026-07-15` (after both its receipts) reads `unpriced_receipt_count` **2**, `unpriced_receipt_count_window` **0**, `awaiting_both_count` **2**, `carried_receipt_count` 2 and `carried_receipt_php` **₱0** — carried, counted, and worth an unknown amount. `first_receipt_date` / `last_receipt_date` / `first_payment_date` / `last_payment_date` likewise stay **all-time**: "first receipt" means the first one, not the first one after a figure somebody quoted.

**The group rollup sums the opening term too**, and the documented invariant survives because every measure including the opening is linear: **the group total equals the sum of its members' `running_balance_php` exactly** — verified across all 12 groups, and on a temporary PULVERA→BRIX pair (openings −₱1,500,000.55 and −₱333,333.33 → group opening −₱1,833,333.88, group running **−₱31,448,599.43** = the exact sum of the two member rows). Because members may legitimately be stated as of **different** dates, the group exposes `opening_as_of_date` (the one date when they all agree, otherwise **NULL**, so a UI can never print a date true for only some members), plus `opening_as_of_date_min` / `_max` and `opening_supplier_count`.

**The writer refuses, each with a toast-ready message** (all 10 verified live): an unknown supplier, a blank/NULL supplier, a NULL `as_of_date`, a NULL amount, `NaN`, `±Infinity`, more than 2 decimal places, and a future `as_of_date`. The future check is measured in **`Asia/Manila`, not UTC** — PH is UTC+8, so between midnight and 8am in Cebu `current_date` is still yesterday and would refuse a figure stated for the day it actually is. The decimal message is worded carefully: individual receipts **do** legitimately price to fractions of a centavo and nothing is wrong with them; this is the one figure a person states by hand. **It deliberately does NOT refuse** (all verified): a **zero** amount ("we are square with this trader as of that date" is a real answer, not a blank), a **positive** amount (the supplier owes us — an advance), a **re-statement of exactly the same numbers**, `as_of_date` = today, or a **backdated** revision.

**Read `has_opening_balance`, never the amount**, to tell a stated ₱0 from "nothing has ever been stated" — both read `opening_balance_php = 0`. The synthetic `is_unassigned` row can never carry one (`rc_supplier_opening_balance.supplier_code` is NOT NULL and FK'd), so it reads `has_opening_balance = false`, `opening = 0`, `carried_* = 0` and `running_balance_php = running_balance_all_php`.

**An opening balance is NOT a period boundary.** It is a stated fact about the past with an as-of date; it closes nothing, it starts no period, and the full history is still readable beside it. Step 6's `cenapro.rc_balance_period` remains unbuilt and is a different thing. **Nothing is seeded** — every figure is a real fact Renzo has to get from a supplier statement or a confirmation call, and inventing one (even a zero) is exactly the fabrication the audit discipline exists to prevent. All 12 suppliers read `has_opening_balance = false` today.

**Verified live** — the file on disk is byte-identical to the applied SQL (`applied statement + trailing newline` → md5 `735dd509def3c8c2fdc7a8d925b74ced`; the rpc body in `pg_proc.prosrc` → md5 `18c62ebd54a710730712e341ad060afa`). BRIX with `as_of 2026-08-03`, an opening of **−₱4,200,000**, a ₱190M transfer *before* the cutoff, a ₱3M transfer *on* it, a ₱250,000 refund *after* it and a ₱1,691.25 write-off gave `receipts_php` **4,751,691.25** (6 receipts) / `carried_receipt_php` **207,917,771.25** (275) / `payments_php` **2,751,691.25** / `cash_out` 3,000,000 / `cash_in` 250,000 / `cash_net` 2,750,000 / `adjustment` 1,691.25 / `carried_payment_php` **190,000,000** / `running_balance_php` **−6,200,000** / `running_balance_all_php` **−19,917,771.25** — every figure exact, all four closure invariants and the cross-check TRUE, and the other 11 rows md5-identical (`2d510abc2068a7082dc38dafc857077d`). Direct INSERT as `authenticated` succeeds **despite the identity sequence being revoked** (an IDENTITY column does not consult the sequence ACL, unlike a `serial` DEFAULT `nextval`). `anon` holds **zero** grant rows on any new relation, no EXECUTE on either new function and no USAGE on the sequence; the appender is SECURITY INVOKER with `search_path=""`; all four new/changed views are `security_invoker=true`. Advisors: **0 ERROR** — the 4 new findings are all the project-wide `pg_graphql_authenticated_table_exposed` WARN. Every test ran in a rolled-back transaction: after the run **0 opening-balance rows, 0 payments, 0 suppliers with a parent**, `rc_delivery` untouched at 971 / ₱729,637,074.1125, `view_rc_delivery` still 60 columns, 13/13 balance rows, BRIX back to −₱212,669,462.50. *(The identity sequence sits at `last_value = 14` — sequences are non-transactional, so the rolled-back tests consumed ids 1–14 and Renzo's first real revision will be id 15. Irrelevant to correctness: "current" is the greatest id, and gaps do not matter.)*

### Allocation — assigning a payment to particular deliveries (2026-08-06, Step 4, migration `20260806060000`)

**Liquidation Step 4 — the join between the money going out and the trucks that came in**, and the phase that earns the feature (the median cheque covers four to eight receipts). It answers "which receipts did that cheque pay for" and its mirror, "how much of this cheque is still unassigned". Prompted by Renzo, 2026-08-06: *"An add cheque button in deliveries page would be nice. Being able to right click on a delivery and then assign a cheque to it… That would make the liquidations page more of a summary page."* **DATA LAYER ONLY — no UI.** Strictly additive: no column on `rc_delivery`, nothing written in it, `view_rc_delivery` untouched (still 60 columns), and the Step-3/3b balance figures are **provably unchanged** (see "Verified live" below).

**`cenapro.rc_payment_allocation` — many-to-many, with the amount ON THE EDGE.** A payment is not 1:1 with a receipt in both directions at once: one cheque routinely settles several truckloads, and one truckload is routinely settled by a downpayment now and the balance later. `rc_delivery.paid_by_payment_id` fails the first case, `rc_payment.delivery_id` fails the second — so it is a join table, and the amount on the edge is not decoration, it **is** the entire content of a partial payment. `payment_id` (FK **ON DELETE CASCADE** — an allocation has no existence apart from its payment) · `delivery_id` (FK **ON DELETE RESTRICT**, load-bearing — see the delete rule below) · `amount_php` (CHECK > 0) · `payee_group_code` (NOT NULL) · `note` · `deleted_at`/`deleted_by` (SOFT) · `row_version` + actors. **Partial UNIQUE `(payment_id, delivery_id) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`** — one LIVE edge per pair, because two partial payments from the same cheque to the same receipt is one larger edge, not two rows; a released edge does not block re-creating the pair (verified: 2 rows for the pair, 1 live).

**`payee_group_code` is the row's own evidence of its legality**, and it is **derived, forced and frozen** by `cenapro.fn_touch_rc_payment_allocation`. `group_code` answers "may this payee be paid for this receipt **right now**"; the question a dispute asks six months later is "**was** that allocation legal when it was made" — and re-pointing a parent silently changes today's answer. So the resolved group is stamped on the row from `cenapro.view_rc_supplier_group` (never accepted from a caller — that would let a caller forge it) and re-frozen from OLD on every UPDATE (so an amount edit cannot re-date the judgement). It carries an FK ON UPDATE CASCADE, so it follows a supplier **re-key** (same trader, new code) but never a **re-parent**. The same trigger **refuses** any attempt to move an edge to a different payment or receipt (SQLSTATE 23514, readable message) rather than silently reverting it.

**The two invariants — one enforced, one deliberately not.** (1) **ENFORCED: a payment's live allocations may never exceed its `amount_php`** — belt and braces, a friendly RPC refusal naming the overshoot AND the constraint trigger `cenapro.fn_check_rc_payment_allocation_total`. It is registered on **both sides of the arithmetic**: on `rc_payment_allocation` (assign too much) and on `rc_payment` (edit the cheque **down** below what is already assigned — a hole the brief did not mention and the easier one to fall into). It raises **SQLSTATE 23514 with a human-readable MESSAGE**, so Step 3's existing `WHEN check_violation` handler in `cenapro_save_rc_payment` reports it verbatim as a toast-ready `invalid` **with that function unmodified**. DEFERRABLE INITIALLY IMMEDIATE, so the exception still fires inside the calling statement. (2) **NOT enforced: a receipt's allocations MAY exceed its `total_price_php`** — decision 13, *"record it. It will be reflected in the running balance anyway."* No refusal, no CHECK, no threshold; it reads `over_allocated`.

**Sub-supplier legality is checked in the RPC, at write time only.** Legal when the payment's payee IS the receipt's trader **or** they share a `group_code` (read from `cenapro.view_rc_supplier_group`, never re-derived). The refusal **names both traders**. A receipt with a **NULL `supplier_code` can never be allocated** and is refused by name. Legality is deliberately **not** in a constraint trigger: arithmetic invariants are timeless, but "this payee may be paid for this receipt" is a judgement about a moment — a parent detached next March would otherwise make the trigger refuse an unrelated amount edit on an allocation that *was* legal when made.

**Three new read models, all `security_invoker`.**
- **`cenapro.view_rc_delivery_settlement`** (19 cols) → `public.cenapro_rc_delivery_settlement`. One row per receipt: `allocated_php`, `balance_php`, `allocation_count`, `payment_ids`, `is_priceable`, `is_allocatable`, and **`settlement_status` in {`unpriced`, `unpaid`, `partial`, `settled`, `over_allocated`}**. ⚠️ **`unpriced` is tested FIRST and never collapses into `settled`** — `total_price_php` COALESCEs a missing weight or price to exactly ₱0, so an unpriced receipt with no allocations satisfies "allocated ≥ total" under any naive ordering, and the gap is invisible in pesos. **`balance_php` is NULL — not 0 — on an unpriceable receipt**, because the honest answer to "how much is still owed" is *nobody knows yet*; a SUM gives the same peso figure either way, which is exactly why the difference has to be visible in the column. Counts LIVE allocations of LIVE payments only: a voided cheque settles nothing.
- **`cenapro.view_rc_payment_state`** (34 cols) → `public.cenapro_rc_payment_state`. `view_rc_payment` + `allocated_php` + **`unallocated_php` (THE definition)** + `allocation_count` + `is_advance`. §4.4: a cash advance needs no column, no flag and no table — it simply **is** a payment whose allocations sum to less than its amount, so **`WHERE is_advance` IS the cash-advance list** and Step 5 needs no new object.
- **`cenapro.view_rc_payment_allocation`** → `public.cenapro_rc_payment_allocations`. The edge list with both ends folded in (cheque no / payee / date; the receipt's date, truck and payable total) plus `is_subgroup_allocation` for §7a's trader label.

**The balance views gained 4 columns each** (53 → **57**, 49 → **53**): `advance_php`, `advance_payment_count`, `unassigned_incoming_php`, `advance_php_window`. `advance_php` was deliberately omitted in Step 3 (with no allocations it would have read 100% of every payment); it is now summed from `view_rc_payment_state.unallocated_php` over **live outgoing** payments — the `p` CTE aggregates that view instead of `view_rc_payment`, so `unallocated_php` has one definition. **It is NOT a balance term** — every peso in it is already inside `payments_php`, so adding it to anything double-counts — and **`running_balance_php` is unchanged** (`opening + payments − receipts`); allocation *refines* what a payment was for, it does not change what is owed. It is **ALL-TIME, not windowed**, the same deliberate asymmetry as `unpriced_receipt_count`: an unassigned remainder on a pre-cutoff payment is still money nobody has pointed at a receipt (`advance_php_window` is the windowed twin; the all-time one is primary). `unassigned_incoming_php` keeps the 0.01% mirror separate rather than netted, so a careless reader cannot cancel two opposite facts.

**The write path — one code path, both doors.**
- **`public.cenapro_save_rc_payment_allocations(p_payment_id, p_expected_row_version, p_allocations)`** — replaces one payment's **whole live allocation block** atomically ("apply this cheque across four receipts", no half-applied cheque). `cenapro_save_rc_delivery_samples` note for note: the **parent UPDATE comes first**, so the payment is row-locked and its compare-and-set has fired before any child row moves. Edges absent from the payload are **soft-deleted**, never hard-deleted. **The write is two statements and the order is load-bearing:** the soft-delete can only lower the total, and the upsert is a SINGLE statement whose AFTER-ROW constraint trigger therefore fires once against the FINAL state — spread over several statements, a legal rearrangement (move ₱200k from receipt A to B) would trip the invariant halfway through depending on write order. `ON CONFLICT (payment_id, delivery_id) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL DO UPDATE … WHERE` skips lines that did not move, so a re-save churns no `row_version` and writes no audit row.
- **`public.cenapro_allocate_delivery_to_payment(p_payment_id, p_expected_row_version, p_delivery_id, p_amount_php, p_note)`** — the **delivery-first door**. Built ON TOP of the block RPC (merges its one edge into the live block and delegates), so there is one write path, one set of invariants and **zero duplicated validation**. It **SETS, it does not add** (the block RPC is a replace; an "add" would make the same call twice mean two different things) and returns `previous_amount_php`. `p_amount_php` NULL means `LEAST(what the receipt still owes, what the payment still has unassigned)`, computed in SQL — refused when the receipt has no price yet or nothing is left.
- **`public.cenapro_restore_rc_payment_allocation(p_id, p_expected_row_version)`** — §5c's reversibility. Refuses when the pair has since been re-created, when the payment is deleted, and when restoring would overshoot the payment.
- **An allocation amount is deliberately NOT limited to 2 decimal places** (unlike a stated opening balance): 19 receipts price to sub-centavo fractions, so "settle in full" must be able to assign ₱1,027,132.875 exactly.
- **An allocation to an UNPRICED receipt is deliberately ALLOWED.** `is_allocatable` greys it out on the delivery-first door (§7a) but the RPC records it, because "priced but not yet weighed" is a normal daily stage and a downpayment on a truck weighed tomorrow is an ordinary act.

**Deleting a receipt that has money against it: WARN, then RELEASE.** `public.cenapro_delete_rc_delivery` gained a third argument `p_release_allocations boolean DEFAULT false` (**DROP + CREATE, never CREATE OR REPLACE** — a new signature would leave the 2-arg version as an overload PostgREST cannot choose between; there is now exactly one, `pronargs = 3`). By default it **REFUSES** with the distinct outcome **`has_allocations`**, the real `allocated_php`, the real `allocation_count` and a `payments[]` array, so the UI can warn with numbers instead of a generic scare. With the flag it removes those edges in the **same transaction** and then deletes the receipt; the money reappears on every cheque immediately because `unallocated_php` is derived. ⚠️ **The edges are HARD-removed, not soft-deleted, and the brief that asked for soft-delete-then-delete could not have worked:** `delivery_id` is ON DELETE RESTRICT and the referential check does not know what `deleted_at` means, so a soft-deleted edge still refuses the DELETE. §5c's own rule covers it — *"every mutation carries a full snapshot, so anything can be reconstructed even when it was hard-removed upstream"* — and each removal writes a DELETE audit row with the whole edge in `snapshot` and `source = 'cenapro_delete_rc_delivery:release'`. So the split is: **a human removing an edge = SOFT (restorable); the receipt itself being destroyed = HARD, trailed, with the reason.** RESTRICT still does its job — the ordinary ledger delete is refused and raw DML cannot cascade money away. Already-released edges and edges on a voided payment hold no live money, do not gate anything, and are removed in every path and reported as `inert_allocations_removed`. The row is locked `FOR UPDATE` before anything is removed and the mutation sits in a nested block that rolls back on a version race, so a race can never destroy the money and leave the receipt standing. **With no allocations at all the behaviour and the return payload are unchanged** — the existing 2-arg caller in `deliveries/actions.ts::deleteDelivery` keeps working byte-for-byte.

**The audit trail.** `cenapro.rc_payment_audit` gained `entity` (`payment` | `allocation`, DEFAULT `'payment'` so `fn_audit_rc_payment` needed no change), `allocation_id` and `delivery_id`, plus an entity-shape CHECK and two partial indexes — **ONE table, always keyed by `payment_id`, so one cheque's whole history including every re-assignment is a single indexed query.** Same idiom as `rc_delivery_audit` (that is why that shape was chosen): append-only, written only by the SECURITY DEFINER trigger `cenapro.fn_audit_rc_payment_allocation`, **AFTER not BEFORE**, `updated_at`/`row_version` excluded from the diff (so a no-op write records nothing), no write grant to any client role, RLS SELECT-only. A separate table was rejected: it would answer "what happened to this allocation" but need a UNION for "what happened to this cheque", which is the question anybody actually asks. `delivery_id` is on the row so the trail stays readable after the receipt is gone. **Read `entity` before reading `amount_php`** — on a payment row it is the cheque's amount, on an allocation row the edge's. The accessor `public.cenapro_rc_payment_audit` is **widened** (three columns APPENDED, so `entity` reads last — an ordering Postgres imposes on `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW`, not a preference).

**Verified live** — the file on disk is **byte-identical to the applied SQL** (`md5(statements[1])` = `md5(file)` = `006c99bef023e4d293b000dc4065921d`), and every test ran in a **rolled-back transaction** as `authenticated`. The Step-3/3b balance figures are **provably unchanged**: an md5 over the pinned pre-change 53-column projection is `3a8256eb852a0184beabf3d950fad8c9` and the 49-column group projection `edf5aeafc9223dbe6977a7970f01b9b8` — identical before and after, 13/13 rows. One cheque of ₱3,200,000 across four BRIX receipts in ONE call → `inserted 4`, allocated **3,183,957.50**, unallocated **16,042.50**, all four `settled` with `balance 0`; a second call dropping one edge and lowering another → `updated 1 / unchanged 2 / released 1`, allocated **2,133,623.75**, the dropped edge **soft-deleted**, statuses `partial, settled, settled, unpaid`, BRIX `advance_php` **1,066,376.25** and `running_balance_php` **−209,469,462.50** (= −212,669,462.50 + 3,200,000, hand-checked). The 2 unchanged edges wrote **zero** audit rows. Over-allocating a **receipt** was ACCEPTED and read `over_allocated  balance −174,985`; over-allocating the **payment** was refused by the RPC *and* by raw DML against the constraint trigger; **editing the cheque down** below its allocations was refused through the unmodified Step-3 RPC. A BRIX cheque was refused on a PULVERA receipt naming both traders, then **pushed through** after PULVERA was made a sub-supplier (`payee_group_code = BRIX`, `is_subgroup_allocation = true`), and the group total still equalled the sum of its members for both `running_balance_php` and `advance_php`. **SEVILLA's two receipts read `unpriced`, not `settled`**, with `balance_php` NULL (5 unpriced / 966 unpaid across all 971). Refused with readable messages: the no-payee receipt by name, a duplicated receipt, an unknown key (`unsupported_field`), a stale `row_version`, a blind write, a zero amount, a re-pointed edge. Accepted as designed: a ₱300,000 downpayment on an unpriced receipt (still reads `unpriced`, `balance NULL`), and a ₱1,027.875 sub-centavo amount. Release-on-delete: ₱800,000 over 2 edges → `has_allocations` naming both payments, then with the flag `deleted` and the two payments' `unallocated_php` went **774,985 → 1,274,985** and **600,000 → 900,000**, with `DELETE allocation src=cenapro_delete_rc_delivery:release` in the trail; a receipt with **no** allocations deleted exactly as before. `authenticated` got **42501** on forging and on erasing an audit row; `anon` holds **zero** grants on every new relation and **no EXECUTE** on any new function. Advisors: **0 ERROR** (144 lints; the 7 findings on the new objects are all the project-wide `pg_graphql_authenticated_table_exposed` WARN). A whole-table settlement scan is **1,023 shared buffer hits / 8.4 ms** over 971 receipts. After the run: **0 allocations, 0 payments, 0 audit rows, 0 bank accounts, 0 suppliers with a parent**, `rc_delivery` untouched at 971 / ₱729,637,074.1125, `view_rc_delivery` still 60 columns. **Nothing is seeded** — there is no real cheque yet, so there is no real assignment to record.

### RLS + grants
RLS enabled on all eight `rc_*` fact/dimension tables (`rc_supplier`, `rc_destination`, `rc_delivery`, `rc_delivery_sample`, `rc_bank`, `rc_bank_account`, `rc_payment`, `rc_payment_allocation`) with the platform single-org posture (`TO authenticated`, `USING/WITH CHECK (true)`); enforcement of roles lives in the server-action layer. **The three audit tables, `rc_delivery_audit`, `rc_supplier_audit` and `rc_payment_audit`, are deliberately different** — RLS on, but a SELECT-only policy and no write policy at all, because nothing may ever write them except their own SECURITY DEFINER triggers (see "Audit trail", "Supplier subgroups" and "Liquidation" above). **`rc_supplier_opening_balance` is a fourth posture, unique to it:** grants are exactly `SELECT, INSERT` and the policies are exactly SELECT + INSERT — **no UPDATE and no DELETE, neither as a grant nor as a policy** — because it is append-only but, unlike the audit tables, is written by a SECURITY INVOKER rpc running as `authenticated` rather than by a definer trigger (see "Opening balances" above). Note the base-table write grants on `rc_bank` / `rc_bank_account` / `rc_payment` / `rc_payment_allocation` exist only because the RPCs are SECURITY INVOKER; they are **not** a second write door, since `cenapro` is not exposed to PostgREST and every liquidation accessor in `public` is read-only. (`rc_payment_allocation` needs DELETE as well as UPDATE, because releasing an edge when its receipt is destroyed is a hard removal — see "Allocation" above.) Their identity sequences are revoked from `anon` and `authenticated` by name. **`anon` has nothing** — the `cenapro` schema carries a DEFAULT ACL of `{anon=r, authenticated=arwd, service_role=arwd}`, so every new relation there is BORN readable by `anon`; the migration revokes explicitly and grants back. Verified: `anon` appears in **zero** rows of `information_schema.role_table_grants` for any `rc_*` object, and `has_function_privilege('anon', …, 'EXECUTE')` = false on all three RPCs. Security advisors: **0 ERROR**; the only findings on the new objects are the project-wide `rls_policy_always_true` (the documented single-org posture) and `pg_graphql_authenticated_table_exposed` (the intended cost of granting SELECT to `authenticated`, same as every ICTC table).

### The import
`node scripts/cenapro/import-rc-deliveries.mjs [--dry-run] [--refresh] [--input=PATH] [--sheet=NAME]` — REST/443 with the service-role key (the sandbox cannot reach Postgres :5432). Idempotency key is `UNIQUE (source_sheet, source_row)`, a **full** (not partial) unique constraint so PostgREST's `on_conflict=` can target it; both columns are NULL on app rows and NULLs are distinct in a unique index, so hand-entered receipts are unconstrained. **Default resolution is `ignore-duplicates`** — an already-imported row is left alone, because by then a human may have resolved its flags in the app; `--refresh` switches to `merge-duplicates` as an explicit, destructive choice. Proven idempotent: on import day a second run left the counts at 991 / 244. **A re-run today would re-insert the 22 deleted duplicates** — their `(source_sheet, source_row)` keys are free again, and the importer has no memory of a human deletion. Anyone re-running it must scope the input or expect to delete them a second time.

**Verified live after import** — 991 deliveries · 244 samples · 12 suppliers · 16 destinations; `total_price_php IS DISTINCT FROM sheet_total_php` = **0 rows**; `deduction_pct NOT NULL` = 142; `price_adjustment_php_kg NOT NULL` = 12; `is_suspected_duplicate` = 22; `delivery_date IS NULL` = 2; rows carrying `import_flags` = 34 (22 suspected_duplicate · 5 destination_unmapped · 3 supplier_no_trader_prefix · 2 date_unparseable · 1 bd_out_of_range · 1 supplier_unmapped). The whole write path (insert → update → stale-version conflict → unsupported field → blind-write refusal → samples → sample version conflict → delete → cascade → delete-again) was proven as the `authenticated` role in a rolled-back transaction, 16/16.

1. **22 receipts were deleted, not 20.** 991 − 971 = 20, but 2 receipts have since been *created in the app* — so the real movement is 991 → 969 imported rows, plus 2 app rows = 971. *(An earlier version of this note read "the 20 pasted duplicates were deleted"; that was an arithmetic slip, corrected 2026-08-05 from live counts. The flags are the cross-check: 34 − 22 = 12.)* The deleted copies carried **₱17,185,938.70**. The keep-or-drop decision was Renzo's, it was made, and it was executed as a hard DELETE that left **no trace anywhere** — which is exactly why `rc_delivery_audit` now exists.
2. **The in-app INSERT path has now been used.** Older notes — including the 2026-08-04 handoff's "single most important state fact" — say *"all rows are `sheet_import`; not one row has been created or edited in the app."* **Both halves are now false:** 2 rows are `provenance = 'app'` and 13 rows sit at `row_version > 1`.
3. **`sheet_total_matches` is now FALSE on 5 imported rows, and that is CORRECT, not drift.** They are the five 2026-08-04 receipts (`source_row` 1421–1425: NEGROS, BRIX ×2, PULVERA, NOVAL) that arrived with an agreed price but **no weight** — the workbook printed `TTL PRICE` = 0 and that 0 is faithfully preserved in `sheet_total_php`. Weights were typed into the app on 2026-08-05, so the DB-computed `total_price_php` is now right and the frozen workbook witness is simply behind. **Never "repair" `sheet_total_php` to clear the flag** — it is a record of what the sheet said, not a second opinion on what is owed.

**Money that cannot be computed yet: 5 receipts.** `total_price_php = 0` on exactly **5** rows, and those are exactly the **5 unpriceable** ones (4 have no `gross_weight_kg`, 3 have no `base_price_php_kg`, 2 have neither). A ₱0 total means *"we cannot say yet"*, never *"nothing is owed"* — the generated column `COALESCE`s both factors to zero so a SUM works and the sheet's own printout is reproduced. **Any balance built on `SUM(total_price_php)` must carry an explicit priceability predicate** (`gross_weight_kg IS NOT NULL AND base_price_php_kg IS NOT NULL`), or it will show every one of these as fully settled the instant it exists. See `.agents/prompts/liquidation-feature.md` §3.4.

Of the 12 surviving flags, **10 describe a condition a human has already repaired and 2 are still live** — see "Flag resolution" above, which is why `has_import_flags` is no longer the right lens predicate.

**Verified live after the duplicate-pairing migration (2026-08-04)** — 22 groups, every one of size 2, across exactly three days: **2026-04-06** 9 groups (₱6,940,941.50) · **2026-04-07** 7 groups (₱5,315,502.20) · **2026-04-08** 6 groups (₱4,929,495.00) = **₱17,185,938.70** carried by the 22 copies. All 44 peer edges are reciprocal, none dangling, none self-referencing; `duplicate_group_key IS NULL` ⟺ `duplicate_peer_ids IS NULL` ⟺ `duplicate_group_size = 1` on every row. No other date in the 991 produces a group. `anon` still holds **zero** grants on either view; both remain `security_invoker=true` with `SELECT` to `authenticated` + `service_role` only. **No data was modified** — the migration is view-only, per decision 3. **All 22 copies were deleted the same day, so every figure in this paragraph is now historical: the derivation still runs on every read and currently groups nothing** (see the STATUS note under "Duplicate pairing" above).
