Kept in the repo as the record of what was transcribed. **Generated — never hand-edit.** |

### The one piece of arithmetic on the client, and why it is not a second implementation
CLAUDE.md forbids computing weighted averages in TypeScript, and neither page does. Totals, coverage, the four weighted averages and their per-metric denominators are all READ from `cenapro_ccc_analysis_daily` / `_monthly`.

`adjustAggregate()` does **not** re-aggregate. It takes a SQL aggregate and applies per-group DELTAS to the numerator/denominator pair SQL already published, so:

- with **no** adjustments it returns the SQL row's numbers untouched (an early return — not even a float round-trip), which is why the regression figures below render byte-for-byte from SQL;
- with adjustments it answers the one question the DB cannot answer yet: *"what would this total read if the number I am typing right now were saved?"*

Three callers, all on the ledger: an **unsaved metric edit** (swap the group's stored metrics for the typed ones), the **source filter** (`dropWeight: true` — the group's tonnage leaves the period too), and since 2026-08-03 an **unsaved WEIGHT edit** (`afterKg` — the group is taken back out at the weight SQL counted it at and put back in at the weight it would carry once saved). A save writes through the RPC, `revalidatePath` + `router.refresh()` land the DB's own answer, and the preview disappears. There is no path where a rendered number is a TypeScript re-derivation of stored state.

**`afterKg` is why a weight edit moves the averages, not just the totals.** A weight is not a spectator in this ledger — it is the thing every average is weighted BY — so restating it moves the day total, the month total, each metric's own denominator (`wtdKg`) and therefore the four weighted averages. Verified live: dropping the 2026-05-01 FLEC/WHSE 7 group's second draw from 14,835 → 4,835 kg moved `Σ DAY TOTAL` 48,767 → 38,767, its wtd BD 0.5647 → 0.5646, and the frozen footer 1,134,070 → 1,124,070 kg / 0.5602 → 0.5601. Discard restored every figure exactly.

### Saving — ONE button, two write paths, per-row verdicts
The Save button builds one payload per dirty **group** (readings) and one per dirty **draw** (weights) and calls `saveQcWeights` then `saveQcSamples`. Nothing is all-or-nothing across the two — each row carries its own concurrency check — so results are applied per row: what landed loses its pending text and a normal auto-dismissing success toast fires (`Saved 3 samples · 2 weights`); **what failed KEEPS what was typed** so nothing has to be retyped. The button reads `Save 3 samples · 2 weights`, and the inline banner merges both kinds into one list with one Copy button.

| Outcome | Surfaced as |
|---|---|
| `inserted` / `updated` | `toast.success('Saved N sample groups')` (2.5s) + `router.refresh()`. |
| `version_conflict` | Persistent inline banner + `errorToast`: *"Someone else changed this sample while you were editing. Reload to see their values."* A **Reload** button re-fetches. Never a silent retry or force-write. |
| `already_exists` | *"A sample was logged for this group while you were typing. Reload, then edit the value that is now stored."* + Reload. |
| `not_found` | *"That sample was deleted while you were editing. Reload the ledger."* + Reload. |
| `no_metrics` | **Prevented client-side.** A group whose four metrics all end up blank never leaves the browser: the save bar turns amber (*"1 group has every metric cleared — that would delete the reading"*), the offending cells get a rose ring, the inline banner names the group, and NOTHING is sent. (The server action re-checks anyway.) |
| `invalid_key` / RPC error | The raw message, in the banner + `errorToast`. |

And the four weight outcomes (`cenapro_update_event_weight`), which decorate the **draw's WT cell** rather than the group's lead row:

| Outcome | Surfaced as |
|---|---|
| `updated` | Folded into the same success toast + `router.refresh()`. |
| `conflict` | Persistent inline banner + `errorToast`, naming BOTH numbers: *"The stored weight is now 16433 kg, not the 16428 kg on your screen. Someone changed it while you were editing — reload to see the current value."* A **Reload** button re-fetches. The compare-and-set refused to write; never a retry or force-write. |
| `not_found` | *"That receipt row was deleted while you were editing. Reload the ledger."* + Reload. |
| `invalid` | **Prevented client-side** by the shared `parseWeightKg`: the save bar turns amber (*"1 weight is not a valid number of kilograms"*), the cell gets a rose ring, the banner names the draw (`MAY 01 · W7 · W7 · C1 · WT`), NOTHING is sent, and no **Reload** button is offered — reloading cannot fix something the operator typed. (The RPC re-checks anyway.) |

The inline banner is the non-toast half of the error HARD RULE: it persists until dismissed and carries a **Copy** button that grabs every failure line at once.

### Editing a weight — the asymmetry that inverts
A lab reading covers a whole sample group, so it is typed once on the group's lead row and the siblings render `〃`. **A weight is the opposite: it belongs to ONE physical draw and never carries over.** Inside the tinted editable block a sibling row's four metric cells are inert `〃` while its `WT` is a live cell like any other — that contrast IS the signal that WT is individually editable. An unsaved weight additionally takes the primary tint plus a left rail, so a pending value is never mistaken for a stored one.

- **Concurrency is COMPARE-AND-SET, not a row version.** `cenapro.production_event` has no `row_version` column and adding one would drag the production ledger's bulk upsert into respecting it too — much wider than a typo fix warrants. The client sends the weight it is looking at; the UPDATE fires only while the stored value still equals it, checked in the same statement. See "The write path" below.
- **The display must be lossless.** The WT cells use a local `formatKgExact` (`toLocaleString`, up to 3 dp), NOT the `formatKg` the sums use. `formatKg` rounds to whole kilograms — correct for a total, lethal in a cell you type over, because a stored `9,583.5` would display `9,584` and committing would write the rounded number back. Nothing on record is fractional today (0 of 1,108 rows); the write path accepts 3 dp, so the display is lossless *before* the first one arrives.
- **A cleared WT is blocked, never a delete.** `weight_kg` is NOT NULL with `CHECK (> 0)`; a blank cell is an `invalid` the save bar refuses. Adding and deleting rows stays in the production ledger — this screen only corrects a value.

### The sample grain
A **sample group** is `(recv_date, source_location_code, effective warehouse)` — one lab sample covers every draw from the same source + warehouse on a day. Partner receipts are the rows where `partner_equipment_code IS NOT NULL`. The effective warehouse is `coalesce(warehouse_code, plant_code)`: FLEC/DVO rows carry a real `warehouse_code` (`WHSE 1/3/5/7`), while TNK 1–4 / W7 draws have it NULL and the sheet's WHSE is the `plant_code` (`W6`/`W7`). That is why the sample table stores a resolved NOT NULL `whse_key` and why `effectiveWhseKey()` exists.

**The `〃` fan-out** is the data model made visible: the editable cells live on a group's FIRST draw, and its sibling draws render `〃` and inherit the value. Typing on the lead row covers the whole group's weight — verified live: setting BD=1 on the 2-draw 2026-05-01 FLEC/WHSE 7 group moves the day total to **0.9254**, the full-group-weight answer (40,437 kg), not the lead-draw-only figure.

### The four metrics that must appear on both pages
Renzo's requirement: **daily kg totals, monthly kg totals, daily weighted averages, monthly weighted averages** — visible on the ledger AND the breakdown.

| Metric | QC Ledger (entry) | QC Breakdown (reading) |
|---|---|---|
| Daily kg | `Σ DAY TOTAL` row under every day block | daily rollup table's `TTL WT` column |
| Monthly kg | frozen `Σ <MONTH>` footer + the toolbar chip | monthly rollup row + the daily table's `Σ` footer |
| Daily wtd avgs | the four analysis columns of `Σ DAY TOTAL` **and** the KPI tile sparklines | daily rollup table + the `Daily` grain of the metric panels |
| Monthly wtd avgs | KPI tile headline **and** the frozen footer | monthly rollup row + the `Monthly` grain of the metric panels |

**Verified reconciliation figure (re-verified in the browser 2026-08-01, now sourced from SQL):** May 2026 = **1,134,070 kg**, weighted **0.5602 / 2.80 / 0.73 / 11.63**. Rendered identically by the ledger's toolbar chip, the ledger's frozen `Σ MAY 2026` footer, the breakdown's monthly MAY row and the breakdown's daily `Σ MAY 2026` footer. May carries no DVO, so `all` and `ex_dvo` coincide there — June and July do have DVO, which is why the ledger's footer captions the included DVO tonnage (`incl. 720,958 kg DVO` in July) while the breakdown stays strictly ex-DVO.

**Coverage is never silent.** A weighted average whose denominator is short of the row's total weight always says so: the day rows caption `wtd of NN% sampled`, the frozen footer captions each metric `wtd · NN%` (amber under 100% — May's ASH reads `wtd · 98%` because 2026-05-08 FLEC/WHSE 5 genuinely has no ASH), the toolbar chip carries the month's coverage, and both breakdown tables carry a `Cov` column. July 2026 renders kg with em-dash metrics and 0% coverage everywhere — correct, and the reason the feature exists. In the charts the same honesty is `connectNulls={false}`.

### Default month
Both routes resolve `?m=` identically (`resolveQcMonth`): a valid URL month, else **the newest month with receipts** (July 2026 today). Deliberately the same on both — they share one `?m=` axis and cross-link, so different defaults would make the first click between them look like a navigation bug, and the newest month is where the outstanding entry work is. *(The evaluation draft A defaulted to the newest well-covered month instead, which was an artefact of wanting the demo to show filled cells. One line in `resolveQcMonth` if Renzo prefers that back.)*

### Keyboard model (the ledger)
Renzo: *"tabbing and entering should allow us to navigate it like how excel/google sheet would let us."* The editable cells run the canonical **Blackwood Table** stack: `useGridKeyboardNav` (`lib/hooks/`) over the shared `GridCell` + `EditInput` (`components/shared/grid/`) — the same hook RC IN's bulk grid and the production schedule use.

**The resolver is local (`createQcNavResolver`), not the shared `createCoordinateNavResolver`** — and the reason is the whole design. The shared factory's `columnMap` is per-COLUMN; this grid needs per-CELL addressability:

- **The coordinate space is `entryRows.length × 5`**, columns in visual order `WT · BD · ASH · GRIT · MC` (nav col 0 = weight, nav col n = `METRICS[n-1]`). Row `n` is the nth **DRAW** in visual order — it used to be the nth sample GROUP, and it had to change, because a weight belongs to one draw and every draw's WT must be addressable.
- **The two column families disagree about which rows they occupy.** WT is live on EVERY row; the metrics live only on a group's LEAD row and have **no coordinate at all** on a `〃` sibling. Day-header rows, `Σ DAY TOTAL` rows and the frozen footer have no coordinates either.
- The behavioural consequence is the data model's own asymmetry: **ArrowDown in a metric lane walks lead-to-lead, skipping siblings exactly as it did before this change; ArrowDown in the WT lane walks draw-to-draw.**
- Tab off MC wraps to the next row's WT; Shift+Tab off WT wraps back to the previous row's last live cell; arrows clamp at the edges (ArrowRight on a sibling's WT goes nowhere — there is nothing live to its right).
- `enableEnterAnchor: true` — the first Tab of a run records the lane, and a later plain Enter drops one row and **returns to that lane** (WT→BD→ASH→GRIT→MC→Enter lands on the next row's WT; a run started at BD comes back to BD). When the anchored metric lane does not exist on the rows below, the resolver walks past the siblings to the next row that has it.
- Click SELECTS (ring only); a printable key types over; F2 / double-click edits in place keeping the value; Escape reverts to the pre-edit snapshot.
- The `min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-auto` scrollport is the keyboard host (`role="grid"`, `tabIndex={0}`); cells are `tabIndex={-1}`, so Tab is the grid's to interpret and can never walk out of the sheet mid-entry.
- **`setCell` prunes no-op edits.** A value equal to the STORED one deletes its entry instead of recording one, so Escape (which replays the pre-edit snapshot through `setValue`) leaves the group genuinely clean rather than inflating the dirty count.
- **Delete/Backspace is already undoable here, and that is not an accident (audited 2026-08-04).** This ledger wires **no `range` slot and no `useCellDelete`**, so every Delete/Backspace falls through to `useGridKeyboardNav`'s single-cell branch — `edit.start(active, '')`, which snapshots the pre-edit value in `useGridEditSession` *before* blanking the cell and opens an editor over it. Escape therefore restores it, through the ordinary editing-mode revert. Do **not** add a range-delete path here without also giving it a snapshot: the unsnapshotted `range.onDelete` → `range.clear()` pair in the shared hook is exactly the trap the Cenapro RC Deliveries ledger had to work around in its own wrapper.
- **Focus never scrolls (2026-08-04).** `HTMLElement.focus()` scrolls its target into view with block AND inline `"center"` through every scrolling ancestor, and `"center"` always computes a target — so it fires even when the cell is already fully visible. `focusCell` is the `onAfterMove` handler, i.e. it ran on **every** Tab / Enter / arrow, and its `root.focus()` re-centred the page each time. All three focus sites (`focusCell`, `revertCellEdit`, the commit) now pass **`{ preventScroll: true }`**. The `scrollIntoView({ block:'nearest', inline:'nearest' })` inside `focusCell` **stays** — that is the deliberate caret-follow, minimal by construction and a no-op for a cell already in view. Focus still moves; only the implicit re-centre is refused. See "Focus must never scroll" in `components/shared/grid/CONTEXT.md`.

**Driven and verified in a real browser (2026-08-03, MAY 2026, real data).** The first three rows are a 2-draw group (25,602 + 14,835 kg, lead + `〃`) then a 1-draw group (8,330 kg):

| Keys sent | Landed on | What it proves |
|---|---|---|
| click WT row 0 | `(0,0)` "25,602" | WT is selectable; click selects, does not edit |
| `Tab` | `(0,1)` "0.565" | WT → BD, visual order |
| `Tab ×3` | `(0,4)` "10.80" | across to MC |
| `Tab` | `(1,0)` "14,835" | **wraps onto the SIBLING row's WT** — a cell no metric can reach |
| `Tab` | `(2,0)` "8,330" | skips the sibling's four inert `〃` cells entirely |
| `ArrowDown ×2` | `(4,0)` "4,192" | WT lane walks draw-to-draw |
| `ArrowRight` then `ArrowDown` | `(6,1)` "0.560" | **metric lane skips the sibling row 5** — pre-existing behaviour, preserved |
| `ArrowLeft` then `ArrowDown` | `(7,0)` "3,433" | **same rows, WT lane lands ON the sibling** — the asymmetry, both directions |
| `ArrowRight ×3` from `(7,0)` | `(7,0)` | clamps; a sibling's `〃` cells are genuinely unreachable |
| click `(0,0)`, `Tab ×4`, `Enter` | `(1,0)` | Enter-anchor returns to the WT lane the run started in |
| press `4` | editor opens `"4"` | type-to-edit on a weight |
| type `835`, `Enter` | committed | row 1 WT → `4835`, **row 0 still 25,602 — no fan-out**; `Σ DAY TOTAL` 48,767 → 38,767, wtd BD 0.5647 → 0.5646; footer 1,134,070 → 1,124,070 kg, 0.5602 → 0.5601; button `Save 1 weight` |
| press `0`, `Enter`, click Save | blocked | amber *"1 weight is not a valid number of kilograms"*, 1 rose-ringed cell, banner *"MAY 01 · W7 · W7 · C1 · WT — A weight must be a positive number of kilograms."*, **no Reload button**, nothing sent |
| click Discard | restored | weights 25,602 / 14,835 / 8,330; day total 48,767 / 0.5647; footer **1,134,070 · 0.5602 / 2.80 / 0.73 / 11.63** |
| `Tab` to `(0,1)`, press `1`, `Enter` | `Σ DAY TOTAL` BD = **0.9254** | the documented fan-out regression still holds — the sibling still reads `〃` and the reading still covers the group's full 40,437 kg |

## CCC Analysis — LIVE BACKEND (shipped 2026-08-01)

**Migrations:** `supabase/migrations/20260801073405_cenapro_analysis_sample.sql` (table + accessor), `..073646_cenapro_ccc_analysis_views.sql` (aggregates), `..073853_cenapro_analysis_write_path.sql` (RPCs), **`20260803060000_cenapro_event_audit_and_weight_write.sql`** (the production_event audit trail + the weight RPC — see "Auditing production_event" below), **`20260803090000_cenapro_add_partner_draw.sql`** (the partner-draw ADD RPC — see "Adding a partner draw" below; no new table, no new trigger, no view change). **Backfill:** `scripts/cenapro/backfill-ccc-analysis-samples.mjs` (re-runnable; `--dry-run` supported). **Frontend contract:** `lib/cenapro/ccc-analysis.ts`.

### The table — `cenapro.analysis_sample`
One row per `(sample_date, source_location_code, whse_key)`. `bd` / `ash` / `grit` / `mc` are **independently nullable** (2026-05-08 FLEC/WHSE 5 genuinely has no ASH). Provenance: `source` (`sheet_backfill` | `app`), `notes`, `row_version`, `created_at/by`, `updated_at/by` (FK→`profiles`, ON DELETE SET NULL). RLS on, single-org (`authenticated` = full CRUD, `anon` nothing).

**The NULL-warehouse uniqueness trap, and how it's solved.** The grain's third component is the *effective* warehouse = `coalesce(warehouse_code, plant_code)` — tank and W7 draws carry a NULL `warehouse_code`. A UNIQUE spanning a nullable column would NOT de-duplicate those rows (`NULL <> NULL` in a unique index), so every tank sample would be insertable an unlimited number of times. The table therefore stores the **resolved token in a NOT NULL `whse_key` column** and the unique constraint `cenapro_analysis_sample_natural_key` spans three NOT NULL columns. `cenapro.fn_canon_token(text)` (IMMUTABLE: trim / collapse whitespace / uppercase) is the ONE normalization definition, shared by the table's CHECKs, the views' join key and the write RPCs; `canonToken()` in `lib/cenapro/ccc-analysis.ts` is its JS twin. CHECK bounds are **unit sanity only** (bd 0–5, the three percentages 0–100) — deliberately far wider than the observed sheet ranges, because a tight "observed range" bound would reject the outlier an analyst most needs to see.

### The views (aggregation lives HERE, not in TS)
| Object (public accessor) | Grain | Provides |
|---|---|---|
| `cenapro_analysis_samples` | one lab sample | The raw ledger, **all 500 rows** including the 117 that match no event group yet. Auto-updatable. |
| `cenapro_ccc_sample_groups` | (date, src, effective whse) | `draw_count`, `total_kg`, the joined `bd/ash/grit/mc`, `is_sampled`, `is_complete`, `missing_metric_count`, `source_group`, `is_dvo`, and **`sample_row_version`** (feeds the save RPC — NULL means "unsampled, create it"). |
| `cenapro_ccc_analysis_daily` | `sample_date` × `scope` | `total_kg`, `sampled_kg`, `coverage`, group counts, the four `wtd_*` + their per-metric denominators `wtd_*_kg`. |
| `cenapro_ccc_analysis_monthly` | `month_start`/`month_key` × `scope` | Same, plus `day_count`. |

**Weighted average = `SUM(kg × metric) / SUM(kg)` over the groups carrying THAT metric, per metric independently** — a group missing ASH still contributes its full weight to BD/GRIT/MC, which is why each metric ships its own denominator. Proof in live data: 2026-05-08 has `wtd_bd_kg` = 55,207 but `wtd_ash_kg` = 35,233.

**Ex-DVO is a `scope` COLUMN, not a frontend filter.** The daily and monthly views emit **two rows per period**: `scope='all'` (entry ledger) and `scope='ex_dvo'` (the reading page's headline). Both come from one aggregation expression so they cannot disagree, and `all_kg` / `dvo_kg` / `ex_dvo_kg` are period-wide on *either* row — so a single `ex_dvo` row already carries the breakdown's whole day shape (ex-DVO total + DVO tonnage + ex-DVO weighted averages) without a second query. Always filter on `scope`.

**Regression, verified from SQL:** `month_key='2026-05'`, `scope='ex_dvo'` → `total_kg` **1,134,070**, wtd **0.5602 / 2.80 / 0.73 / 11.63** — identical to what the drafts render.

### The write path
- **`public.cenapro_save_analysis_sample(p_sample_date, p_source_location_code, p_whse_key, p_bd, p_ash, p_grit, p_mc, p_expected_row_version, p_notes) → jsonb`** — SECURITY INVOKER, `authenticated` + `service_role`. Upserts by the natural key, canonicalizes the key server-side, stamps `created_by`/`updated_by = auth.uid()` and flips `source` to `'app'`. **Optimistic concurrency:** pass `p_expected_row_version = NULL` to CREATE (a losing race returns `already_exists`, never a clobber) or the `sample_row_version` you read to UPDATE (a mismatch returns `version_conflict` **plus the current version**). The version is checked in the SAME statement as the write — the `fn_save_schedule_day` idiom, no read-then-write. `row_version` is bumped by a **trigger**, so a raw write through the accessor view advances it too and cannot silently defeat the lock. Outcomes: `inserted` / `updated` / `already_exists` / `version_conflict` / `not_found` / `no_metrics` / `invalid_key`.
- **`public.cenapro_import_analysis_samples(p_rows jsonb) → jsonb`** — **`service_role` ONLY.** The idempotent sheet import. `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` is gated on `source = 'sheet_backfill'`, so a re-run can **never** clobber a reading an operator typed in the app, and on the values differing, so an unchanged re-run honestly reports `updated: 0`.
- **No delete RPC on purpose** — `public.cenapro_analysis_samples` is auto-updatable with DELETE granted to `authenticated`, and a delete carries no provenance an RPC could add.
- **`public.cenapro_add_partner_draw(p_recv_date, p_source_location_code, p_partner_equipment_code, p_grade_code, p_shift_code, p_weight_kg, p_prod_date, p_warehouse_code, p_flec_count, p_whse_side, p_batch, p_batch_year, p_notes, p_allow_duplicate) → jsonb`** (2026-08-03) — see "Adding a partner draw" below.
- **`public.cenapro_update_event_weight(p_event_id uuid, p_expected_weight_kg numeric, p_weight_kg numeric) → jsonb`** (2026-08-03) — SECURITY INVOKER, `SET search_path = ''`, EXECUTE revoked from `PUBLIC`, granted to `authenticated` + `service_role`. Changes ONE `cenapro.production_event.weight_kg`. **Compare-and-set, not a row version:** `UPDATE … WHERE id = $1 AND weight_kg = $2`, the check in the SAME statement as the write. `production_event` has no `row_version` column and adding one would force the production ledger's bulk upsert to honour it too, so the caller sends the value it is looking at instead. Zero rows matched → re-read: row gone ⇒ `not_found`, row moved ⇒ `conflict` **plus the CURRENT weight** in the message (`trim_scale`d, so a stored `16434.0` reads `16434`). Guards before any write: id present, expected present (a NULL expected would be a blind write), `> 0`, `scale ≤ 3`, `≤ 10,000,000` (unit sanity only — the heaviest draw on record is 139,917 kg, and a bound tuned to observed data would reject the first genuinely large receipt). Stamps `cenapro.audit_source = 'qc_ledger'` so the trail knows which surface wrote — **and clears it again immediately after the UPDATE**. `set_config(…, true)` is TRANSACTION-local, not statement-local; left set, a later direct write through the auto-updatable view in the same transaction would be trailed as `qc_ledger` when it was nothing of the sort. Each PostgREST request is its own transaction so it could not bite through the app, but a `source` that is only conditionally true is worse than no `source`. Outcomes: `updated` / `conflict` / `not_found` / `invalid`.

### Adding a partner draw — `public.cenapro_add_partner_draw` (2026-08-03)

**Why the QC Ledger gains an ADD, and why only this one kind of row.** Renzo: *"Partner sends their totals on a piece of paper and we log those in. Everything they give us is how much they're reporting. Whatever we put into inventory (flecon bagged inventory) is reported on a separate sheet."* Two source documents, two entry surfaces. **Partner draws are added here; flec bagging stays in the Production ledger** — and that boundary is enforced by the RPC, not by the UI, so a hand-rolled client call cannot cross it.

**Why an RPC and not a plain INSERT through the auto-updatable view.** `authenticated` already *can* insert an event by hand; what it cannot do by hand is keep the four invariants below. Each one is silent when it fails, which is why none of them may live in the caller:

| Invariant | Why it cannot be client-side |
|---|---|
| Partner-draw only | Nothing in the view stops a `flec_bagging` row. |
| `plant_code` **defaulted** from the source (TNK 1–4→`W6`, `W7`→`W7`, `W6`→`W6`, FLEC→NULL, per CENAPRO_SCHEMA §8.2), and any supplied value **validated** against `cenapro.plant` | For a tank draw the plant **IS** the QC ledger's `whse_key` (`coalesce(warehouse_code, plant_code)`). A NULL or a nonsense value there files the row under an empty/phantom key in a sample group of its own. **This is an argument for plant being present and REAL, not for it being underivable from the operator** — see "PLANT is typable" below (2026-08-04). |
| `batch` = the label RUNNING at `recv_date` | Batch is **not** the calendar month. JULY first appears 2026-06-27 while JUNE rows keep arriving to 06-29; **13 dates in live data carry two labels at once.** |
| Source-conditional bag fields | A `flec_count` silently dropped from a FLEC draw is an under-consumed warehouse nobody notices — **263 of the 714 partner draws (37%) are FLEC-sourced**. |

**Validation — every one of these REJECTS, none coerces.** `partner_equipment_code` required (blank, or `FLEC`/`BAGGING`, → `wrong_surface` pointing at the Production ledger); the machine must be `C1`–`C4`/`RK1`–`RK4` and it alone decides `disposition_kind` (crusher→`partner_crusher`, kiln→`partner_kiln`); `recv_date`/`source`/`grade`/`shift` required and each checked against its dimension table so the caller gets a sentence rather than a raw FK violation; `recv_date` inside `2025-01-01 … today+30d` and `prod_date` (optional) not after `recv_date` nor more than 270 days before it — **both year-typo guards, the failure mode that stranded five flecon movements for months**; `weight_kg` positive, ≤ 3 dp, ≤ 10,000,000 kg — the *same rules and the same wording* as `cenapro_update_event_weight`, and `parseWeightKg()` is their shared JS twin. **DVO is refused by name** (`unsupported_source`) — a container van into WHSE 3 under its own batch code is a different document and still deferred in v1; its 69 historic rows stay visible and editable.

**Source-conditional bag fields.** `FLEC` → `warehouse_code` REQUIRED (and must be a `flec_count` warehouse — WHSE 1/2/5/7, not WHSE 3) and `flec_count` REQUIRED, `whse_side` optional. Anything else → all three must be ABSENT, and supplying one is a refusal naming the source, never a silent drop. A FLEC draw saved **without** a side still succeeds but returns a `notice`: `cenapro.flec_ledger` counts only sided rows, so that outflow will not move the LS/RS balance until a side is set.

**`batch` resolution.** Default = the label carried by the most rows on the **nearest logged `recv_date` at or before** the new one, ties broken by whichever label **started** most recently — which is what makes a changeover day resolve forward to the new batch. Overridable with `p_batch` (+ optional `p_batch_year`); an explicit label inherits the year that label last carried, so `'JULY'` joins the existing JULY period instead of opening a second one. `batch_year` is passed explicitly (the trigger only fills it when NULL), so the row lands in the resolved period, not the calendar year of the receipt. `batch_resolution` (`running` | `explicit` | `calendar`) comes back so the UI can show which batch it picked. Live: 06-26→`JUNE`, **06-27 (the changeover)→`JULY`**, 08-01 (JULY×11 vs AUGUST×1)→`JULY`, 08-03→`JULY`.

**Duplicates: a warning, not a constraint — and the reason is in the data.** Fourteen `(recv_date, source, machine, grade, shift)` keys already carry two draws, so a hard UNIQUE on that key would reject real work. But **all fourteen differ somewhere in `unique_tag`**, because the `unique_tag` UNIQUE index is what actually forbids storing the same row twice. So the two cases answer separately: an **identical** row (same tag) → `already_exists`, a hard refusal carrying the row already stored — the DB cannot hold it whatever the intent, and in practice it is a double-submit; a **same-key but otherwise different** row → `duplicate_warning`, a SOFT refusal listing the existing draws that the UI confirms through by re-sending with `p_allow_duplicate: true`. The ambiguity is the operator's to resolve — only they know whether the slip lists two trips or they keyed one twice. A `unique_violation` caught at INSERT time returns the same `already_exists`, so a race and a repeat read identically (the remedy is identical too).

**Posture:** SECURITY INVOKER, `SET search_path = ''`, EXECUTE revoked from `PUBLIC`, granted to `authenticated` + `service_role`. Stamps `provenance = 'qc_ledger'` and `cenapro.audit_source = 'qc_ledger'` (cleared immediately after the INSERT, same transaction-local discipline as the weight RPC), leaves `dirty` at its default, and never sends `unique_tag`/`disposition_kind` from the caller (`plant_code` MAY now be supplied — see "PLANT is typable" below). **No new audit mechanism** — the existing `tr_cenapro_pe_audit` trails the INSERT with the actor. **No view change** — `cenapro.view_ccc_sample_group` is built from `production_event` itself, so a correctly-shaped row joins the right group automatically; the RPC returns that group's exact key in `sample_group` so the UI can scroll to it without re-deriving the `coalesce`. Outcomes: `inserted` / `duplicate_warning` / `already_exists` / `wrong_surface` / `unsupported_source` / `invalid_key` / `invalid`. **Frontend contract:** `AddPartnerDrawOutcome` / `AddPartnerDrawResult` / `AddPartnerDrawArgs` / `BatchResolution` / `ExistingDraw` in `lib/cenapro/ccc-analysis.ts`.

**Proven live, then rolled back (2026-08-03), as `authenticated` with a real `auth.uid()`:** a tank draw inserts (`plant W6`, `whse_key W6`, batch `JULY/2026`); a FLEC draw with WHSE 7 + 38 bags + LS inserts (`plant null`, `whse_key WHSE 7`); FLEC missing `flec_count`, FLEC missing warehouse, tank with `flec_count`, tank with warehouse, tank with side, weight 0, weight 4 dp, `recv_date` 2016, `prod_date` after `recv_date` all refuse; no-equipment and `FLEC`-as-equipment both `wrong_surface`; `C9`/`5X9`/`Z`/`TNK 9` `invalid_key`; DVO `unsupported_source`; the four boundary dates resolve as listed above; the audit trail wrote exactly **2** rows for the 2 inserts, `operation INSERT`, `source qc_ledger`, `changed_by 2503f371-…`, `changed_by_role authenticated`; both draws matched **exactly one** `cenapro_ccc_sample_groups` row with no view change; `anon` denied on the function (42501) and on the base table (42501). After `ROLLBACK` the table is byte-identical — **1,126 rows / 16,202,719 kg / md5 `ad3ab4eac131b456c844bd1e02c7e431`**, and May 2026 still reads 1,134,070 kg / 0.5602 / 2.80 / 0.73 / 11.63.

### PLANT is typable — `p_plant` (2026-08-04, migration `20260804080000_cenapro_partner_draw_optional_plant.sql`)

**The rule it replaces was asymmetric, and the asymmetry had no defence.** Renzo: *"I don't understand how PLANT has to stay this way when it's very much typeable in the production ledger? Isn't the main goal here to bring that functionality into add draw?"* He is right. `cenapro_add_partner_draw` INSERTs into `cenapro.production_event` — **the same table the Production ledger writes** through the auto-updatable `public.cenapro_production_events` view, where `plant_code` is an ordinary editable dropdown (`PLANT_CODES` in `../types.ts`, `SelectCell` at `production/production-ledger-grid.tsx`) that anyone may set to any plant. Same column, same table, freely writable on one screen and underivable on the other. And the QC Ledger is precisely where **partner slips** are transcribed — a partner's paper can legitimately name a plant the source mapping does not predict, so refusing the operator's transcription is the wrong default for that screen. The platform's standing rule is that a human arbitrates a disagreement rather than a machine silently picking a side (CLAUDE.md → "Sync Integrity").

**What was kept.** The old comment's fear was real but mis-aimed: `plant_code` is half the QC sample-group key (`whse_key = fn_canon_token(coalesce(warehouse_code, plant_code))`), so an EMPTY or NONSENSE plant on a tank draw forms a phantom group. That argues for the plant being **present and real**, not for it being underivable — a typed value that is a real plant satisfies the key exactly as well as a derived one. So the new rule is permissive with that one guarantee intact:

| `p_plant` | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| omitted / `null` / blank / whitespace | **Derive from the source, byte-for-byte as before.** Blank means "follow the source", **never** "clear the plant" — clearing stays unreachable, so the phantom group stays unreachable. Every pre-2026-08-04 call site is unaffected. |
| a real `cenapro.plant` code (case/whitespace-insensitive — `fn_canon_token`) | **Accepted and stored**, whether or not it agrees with the source. |
| anything else | **Refused** `invalid_key`, by name, listing the real codes and naming what the source would have given: `'W9' is not a known plant. Use DVO, W6, W6/W7, W7 - or leave the plant blank and it follows the source (TNK 3 reports to W6).` |

**A valid plant that DISAGREES with the source is accepted, not refused** — and reported back. `plant_notice` is a **non-blocking** sentence (unlike `duplicate_warning`, there is no confirm round-trip: the operator is copying a slip, not resolving an ambiguity the machine can see) naming both values and, for a non-FLEC source, saying which sample group the draw therefore joins. It is non-null **only** on a real disagreement. `cenapro.plant` is the validation dimension because it is the FK target of `production_event.plant_code` and the very table `source_location.plant_code` is drawn from — the same dimension the derivation reads. `whse_key` is untouched: it already reads `coalesce(warehouse_code, plant_code)`, so a typed plant flows through correctly, and on a FLEC draw (warehouse mandatory) the plant does not affect grouping at all.

**Verdict shape.** `plant_code` keeps its key and now carries the **effective** value (typed when supplied, derived otherwise). Three keys are added on `inserted`, mirroring the `batch_resolution` idiom so the UI can render the disagreement structurally instead of parsing a sentence: **`plant_source`** (`'derived' | 'supplied'`), **`plant_derived`** (`string | null` — what the source alone would have given), **`plant_notice`** (`string | null`). The pre-existing `notice` is unchanged and still carries only the sideless-FLEC remark, so a UI may surface both independently.

**Signature.** `p_plant text default null` is appended **LAST**, after `p_allow_duplicate`, so every positional call is unchanged and every named call simply omits it. The old 14-argument function is **DROPped**, not left beside the new one — two overloads differing only by a defaulted trailing argument are ambiguous to PostgREST's candidate resolution. Exactly one function exists. Grants re-issued against the new signature (a fresh `create` gets PostgreSQL's default EXECUTE-to-PUBLIC): revoked from `PUBLIC`, granted to `authenticated` + `service_role` only — ACL verified byte-identical to before, `anon` denied `42501`. **Duplicate detection follows the plant:** `unique_tag` includes `plant_code`, so a deliberately different plant genuinely yields a different tag and therefore a row the DB can hold; the `already_exists` pre-check compares the EFFECTIVE plant.

**Proven live, then rolled back (2026-08-04), as `authenticated` with a real `auth.uid()`:** the omitted / explicit-`null` / blank paths all return `plant_code W6`, `plant_source derived`, `plant_notice null`, `whse_key W6` on a `TNK 3` draw (identical to the old function); `p_plant := 'W6'` on the same source → `plant_source supplied`, no notice; `p_plant := 'W7'` → stored `W7`, `plant_derived W6`, notice naming both, `whse_key W7`; `p_plant := ' w6/w7 '` canonicalizes to `W6/W7`; `'W9'` and `'PLANT B'` refuse `invalid_key` with the source-appropriate tail; a valid plant does **not** rescue a DVO source (`unsupported_source` still wins); `p_plant := 'W6'` on a `FLEC` draw (derived NULL) inserts with the FLEC-flavoured notice while `whse_key` stays `WHSE 1`; re-sending the `W7` row hits `already_exists`. After `ROLLBACK`: **1,140 rows, 0 `qc_ledger`-provenance rows, 0 audit rows** — no residue. Prod's `prosrc` md5 matches the migration file's body exactly (27,078 chars).

**Frontend half — DONE (2026-08-04, same day).** `lib/cenapro/ccc-analysis.ts` carries `plant_source` / `plant_derived` / `plant_notice` on `AddPartnerDrawResult` (`AddPartnerDrawArgs` picked `p_plant?: string` up from the regenerated `types/supabase.ts` for free); `qc/actions.ts` takes an `AddQcDrawInput.plant` override, forwards it as `if (plant) args.p_plant = plant` (**omitted when blank, never an explicit null** — the file's idiom for every optional arg, and the exact shape the derive path was proven live with) and narrows the three keys back out of the `jsonb`; the QC draft row renders the column as the Production ledger's own `SelectCell` + `plantBadgeClass`. **The UI's contract, stated once:** `DraftDraw.plant` is the OVERRIDE ALONE (`''` = follow SRC), the cell shows `effectivePlant()` with a derived value **ghosted** and a typed one solid, `— follow SRC` clears back to derived, and `p_plant` is sent **iff** `plantOverride()` is non-empty — so `plant_source` keeps its meaning and `plant_notice` fires only on a disagreement the operator actually authored. `plant_notice` surfaces **on the row's own status line**, non-blocking, with no confirm round trip. The two `scripts/verify-qc-draw-cells.ts` assertions that pinned "PLANT is never typed and no `p_plant` is ever sent" are **INVERTED** — they now assert that an override IS sent, that a derived value is NOT, and that blank means derive.

### Adding a draw — SPREADSHEET ROWS (2026-08-04, supersedes the panel below)

**The composer was a form that produced rows. This is the rows.**

- **`Add draw` appends TEN blank rows** in a trailing "New draws" block under the month. **`10 more rows`** under the block adds another ten; there is no cap.
- **A day header's `+ ADD` appends ONE blank row INSIDE that day**, under its saved rows and above its `Σ DAY TOTAL` — "adding a row inside of an existing day".
- **A blank is genuinely blank, BOTH date cells included (2026-08-04).** Renzo: *"when you add draw, leave the new cells in both dates column blank."* Nothing is pre-dated, so a date on a row is always a date someone typed — which is why **`isMeaningfulDraft` now counts `recvDate`** (it used to skip it precisely because every blank was born dated; keeping that skip would have made a row with only a date typed look untouched and be dropped by Save without a word). Ten fresh blanks still light nothing up and show no status line, so `needs a date` being the common starting state costs nothing.
- **`anchorDate` decides which block a row renders in and is FIXED at creation.** Deliberately not `recvDate`: the date is typable, so keying layout off it would make a row jump to another block — or into one that does not exist — between two keystrokes of retyping it. Blank dates did **not** change that: `anchorDate` is the only date a blank carries, it is layout rather than data, and the save path never reads it.
- **Three columns joined the grid because the RPC requires them:** `Mach` (`partner_equipment_code` — `cenapro_add_partner_draw` REFUSES a draw without it, and there was no column at all), plus `Bags` (`flec_count`) and `Side` (`whse_side`) for FLEC draws. They render on EVERY row, not just blanks — a row you are typing must look like the rows above it or the grid stops reading as one sheet. Grid is now **15 columns**, min-width **1,146px**; `LABEL_SPAN` is derived (`COLS.findIndex(c => c.key === 'wt')`) rather than the hardcoded `7`, so it can never drift from the column list again. **The bag fields are NO LONGER `disabled` on a non-FLEC source (2026-08-04, second pass)** — that gate had nothing behind it and, because `Whse`/`Side`/`Bags` sit BEFORE `SRC` in the column order, it made typing down a row left-to-right impossible. The source-conditional rule is now stated by `draftBlocker` (and refused by the server and the RPC), never by a lock.
- **ONE Save, three write paths.** New rows go first (they create the sample groups the readings attach to, so a slip transcribed and analysed in one sitting saves in one press), then weights, then readings. `addQcDraws` is a **loop over `addPartnerDraw`, not a new RPC** — every rule that makes a draw legal already lives there and in the RPC beneath it, and a bulk variant would be a second place for them to drift. **Sequential on purpose:** the RPC resolves the running `batch` from rows already logged at or before the date, so a run of same-day draws must see each other land; in parallel every row would resolve against the pre-batch state — usually the same answer, silently wrong on a changeover day.
- **An untouched blank is not an unsaved change.** Ten waiting blanks neither light up Save nor count as ten errors (`isMeaningfulDraft` ignores the date every blank is born with). `draftBlocker` is a **courtesy** naming the obviously-missing field so the operator does not round-trip for it; everything that passes is judged by the RPC and the RPC's own message is what renders. Incomplete rows are **skipped, not lost** — the save bar says so.
- **A refusal keeps what was typed**, per row, like every other save on this screen. Editing a refused row clears its message (the operator is answering it). A `duplicate_warning` sets `needsDuplicateConfirm`, so pressing Save again re-sends that row confirmed — the ambiguity is the operator's, since only they know whether the slip lists two trips or they keyed one twice.
- **Cancelling (2026-08-04).** `Cancel adding` is always on screen while `addingDraws` — including with ten untouched blanks, the state that had no exit. It leaves add mode and drops every draft row; an `AlertDialog` (canonical glass + `animate-modal-enter`, chrome only — no row motion) confirms first and names the count when a draft carries typing. `Discard edits` is now scoped to the readings + weights on SAVED rows, so the two controls never mean the same thing. `Clear N empty rows` was removed as a third near-synonym; per-row removal is the `×` in that row's PLANT cell, which now sits beside the plant dropdown.
- **Dates parse like Excel (2026-08-04).** `Date` and `Prod` are free-text cells, not `<input type="date">`. `parseQcDate` (in `lib/cenapro/ccc-analysis.ts`, the same `normalizeTypedDate` the production ledger and the paste path use) normalizes on blur and on Enter; a bare `6/27` takes the **focused month's year**, since the ledger is month-scoped and that is the month the slip belongs to. Text it cannot read is **left exactly as typed** and named in the row's status line — never a silently written wrong date — and `draftToInput` normalizes again on the way out, so Saving from a focused cell cannot send `6/27` to a `date` column.
- **BD / ASH / GRIT / MC are typable on a draft row (2026-08-04), and the draw is written before the reading.** A reading belongs to a sample GROUP that may not exist until the draw is saved, so `addQcDraws` inserts the draw, takes the group identity **from the RPC's own `sample_group`**, and applies the reading to that group with the version the screen was shown. **Two rows landing in one group with two different values for the same metric is refused and named at both ends** (client-side before the round trip, server-side over the RPC's answer); different metrics on two rows of one group merge. A row whose draw landed but whose reading was refused is marked `drawSaved`: it stays on screen carrying the reason and is never re-sent.
- **A draft row contains its own keystrokes** (`onKeyDown={(e) => e.stopPropagation()}` on the draft `<tr>`). Draft rows sit inside the grid scrollport, which carries `useGridKeyboardNav`'s `handleKeyDown`, and that handler bails only when NOTHING is selected — so with a saved cell selected (the state the moment anyone clicks a WT or a metric) every keystroke typed into a draft input ALSO drove the saved-cell state machine: Tab was `preventDefault`-ed and rerouted into the coordinate space, and a printable character opened an editor on the SAVED cell and typed into it. Pre-existing, but directly the "I can't type across the row" symptom, and fixed HERE rather than in the shared hook or the saved-row model — draft rows deliberately use plain inputs with native Tab order, so their keys simply never leave the row.
- **A supplied PLANT is an override, and only an override.** The cell defaults to the live derivation from SRC and renders it ghosted; `p_plant` travels only when the operator picked something, so `plant_source`/`plant_notice` keep their meaning. `— follow SRC` is the way back, and it means *derive*, never *clear*. An unknown code is the RPC's `invalid_key`, shown verbatim — there is no client-side plant allowlist to drift.
- **A `plant_notice` is INFORMATION, not a gate.** It rides back with a successful insert (a typed plant disagreeing with the source), it lands on the row's own status line in green beside `saved`, and the row is kept on screen (marked `drawSaved`, so never re-sent) purely so the sentence has somewhere to be read. The sideless-FLEC `notice` is surfaced the same way and the two are independent — a FLEC row can carry both. Neither gets the `duplicate_warning` confirm round trip.
- **`scripts/verify-qc-draw-cells.ts` (36 assertions, framework-free)** covers all four: the date parsing (every shape the operators type, the Feb-30 trap, the refusal path, the context year), the editability rules (the column count matches `COLS`, no cell branches its `disabled` on the DATA, the draft `<tr>` contains its keys), **PLANT** (the derivation is unchanged and is what a blank row shows; a typed plant overrides it and canonicalizes; `p_plant` is sent for an override and NEVER for a derived value; blank is OMITTED rather than nulled; the three verdict keys are typed and read back; a notice is never turned into a confirm; the group mirror follows the OVERRIDDEN plant), **blank dates** (both cells start empty, a date-only row IS meaningful, ten blanks are still zero problems, and `anchorDate` never tracks `recvDate` — asserted behaviourally AND by source scan at both ends), and the draft→group reading application (the key mirrors the RPC's `coalesce`, conflicts vs unions, draw-before-reading ordering, no blind force-write). Run `npx tsx scripts/verify-qc-draw-cells.ts`.

### Adding a draw from the ledger — the ORIGINAL panel UX (2026-08-03, RETIRED 2026-08-04)

> Kept as the record of what was tried and why it was replaced. `add-draw-panel.tsx` is deleted; do not resurrect it.

**The shape follows from the source document.** Renzo: *"Partner sends their totals on a piece of paper and we log those in."* A slip is one DAY and several LINES, and each line varies the machine, the grade and the number while repeating everything else. A modal costs four interactions per line (open · fill · submit · reopen) against a transcription that costs one, so the composer is a **docked panel**, not an overlay:

- **It opens once per slip and stays open.** A commit clears only the per-draw values (weight, bag count, note) and keeps the date, source, machine, grade, shift and warehouse, then returns focus to the weight box. A run of draws is `12400 ⏎`, retab the machine, `9880 ⏎`.
- **`Enter` anywhere in the form adds the line** (it is a real `<form onSubmit>`), which is the keystroke that makes transcription fast.
- **It does not dim the ledger.** The panel is a sibling of the grid inside one flex row, so each committed row appears in the day block beside it with its analysis cells ready and the `Σ DAY TOTAL` / `Σ MONTH` restated. That is also the confirmation — the operator watches the day build as they read the slip out.
- **A running receipt** under the form lists what this sitting has added — `05-14 · TNK 2 · C1 · 14,250` with the batch it landed in and how the RPC resolved it (`the batch running that day` / `the batch you named` / `the calendar month — no batch history…`), plus any `notice`.
- **Scoped to the month in view.** The date box is clamped to the month's `[from, lastDay]` and a date outside it is a client-side blocker. A draw saved into a month the operator is not looking at is a correct write they cannot see, which reads exactly like a failure. (Consequence: a month with no receipts yet is unreachable, because `resolveQcMonth` + the month picker are both driven by months that HAVE data. See "Not built" below.)
- **Client-side validation is a courtesy, never the authority.** Required fields, `parseWeightKg` (the RPC's own shared JS twin), a whole-number bag count and the FLEC-conditional rule are all checked before the button enables, with the single most relevant blocker printed under it. Everything that gets through is judged by the RPC, and the RPC's `message` is what is rendered.

**Source-conditional fields.** `source = FLEC` reveals an "OUT OF BAGGED STOCK" block — **Warehouse** (required, flec-count warehouses only: WHSE 1/2/5/7, never WHSE 3) and **Flecs out** (required), plus an optional **Side** whose cleared state reads `Not stated` rather than an empty placeholder. Any other source renders none of the three AND clears them on the switch, because the RPC refuses them by name — hiding a field while still sending it would turn a deliberate refusal into a mystery. The **sideless notice is surfaced twice**: pre-emptively, as an amber line under the Side picker whenever a FLEC draw has no side (*"Without LS or RS the flec ledger will not count this draw, so the warehouse balance will not move until a side is set"*), and again afterwards as the RPC's own `notice`, verbatim, on that draw's receipt line.

**One treatment per outcome** — the difference between them is the AFFORDANCE, not the wording, because the RPC's message is already written for the operator and names the offending value:

| Outcome | Treatment |
|---|---|
| `inserted` | Receipt line + the batch it landed in; the row is marked and scrolled to in the ledger using the RPC's own `sample_group`, never a re-derived `coalesce`. |
| `duplicate_warning` | Amber confirm block listing the `existing[]` draws (weight · batch · warehouse/side · prod date) + **Add anyway**, which re-sends the STORED input plus `p_allow_duplicate`. Editing any field withdraws the block — the confirmation belongs to the exact values it was raised for. |
| `already_exists` | Rose block, **no** add-anyway. Offers **Open the existing row** when that id is among the rows on screen, and says so plainly when it is not. |
| `wrong_surface` | Rose block + a link to `/cenapro/production`. |
| `unsupported_source` · `invalid_key` · `invalid` | Rose block, message verbatim, **no Reload affordance** — reloading cannot fix something the operator typed. |
| transport failure | `'rpc_error'` — the same rose block AND a persistent `errorToast` (error HARD RULE). |

Every inline block carries a **Copy** button (the inline half of the error HARD RULE) and `role="alert"`.

### Where the ADD form's options come from (and the accessor that would replace it)

The composer's pickers must not hide a machine the partner's slip names, so the lists are read from the database — but **not from the dimension tables, because nothing in the app can reach them.** `cenapro.source_location` / `partner_equipment` / `grade` / `shift` / `warehouse` live in a schema PostgREST does not serve (`Only the following schemas are exposed: public, graphql_public`), and no `public` accessor for them exists. `loadQcDrawOptions()` therefore reads the five code columns off `public.cenapro_production_events` — every distinct value there is FK-constrained, so it is by definition a live dimension row — and merges them over the canonical code constants in `../types.ts` (which that file already documents as an exact mirror of the seed). Canonical order first, then any value the data knows that the constants do not, so a code added to the seed surfaces on its first row.

**PLANT is the one list NOT read this way (2026-08-04).** Its four codes come straight from `PLANT_CODES` in `../types.ts` — the same constant the Production ledger's plant dropdown renders — because `cenapro.plant` is the RPC's own validation dimension and the two must offer exactly the same set or the UI would let an operator pick a value the server refuses. There is nothing to discover: a plant is not a code that arrives with new data the way a machine or a warehouse does.

**Why the merge is needed and not belt-and-braces:** the fact table alone is missing **C3, C4, 4X8 and WHSE 2** today — four options an operator would have had no way to pick. **What the merge still cannot fix:** a code seeded into a dimension table that neither the constants nor any event knows. That needs the accessor:

```
## Backend Request: expose the cenapro dimension tables read-only
**File:** a new migration (no change to the add-draw RPC, the sample table or the aggregate views)
**Object:** `public.cenapro_dimensions` — a `security_invoker` UNION view over
  cenapro.source_location / partner_equipment / grade / shift / warehouse
**Columns:** kind text ('source'|'equipment'|'grade'|'shift'|'warehouse'), code text,
  display_name text, sub_kind text (partner_equipment.kind, source_location.kind,
  warehouse.default_unit), sort_order int
**Grants:** SELECT to authenticated + service_role; REVOKE from anon (the DEFAULT-ACL trap below)
**Called from:** `loadQcDrawOptions()` in app/(app)/cenapro/qc/data.ts — replaces its whole
  body with one ~30-row read, drops the constants merge, drops a full-history scan per page
  load, and gains real display names ("Crusher 1", "Morning") plus a truthful crusher/kiln
  split from `partner_equipment.kind` instead of the code-shape guess it makes today.
```

**✅ Fixed 2026-08-04 — an empty month IS now reachable.** `resolveQcMonth` accepts any WELL-FORMED `?m=YYYY-MM` (guarded by `isValidMonthKey`, so `?m=banana` and `?m=2026-13` still fall back) rather than requiring the month to carry receipts, and `MonthYearPicker` no longer `disabled`s the empty months — all twelve are selectable, the `· no data` suffix stays so an absent month is still visible as information, the year list always offers the CURRENT year (on 1 January the newest year with receipts is last year's), and a year change with no receipts in the target year lands on the same calendar month instead of doing nothing. Both routes change together because they share `resolveQcMonth`. `loadQcLedgerData` already returned an `empty` shape for a month with no rows, and the grid already rendered "No partner receipts in <MONTH>" + an **Add the first draw** button (`defaultAddDate` falls back to `${month}-01`) — that path was simply unreachable. **`monthKeys` deliberately still means "months that HAVE receipts"**; padding it would have made the picker's `· no data` suffix lie.

### Auditing `cenapro.production_event` (2026-08-03)
`production_event` carried the partner-receipt weights every weighted average is weighted by, had exactly ONE trigger (`tr_cenapro_pe_unique_tag`) and **no audit of any kind** — the production ledger already allowed in-place `weight_kg` edits and nothing recorded who changed what. The QC Ledger makes that edit far more convenient, so the trail had to exist first.

- **`cenapro.production_event_audit`** — `id` (identity), `event_id` + `unique_tag` + `recv_date` (denormalized, so the trail stays readable after the row is deleted), `operation`, **`changed`** (UPDATE only: `{"weight_kg": {"old": 16433.0, "new": 16434.0}}` — only the columns that actually moved, `updated_at` EXCLUDED because the unique_tag trigger bumps it on every write; `{}` on INSERT/DELETE where the whole row is the change), **`snapshot`** (NEW, or OLD on DELETE), `source`, `changed_at`, `changed_by` (`auth.uid()`), `changed_by_role` (`auth.role()`).
- **Written by a trigger, not by the RPC.** `weight_kg` has TWO live write paths — this RPC and the production grid's direct UPDATE through the auto-updatable view. An RPC-side audit would trail one and quietly miss the other, which is worse than none because it looks complete. `tr_cenapro_pe_audit` (AFTER INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE) catches every writer, including a future sync or a manual SQL fix, and **skips an UPDATE whose only difference is `updated_at`**.
- **`cenapro.fn_audit_production_event()` is SECURITY DEFINER** so it is the ONLY thing that can write the table: no role holds INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on it. A SECURITY INVOKER trigger would need INSERT granted to `authenticated`, and a grant that lets the trigger write also lets a client forge a row.
- **NOT `public.audit_logs`, deliberately.** That is ICTC's, and it is not an inert log — it is read by the ICTC audit UI, joined by `audit_comments` / `notification_subscriptions`, and feeds `_insert_notification`. Cenapro rows landing there would surface inside another tenant's screens. **NOT `cenapro.drift_log` either** — that is ingest telemetry ("this workbook row was parked / excluded"), with no old→new pair and no actor.
- **No FK on `changed_by`** (unlike `cenapro.analysis_sample`, which FKs `created_by`/`updated_by` → `profiles` with ON DELETE SET NULL): an audit row must outlive the account that wrote it.
- **`public.cenapro_production_event_audit`** — the read-only `security_invoker` accessor (a trail nobody can read from the app is half a trail). SELECT granted to `authenticated` + `service_role`; `anon` revoked on both it and the base table, per the grants trap below. Verified live: `anon` EXECUTE on the RPC → denied; `anon` SELECT on the trail → denied; `authenticated` INSERT into the trail → denied.

### Backfill outcome (2026-08-01)
**All 500 sheet samples imported** — the ~117 that place nowhere are real lab readings for pre-June DVO container vans whose weights the DB does not carry yet (DVO is deferred out of Cenapro v1); dropping them would mean re-transcribing the sheet the day DVO lands, and they cost nothing because the views join *from* event groups. **383 groups carry a sample** (382 exact + 1 remap), **July 2026 = 144 groups / 0 sampled** — the live gap the feature fills.

The one remap: `2025-12-02|FLEC|WHSE 5` → `…|WHSE 7`, because the only Dec-2 FLEC draw in the DB is under WHSE 7; the original sheet key is preserved in `notes`. **Deliberate divergence from the draft's matcher:** the draft's `date|src` fallback also lends an already-placed sample to a *second* group (2026-02-22 FLEC/WHSE 7's reading shown against FLEC/WHSE 5, 2,249 kg). The backfill refuses that — the sheet never sampled that draw, and guessing would inflate coverage and pull a weighted average toward a number nobody measured. February coverage therefore reads **98.99%** here vs 99.2% in the draft; its four weighted averages are unchanged, and no other month moves.

### Grants trap (applies to every future `cenapro` object)
The `cenapro` schema carries a DEFAULT ACL `{anon=r, authenticated=arwd, service_role=arwd}` from the original `create_cenapro_schema` migration. **Every new relation is born with `anon=SELECT` and `authenticated=INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE`, silently, whatever the CREATE says** — which contradicts the Phase-4 "anon has no data access" posture and makes a read-only reporting view look writable. The views migration REVOKEs both explicitly; do the same for anything new.

### Two traps the Cenapro production grids share (2026-08-05)

**1. A cell editor may never use React's `autoFocus`.** react-dom's `commitMount` is a bare
`domElement.focus()`, and `HTMLElement.focus()` scrolls the element into view with block/inline
**`"center"` through every scrolling ancestor** — and because `"center"` always computes a target,
it fires *even when the element is already fully visible*. So starting an edit jogged the page.
`production-ledger-grid.tsx` (4 `<Input>`) and `draft-entry-zone.tsx` (3) now use
`ref={focusNoScroll}` from `lib/utils.ts`; `production-daily-block.tsx`'s `TypeaheadInput` uses a
merged ref callback so its own `inputRef` stays populated. Every `gridRef.current?.focus()` in
`production-ledger-grid` (4), `production-endless-sheet` (3), `production-endless-pivots` (3) and
`production-daily-block` (1) now passes `{ preventScroll: true }`. The two `.select()` companions
and `production-daily-block`'s deliberate `scrollIntoView({block:'nearest'})` are **preserved** —
`nearest` is a no-op for a visible cell, so that scroll is the grid's own caret-follow and intended.

**2. Row borders declared on a `<tr>` are inert.** These grids set `border-collapse: separate`,
which is load-bearing here — under `collapse` a border belongs to the TABLE rather than the cell,
so a `position: sticky` frozen column's borders scroll away and the pinned block loses its edges.
But in the separated-borders model **the CSS spec paints borders on table CELLS ONLY**: a border
on `<tr>`, `<tbody>`, `<col>` or `<colgroup>` is ignored outright. So the `border-b` on
`production-endless-sheet`'s header + both body rows, and `production-ledger-grid`'s row + header,
was never painted — the sheets read as columns with no rows. The rule now rides on the cells via
`[&>*]:border-b [&>*]:border-b-border/30` (full-weight `border-b-border` for the header↔body
boundary). **Never re-add a `<tr>` border; never flip to `collapse`.** Row heights are unchanged —
preflight makes cells `border-box`, so the 1px rule draws inside the explicit height and
virtuoso's measurement in the endless sheet is undisturbed.

*Both were fixed first on `/cenapro/deliveries`; see that module's CONTEXT.md for the original
diagnosis.*

## QC and Flec Inventory — the `?grid=v2` side-by-side (2026-08-19, half retired 2026-08-20)

Both screens gained a second, **read-only** implementation on the universal table
module, built beside the existing client and reachable only on `?grid=v2`. The
existing components are byte-identical and remain the production path; the default is
unchanged. Recipe and rationale: `lib/table/CONTEXT.md` → "Stage 1D at scale".

**Flec Inventory left the track on 2026-08-20 and its v2 file is deleted** — see the
retirement note below. Everything in this section that speaks of "both" is historical
from that date on; only **QC** still has a v2.

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `qc/qc-ledger-grid-v2.tsx` | The QC ledger on `<BlackwoodTable>`. **SEVENTEEN columns since 2026-08-25, in the Cenapro PRODUCTION ledger's arrangement** — see "QC ledger v2 — the production arrangement" below. (It carried the live `qc-ledger-client.tsx`'s own fifteen, in that file's order, until then; four widths always differed — see the `px-2` note.) **EDITABLE since 2026-08-21** — see "QC ledger v2 — the editing pass". |
| `qc/qc-grid-v2-save.ts` | **NEW (2026-08-21).** The v2 grid's PURE edit + save model — no React, no Supabase, no action call (type-only imports from `./actions`). Owns `parseQcField` / `normalizeQcField` / `cleanPastedQcCell` (the ONE cell verdict, shared by every `ColumnSpec.parse` and by the save), `routeQcEdits` (WHICH row an edit is a save to), `overlayMetrics` (the reading merge), `buildQcSavePlan`, `forgettableRowIds`, `draftFromEdits` (this grid's column keys → the composer's `DraftDraw`), `countQcUnsaved` / `describeQcUnsaved`, the three labels and the three outcome sentences — **plus, since 2026-08-25, `QC_COLUMNS` (THE column arrangement, which the grid renders by mapping), `QC_IMPORTED_COLUMNS` and `isImportedColumn`.** Asserted by `scripts/verify-qc-grid.ts` (**50 assertions**) with no browser and no database. |
| ~~`inventory/flec-inventory-grid-v2.tsx`~~ | **RETIRED 2026-08-20 — deleted.** See below. |

### RETIRED 2026-08-20 — Flec Inventory (`/cenapro/inventory`) leaves the v2 track

Renzo's call on the live review: *"You can take out the v2 for flec inventory cenapro. I
don't think it's appropriate and I think it needs customized behavior that is more niche
than the table we're making."* `inventory/flec-inventory-grid-v2.tsx` is **deleted** and
`inventory/page.tsx` is back to its pre-v2 shape — no `GridVersionBar`, no `parseGrid`, no
`grid` search param, `FlecInventoryClient` rendered unconditionally with its props passed
inline. `?grid=v2` on that route is now **INERT** (an unread query param), never an error.

The bespoke client was never edited for the preview, so this is a straight subtraction —
the screen renders exactly what it rendered before the preview existed. Nothing shared was
removed: the preview exercised only public `BlackwoodTable` surface, and its historical
role in `lib/table/CONTEXT.md` ("Stage 1D at scale") stands as a record of what the
platform was proven against. Its niche behaviour — the editable STARTING/openings block,
the append-only opening-balance history dialog, the warehouse + start-date pickers — stays
on the bespoke component, which is the reason it left the track.

The ICTC twin, `/inventory/flecon-bags`, was retired in the same pass and for the same
reason (see `app/(app)/inventory/flecon-bags/CONTEXT.md`).

### QC ledger v2 — the 2026-08-20 consumer-side pass

Four changes to `qc/qc-ledger-grid-v2.tsx`, none of them in `lib/table/**` or
`components/shared/table/**`:

1. **Vertical scrolling works.** `BlackwoodTable`'s focus-scope scroller is
   `<div class="h-full overflow-auto">` inside its own root `<div class="flex min-h-0
   flex-col">` — and that root declares **no height**. As a plain block child of this
   file's `h-full overflow-hidden` wrapper it sized to its CONTENT, so the `flex-1` region
   under it inherited a content height and the `h-full` scroller resolved to exactly the
   height of the rows it held. **A scroller as tall as its content has nothing to scroll**;
   the sheet simply grew past the wrapper, which clips, and every day after the first was
   unreachable. Fixed by passing `className="h-full"` (the component's public prop) so the
   root gets a definite height and `min-h-0 flex-1` can finally shrink below content.
2. **Rows are ordered by SRC within each day** — TNK 1…n numerically, then W6, W7, DVO,
   FLEC; an unrecognised source ranks last and then sorts alphabetically, so a new source
   location appears at the bottom of its day rather than vanishing. The unit of ordering is
   the **GROUP, never the draw**: a lab reading covers a whole sample group and lives on
   its first draw, so re-ordering draws across a group boundary would separate a reading
   from the rows it describes. Sorted ONCE into `orderedDays`, which both the flatten and
   the id index read — two independent sorts are two definitions of the order.
3. **The per-day HEADING row is gone** (*"having a heading row on the table per day is kind
   of redundant given you already have a summary per day"*). The `group-header` row family
   was removed and the date moved into the day total's label:
   `Σ 2026-08-01 · 9 draws · 6 groups · cov 100%`. No blank spacer row was added — the Σ row
   is a solid `bg-muted` band now ruled top AND bottom, a harder break between two runs of
   28px data rows than an empty row would be.
4. ~~**The width clamp stays, with a `TODO(sizing-fill)` marker.**~~ **DONE, later the same
   day — the clamp is GONE and the grid passes `sizing="fill"`.** The clamp existed because
   unclamped `width: 100%` under `table-layout: fixed` scales every `<col>` proportionally
   while the sticky `left` offsets keep using the DECLARED widths, which pins a frozen
   column inside its neighbour; the price was dead space to the right on a wide viewport.
   `sizing="fill"` (`lib/table/CONTEXT.md` → the review pass, item 5) closes it at the
   source instead: the slack is distributed **inside `useTableColumns`' own resolution** and
   the table is rendered at exactly the width that resolution produced, so `table-fixed` has
   no slack left to scale into and every offset is a prefix sum of the widths ACTUALLY
   painted. `useTableColumns` is no longer imported here at all — the consumer computes no
   width of its own.

   **Why the frozen DATE column still pins correctly, by construction.** QC had exactly ONE
   pinned column when this was written (it has FOUR since 2026-08-25 — the reasoning below
   is unchanged and covers any number of them, because
   `distributeFill` skips every column with a `pin` (a pinned width is a wall
   the caret-follow and the drag auto-scroll measure from), so DATE keeps its declared width
   verbatim; and `pinnedOffsets` is computed from the column table **after** the fill, so the
   offsets are prefix sums of the rendered widths either way.) With one pinned column the
   offset is `0` in both modes; with the four of them it is `0 · 36 · 128 · 220`, the same
   prefix sums under both sizings. Verified by reasoning through `use-table-columns.ts` plus the
   two `verify-table-core.ts` assertions that pin *Σ resolved widths === the container* and
   *every pinned offset is a prefix sum of the resolved widths*, and the browser spec that
   renders a declared 48px column as 48px under `fill` — **not** by driving `/cenapro/qc`
   itself, which needs a real login.

~~**READ-ONLY is structural, not a promise.**~~ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-21 for QC — see
the editing pass below.** It read: *no `ColumnSpec` in either declares a `parse`, and
`columnAcceptsEdit` falls back to `spec.parse !== undefined`, so the editor,
Delete/Backspace and the paste loop's per-cell guard all refuse at every coordinate.*
That is still exactly how it worked, and the sentence it ended on — *the row families
still declare each slot's honest `editable` flag, the ROW's half of the verdict, ANDed
with the column's, and what a later editing pass builds on* — is precisely what the
editing pass did build on: the `draw` family's `editable: false` on the four metric
lanes needed no change at all.

**QC is the module's clearest two-family case.** The sheet is one row per DRAW (a
weight belongs to a single draw), but a LAB READING covers a whole sample group and
the existing ledger puts those four metric cells on the group's **first** draw only.
So `draw-first` occupies all fifteen columns and `draw` occupies eleven, returning
**`null`** for BD / ASH / GRIT / MC. `null` means "this row has no cell there", not
"an empty one" — and that one answer drives the keyboard (a vertical run steps over
it), the paste (it never lands there), the selection pill (it is not totalled) and the
tint. Getting it wrong is BUG-024.

The day rule-off goes through `renderChromeRow` (it sits inside the body, which
`summaryRows` cannot reach); the month rollup is a declared summary lane. Chrome keys are
a run **ordinal**, never a date — a chrome key is the virtualiser's React key and a
repeatable value would collide. *(Until 2026-08-20 there were TWO chrome rows per day, a
heading above and a total below; the heading was removed as redundant and its date moved
into the total's label — see the pass note above.)*

**Visibly missing on both, deliberately:** everything that writes or is a different
shape — the ADD/draw composer, the metric editors and their carry-over, Save/Discard,
the month picker, the openings (STARTING) block, the opening-balance history dialog,
the warehouse/date pickers, and the metric charts. Nothing is stubbed. The selection
shows a cell count rather than a SUM, because the module computes its aggregates
internally and does not hand them out (seam 2 in the module CONTEXT).
